# Improvements log archive, November 2026

Older entries moved unchanged from `../LOG.md` (card W3). Month is by position in the old file; grep `../LOG.md`'s index for a card ID.

## S28 — Week Recap: choose the week, and a Competing / Rebuilding split

**User-visible effect.**
- **The Scoreboard's week is a control.** Arrows around the title, "‹ Week 7 Recap ›" (real buttons labelled "Previous
  week" and "Next week"), step back a week at a time to week 1 and forward again to this week's recap; each end's
  arrow is disabled. An earlier week shows its own matchups, scores, tiles, biggest win / worst loss and MVPs & LVPs,
  reads **Final** (no Still in play, no win chances, "Week 6 is over: every result is final." at the foot), and Export
  Recap saves that week's image (`Week_6_Recap_…png`). A week with no matchups in any league says "No matchups in week N
  in your Sleeper leagues." Screen readers hear the week ("Week 6 Recap, final"), focus stays on the arrow (or moves
  to the other one at an end), and swiping between tabs works as before. Leaving the Scoreboard goes back to this week;
  the Dashboard's line and the Lineup strip stay on this week throughout.
- **Competing / Rebuilding in the record.** A third group beside Paid / Free and Redraft / Dynasty: your dynasty and
  keeper leagues, Competing with the Roster tab's "I'm competing this year" on, Rebuilding otherwise. Shown only when
  you have such a league; also in Export Recap's image (it draws the same groups) and the header's (i).
- **The switch shows in every dynasty and keeper league**, whatever the Power Rankings source and with or without an
  In-Season set. Where it changes nothing else, its line reads "Counts this league as Competing on the Scoreboard. With
  an In-Season set and your own rankings, Ovr and position rooms use them too." The Suggested line still needs an
  In-Season set, as before.
- On phones the header's summary ("starts soon", "Record 9–6 · 2 still live") now wraps under the title, so (i) and
  Refresh stay on the title's row.

**Owner's decisions (asked before building).**
1. The control: **arrows around the title**. The Dashboard's line and the Lineup strip **stay on this week**; the choice
   lasts until you leave the tab, no storage key.
2. Grouping: first **(c)** (on = Competing, off = Rebuilding, no switch = Not set), with **the switch shown in every
   dynasty and keeper league**. Since that leaves no league without a switch, I asked again: **off (including never
   touched) = Rebuilding**, two groups only; and the switch shows **whatever the source** (Market Consensus too).
3. MVPs & LVPs already followed `?week=`, so they follow the arrows (no question needed).

**What changed, and where.**
- `js/mls/week/scoreboard.js`: `summarize` counts `competing` / `rebuilding` from each row's `direction`;
  `leagueDirection(kind, competing)` (null for redraft, else by the switch); `stepWeek(week, step, last)` for the arrows.
- `js/mls/week/thisWeek.js`: `weekCtx({ scoreboard })`: the Scoreboard reads `view.chosen` (the arrows' week), the
  Dashboard line and strip this week (`renderThisWeek` builds a second model when they differ). `past` (before the
  recap week) forces Final, every matchup done and no win chances; no projections are fetched for a past week. The
  one-slot `other` cache became `otherWeeks` (a Map per week), and the load state (`view.loads`: loadedAt and failed
  leagues), the win chances (`chancesWeek`) and the week stats are per week too, so each week keeps its own 2 minutes and
  stepping back doesn't drop the recap week the Dashboard shows. A load that finishes after another week was chosen
  starts that week's. `stepScoreboardWeek`, `leaveScoreboardWeek` (nav.js calls it on any other tab), focus kept across
  the card's redraws, an `empty` row kind for Sleeper's `[]`. `splitGroups` adds "Dynasty and keeper, by direction". The
  (i) mentions Competing / Rebuilding in the Record line (kept to one clause: a closed tooltip still takes layout, see
  Screenshots).
- `js/mls/power/rosterCard.js`: `renderCompetingSwitch` shows for `getPowerLeagueKind(league) === 'dynasty'` and words
  `#powerCompetingDesc`; hidden for no league or a league that isn't fully synced (it used to keep the last league's state
  there). `lineup/index.html`: `#powerCompetingDesc`, `#scoreboardWeekAnnouncer` (an `sr-only` live region beside the
  card, which is redrawn whole), the Guide's Scoreboard and step 4 text. `css/mls.css`: `.mls-week-nav`, `.mls-week-step`,
  the heading's flex-basis. `js/mls/main.js`: the `stepScoreboardWeek` action.
- `?week=` still works (starts the Scoreboard on that week, with its notice); the arrows reach up to the later of this
  week and the address's week. docs/TESTING.md's note on it updated.
- `sw.js`: `CACHE_NAME` v2.8.137 → v2.8.138 (no file added). CHANGELOG: two lines under Lineup Strategist.

**Tests.** Unit (`weekScoreboard.test.mjs`): the grouping (on, off, never touched, redraft, keeper in Redraft and
Rebuilding, too early in no count) and `stepWeek`'s ends. `mls-this-week.spec.mjs`: back to week 1 (its own matchups,
118.0 instead of 98.0; Final though its scoreboard has a game 'pre'; no swing or chance; the announcer; focus on Next;
the Dashboard line on week 2; the app on week 2), forward to week 2 (Provisional, the swing back), back again with no
second fetch, a swipe on the header switches tabs, and returning shows week 2; a week with no matchups (the message, no
tiles, no export, no "team wasn't found"); Second Fixture League with the switch off (Rebuilding), on (Competing), off
again, and the redraft league without a switch. Existing split assertions gain "Rebuilding 1–0" and a third group.
`mls-season-rankings.spec.mjs`: the switch now shows in a dynasty league without an In-Season set, with the right line.

**Screenshots.** Main and this branch rendered here and compared with `npm run pxdiff` (after stopping a leftover
server from an interrupted run, which had been serving this branch to both): 38 identical, 4 differ, all by design:
- `desktop/mls-league-scoreboard.png`, `phone/mls-league-scoreboard.png`: the arrows around "Week 2 Matchups" (Next
  disabled); on phones "starts soon" under the title. The PNG grows (desktop 900 → 936px, phone 844 → 918px): the
  32px arrow buttons, and the (i)'s one extra clause, since a closed tooltip still takes up layout on this short
  pre-kickoff page (a first draft with two new tooltip lines made it 1038px).
- `desktop/mls-league-guide.png`, `phone/mls-league-guide.png`: the Guide's Scoreboard and step 4 text.
Not re-taken here (this container renders with Chromium 1243, not CI's 1194): take the four from the pull request's CI
run as docs/TESTING.md describes.

**Checks.** check-precache OK; 471 unit tests pass; Playwright 625 passed, 41 skipped, only `visual.spec.mjs`
failing on the environment message (as on main here); after the last CSS and tooltip edits, this-week, tooltips-onscreen,
mvp-lvp and smoke again: 82 passed.

**Left over.**

### S28, round 2: the owner's questions, and a review as a user

**The LVP note.** The owner asked why I'd noted that a past week's LVPs need its ESPN scoreboard, since they worked in
their testing. They do: the app fetches each week's scoreboard. The note was about the failure case only: an LVP needs
his game "over" (`state === 'post'`, playerRecap.js), so if that one request fails, every state is unknown and the LVP
list is empty beside a Final pill. Fixed (owner: yes): `recapInput` gives a past week's teams `post` where the
scoreboard has no state. Spec `mls-mvp-lvp.spec.mjs`, "an earlier week's LVPs show even without its ESPN scoreboard"
(ESPN aborted for week 1); it fails with the fix taken out (no LVPs) and passes with it.

**Review as a user** (rendered at both widths), and what the owner picked to fix (all of it):
1. "Rebuilding 1–0" alone read like a fourth kind of league → **both halves always show** once any dynasty or keeper
   league is in the record, 0–0 included ("Competing 0–0 | Rebuilding 1–0"); in Export Recap's image too.
2. Getting back to this week took a tap per week → **"Back to week N"** (`.mls-week-back`, `scoreboardThisWeek`), a small
   green link after the arrows on a past week only; focus goes to Previous and the announcer reads the week.
3. The card shrank to "Loading matchups…" and grew back on each step → **the last week drawn stays up, dimmed and
   inert** (`.mls-week-body.is-stale`, `view.lastBody`) under the new title until the new week arrives. A first load
   draws as before. An empty draw made before the load starts never replaces the last real body (the first try did,
   and the spec caught it).
4. The arrows' 32px tap targets → a **48px tap area** (`::before`, inset −0.5rem) without making the header taller.
5. "Week 1 is over: every result is final." repeated the pill → **no foot line for a past week** (and not the "Sleeper
   can still correct stats" line either).
Then the two things that caught my eye (owner: follow up after the fixes; done):
- **MVPs & LVPs no longer repeats the Provisional / Final pill** the header shows a line above.
- **"$ Paid" stays together** in the paid hint on phones (`mls-nowrap`).

**Merge.** F10 (btrerise/My-Draft-Strategist#212) merged to main during this round; merged in (LOG and CHANGELOG
conflicts: both kept, F10's LOG entry first). Main's `CACHE_NAME` is v2.8.138 too, so this branch is now **v2.8.139**.

**Tests.** `mls-this-week.spec.mjs`: "Back to week 2" there on week 1, gone on week 2, one tap back (and the announcer);
"stepping to a week still loading keeps the last week's tiles, dimmed" (week 1's matchups held until released: inert,
two old tiles, then Final and no stale body); no foot line on a past week; the splits with Competing 0–0. `mls-mvp-lvp`:
no pill on the MVPs & LVPs line (Provisional in the header); the image has Competing 0–0 and Rebuilding 1–0; the LVP test
above.

**Screenshots.** Still the same four as round 1 (the Scoreboard and the Guide, both widths); round 2 changes nothing
else in them except the Guide's "Back to week N" sentence (the screenshot Scoreboard is before kickoff: no back link, no
split, no MVPs). Take all four from CI.

## M1 — A Material 3 review of the whole site (research)

**What changed.** No app code, CSS or screenshots. New **docs/improvements/material3-review.md** (not served code, so
`PRECACHE_ASSETS` and `CACHE_NAME` stay) with 13 screenshots in **docs/improvements/material3/**: what already meets
Material 3, then 14 ranked, sized proposals, each with what Material 3 says (linked), what the site does now (a
screenshot), the files, the screenshots it would re-take, app by app or site-wide, and overlaps with open cards.

**How.** All 82 m3.material.io pages needed (Guidelines, Specs and Accessibility of 22 components, plus type, color,
elevation, shape, motion, states, layout and contrast) read in headless Chromium the way S26 did; the session's network
allowed it. A throwaway Playwright script (not committed) walked every tab of both apps and T-Score at 375, 768 and
1280px with the fixture data, measuring target sizes, keyboard focus rings, font sizes, transitions, corner radii and text
contrast, and opened the toasts, the confirm and prompt dialogs and the upload preview.

**Main findings.** Navigation (S21, S26), dark surfaces, dialog structure (F10, merged in #212) and Reduce Motion already
meet. Off: controls under 48px on every tab (the Lineup row's swap 30x23 and lock 26x21, Taken / Pick 30px tall, 18px "i"
icons); focus rings are the browser's default on most buttons and faint on text fields; hover lifts the button instead of a
state layer; toasts go after 3.5s, bounce in and sit over open dialogs; selection in chips and segmented buttons is shown
by color only; 35 font sizes in the CSS, nine under 11px on screen; ten different transition curves; a few text colors
under 4.5:1 (T-Score's labels, the injury badge, DEF text).

**Overlaps.** S15 splits into proposal 2 (focus) and 6 (contrast); proposal 1's lineup-row part folds into S32, which
should also offer a modal bottom sheet for the row's extras on phones; proposal 8 (motion tokens) pairs with S18; F10's
pinned buttons are done.

**The owner's picks** (after reading the ranked list): "Do 1+2+3 together, 4+8, 5+12 as suggested. For the rest,
combine where it best makes sense, either because they're related, or because approximate size means they can go in
one session easily enough." All 14 proposals become cards; the next runbook update adds them:

- **M2 = 1 + 2 + 3: touch, focus and feedback** (~25k tok, site-wide, 0 to 4 screenshots). 48px targets, one green
  focus ring (2px on text fields), state layers for hover and press. Replaces S15's focus half. The Lineup row's swap and
  lock spacing goes to S32.
- **M3 = 4 + 8: messages and motion** (~16k tok, site-wide, 0 screenshots). Toasts as snackbars, plus Material 3's
  motion tokens for every transition. Run before S18 so S18 uses the tokens, or in the same stretch, but not alongside it:
  both edit css/base.css's transitions.
- **M4 = 5 + 12: selection controls** (~16k tok, 16 to 18 screenshots). Checks on selected chips and segments (40px
  segments), and switches at Material 3's size with a check. Both apps' Guide legends where a chip's look changes.
- **M5 = 7 + 13: dialogs and form errors** (~20k tok, 0 screenshots). Grouped because both are about telling you what's
  happening where you are: dialog headlines that name the action, a full-screen upload preview on phones, and errors
  under the field in both apps' Sleeper forms.
- **M6 = 6 + 10: contrast, and T-Score's tabs** (~18k tok, ~20 screenshots). Grouped because the contrast failures are
  mostly T-Score's labels and both re-take T-Score's 12 PNGs, so they're re-taken once. Takes S15's contrast half, so
  with M2, **S15 is fully covered and can come off the runbook**.
- **M7 = 9 for T-Score and Draft Strategist: the type scale** (~25k tok, 28 screenshots). The tokens go into
  css/base.css here; T-Score's stylesheet is small, so the two pages fit one session. Checks every tab at 2x text.
- **M8 = 9 for Lineup Strategist** (~20k tok, 14 screenshots). Standalone: the biggest stylesheet (299 of the site's 434 font-size rules) and
  it waits for S32, which redoes the phone rows' text. Uses M7's tokens, so after M7.
- **M9 = 11 + 14: button emphasis and corners** (~20k tok, most screenshots). Grouped because both re-style buttons
  across every screen and both change the identity a little, so the card shows mock-ups and **asks the owner first**
  (which colored buttons lose their fill; 6 to 8px corners). Last, after the type cards, so screenshots are re-taken once
  more at most.

**Runbook updated** (2026-11-01): cards M2 to M9 added (M2, M3 and M5 in the January step; M4, M6 and M7 in a new
"Before spring" step, one at a time; M8 and M9 with the spring and summer work), each needing M1's branch merged since
its prompt points to the review; S15 shown under Completed as "covered by M2 and M6"; S32's scope names the bottom sheet
and the row's 48px targets; F10 marked merged (#212); the lanes and parallel notes no longer mention S15. The follow-up
line in docs/TESTING.md is removed.

**Left over.** Pages at 200% text are checked in M7 and M8.

### M1, round 2: the rest of Material 3

The owner asked for a second pass "from a different lens": beyond the tangible fixes in round 1, where else could the
rest of Material 3 improve the site's look and feel without changing its identity. Research only.

**What changed.** New **docs/improvements/material3-review-2.md** (linked from the first review) with five more
screenshots in docs/improvements/material3/ (the Dashboard, Lineup, Tracker, Scout and T-Score Top-50 at 1280px). Read
in headless Chromium: the Expressive announcement, the components round 1 skipped (button groups, split button,
toolbars, loading indicator, search, side sheets, FABs, app bars, carousel, navigation drawer) and the Expressive
changes to cards, lists, badges and progress; color roles (surface containers), elevation, shape, motion specs (springs
and their web curves), icons, editorial type; canonical layouts, scaffold, the writing style guide.

**Findings.**
- **Three corrections to written cards**: M3 should use Expressive's web spring curves (spatial ones overshoot a little
  on purpose; round 1 quoted the baseline easing page, which Material 3 now says is no longer maintained), so the
  toast's "no bounce" becomes a smaller, quicker bounce if the owner agrees; M4 should use Expressive's connected button
  group (which replaces segmented buttons: the selected one changes shape and color) and make the site's three looks for
  that control one; M2 can offer Expressive's pressed shape morph (corners tighten) beside the state layer.
- **Nine new proposals (15 to 23)**: the badge legend in a side or bottom sheet instead of a trip to the Guide (and a
  shared sheet S32 can reuse); badges on the navigation (lineups that need you, on the clock); progress and loading
  indicators; two panes on wide screens (the Tracker with your team beside it, and others); surface tones instead of
  boxes in boxes; one or two hero moments; sentence case (fold into M7 and M8); a button group and split button for the
  Lineup card (fold into M9); search bars.
- Looked at and not proposed: FABs, toolbars, carousel, pickers, the abstract shape library, Material Symbols, flexible
  app bars, dynamic color.

**The owner's picks:** "Yes to changing M2, M3, and M4. I want 15-23; group multiple together where it makes sense, and
fold into existing cards if it makes sense at all. Please also feel free to re-order the priority based on estimated
positive impact the change would have. Also, I'm OK making changes mid-season; this season is kind of a Beta
development phase more-so than a fully released product." So:

- **Corrections, all accepted:** M2 offers Expressive's pressed shape morph (corners tighten about 2px) beside the state
  layer; M3 uses Expressive's web curves (spatial ones overshoot a little; the toast's entrance is the owner's call: a
  small bounce or none); M4 becomes Expressive's connected button group, one look for every segmented control on the
  three pages (Lineup Strategist's segments, T-Score's WR / RB pills), with the check optional.
- **Folded into existing cards:** 17 (progress and loading indicators) into **M3** (both are about how the app shows
  it's working, and the loading shape uses M3's motion tokens; M3 grows to ~24k); 23 (search bars) into **M5** (text
  fields; ~26k); 21 (sentence case) into **M7** and **M8**, which re-take the same screenshots anyway (~30k and ~26k);
  22 (button group and Share split button) into **M9**, whose corners (14) moved to M14.
- **New cards:** **M10** navigation badges and hero moments (16 + 20: both rest on "a lineup needs you" and "you're on
  the clock"; ~20k); **M11** the badge legend in a sheet (15; builds the shared sheet, so **S32 now needs M11**; ~12k);
  **M12** two panes on wide screens for Lineup Strategist and **M13** for Draft Strategist (18, one per app; ~20k and
  ~16k); **M14** surface tones and corners (19 + 14: both change every container and the identity a little, so one
  card with mock-ups first; ~25k; M9 now follows it).
- **Order, by expected impact** (owner: changes mid-season are fine, this season is a beta): weeks 10–11 add **M2**
  (felt on every tap) and **M11**; weeks 12–13 add **M10** beside S30, with S32 after M11; the playoffs start with
  **M3**, then S18 on its motion tokens; January **M5** and **M12** beside S25; February–March **M4**, **M6**, **M7**
  one at a time; spring and summer **M13** before draft season, then **M8**, **M14** and **M9**. Runbook updated the
  same day (version 33): those cards, S28 marked merged (#213), S32's need on M11.

## N1 — Research: what notifications would take on this site

**What changed.** `docs/improvements/notifications.md` (new): what each of the three alerts takes with the site open
and with it closed (web push on Cloudflare), a table of alert × open/closed with card counts and risks, the
permission UX, privacy and upkeep, and cited sources. No app file changed: no served code, no `sw.js`, no CSS, so
`CACHE_NAME` is untouched and nothing goes in `CHANGELOG.md`.

**Main findings.**
- Android Chrome only supports `registration.showNotification()` (the constructor throws), so the shared module always
  goes through the service worker; a tap needs a `notificationclick` listener in `sw.js`, which has none today.
- Chrome slows a hidden tab's chained timers to once a minute after 10 s to 5 min hidden and silent, so Draft
  Strategist's 3 s live sync can notice a pick up to ~60 s late. Firefox (budget) and Safari (1 s alignment) are
  milder; Safari needs a real test. Phones pause background pages, so open-site alerts are a computer feature.
- iPhone/iPad: no notifications in a Safari tab; web push only after Add to Home Screen (iOS 16.4+; the manifest is
  already `standalone`).
- Closed-site alerts need a Worker with a Cron Trigger (Pages Functions have no cron), D1 or KV, VAPID keys and a
  Web-Crypto push library. Cron runs once a minute at most; the free plan gives 10 ms CPU per run and 5 cron triggers.
- A server can compute "on the clock" from Sleeper's small draft endpoints. Injury status lives only in Sleeper's
  15 MB player file (once a day, too big for 10 ms CPU), so a closed-site starter alert would miss late scratches.
  Rankings and the recommended lineup are browser-only, so no rankings alert can run closed.
- "Rankings updated" has nothing to alert on: rankings change only when you upload or tap the ROS fetch button, and
  the app already marks Weekly rankings older than 6 days in amber.

**Owner's decision (2026-11-01).** Asked: only while open (recommended), also while closed, or a mix by trigger.
**Answer: C, a mix by trigger.** Starter alerts while the site is open only; "on the clock" while open first, then
also by web push when the site is closed. **N3: dropped**; N2's starter alert gets one line naming leagues whose
Weekly rankings are stale (`getFreshness(…, 6)`, the league cards' rule).

**The N cards after N1** (the runbook's prompts are older: where they differ, this entry wins):
- **N2 — Shared notification module and the Lineup Strategist starter alert (site open).** As the runbook's card,
  plus: always `registration.showNotification()`; add `notificationclick` to `sw.js` (focus or open the right page; a
  middle-number `CACHE_NAME` bump); toast and title-flash fallbacks; the stale-Weekly-rankings line. Its asks stay
  (Sleeper lineup or recommended, minutes before kickoff, Questionable), plus one: refresh injury statuses once before
  each kickoff window (the 15 MB file, at most ~3 times on a Sunday) or use the last sync. Help text: works while
  `/lineup/` is open on a computer.
- **N3 — dropped.** Tick it done in the runbook with this note.
- **N4 — Draft Strategist on the clock (site open).** As the runbook's card, plus: test whether a timer in a dedicated
  Web Worker escapes Chrome's once-a-minute throttle (keep the 3 s timing either way); say how much clock is left from
  the draft's `settings.pick_timer` and the last pick's time; help text says a hidden tab may notice a pick up to a
  minute late. Needs N2.
- **N5 (new) — Web push setup.** Needs N4. A Worker in its own folder (e.g. `workers/notify/`, `wrangler.toml`, the
  repo's first deployed-with-wrangler code), D1 for subscriptions (push endpoint and keys, Sleeper user ID, draft
  IDs, toggles, sent markers), subscribe/unsubscribe Pages Functions beside `functions/api/ffc/`, a Web-Crypto push
  library, the VAPID private key as a Worker secret. **Asks you:** to create the Cloudflare resources and keys (the
  session writes the steps, doesn't create them), free plan or $5/month. First re-check Cloudflare's limits on the
  live pages (this session could only see them through search results).
- **N6 (new) — Push in `sw.js` and Draft Strategist's Settings.** Needs N5. A `push` listener; an "Also alert me when
  Draft Strategist is closed" toggle that subscribes (iPhone: explains Add to Home Screen); a privacy note in the Guide
  (what's stored and how to delete it); Factory Reset and turning the toggle off unsubscribe; storage keys via
  `keys.js`.
- **N7 (new) — The draft poller.** Needs N6. The Worker's cron (once a minute) checks only subscribed drafts that are
  live, alerts N picks ahead and on the clock once each, removes expired subscriptions (404/410), and stops when the
  draft completes. Tests with a stubbed Sleeper, push service and clock.
- Not planned: a closed-site starter alert (injury data too stale); revisit only if a better injury source turns up.

escapes Chrome's throttle (N4 checks it). The runbook page was updated the same day (version 35): N2 and N4
rewritten as above, N3 marked covered by N2, N5–N7 added, and the order and Notifications track adjusted.

## S27 — Who to root for and against this week, across your leagues

**User-visible effect.**
- **The Scoreboard has two halves now**, each folding with a tap on its title (remembered): **League Scoreboard**
  (everything that was there: splits, Biggest win / Worst loss, Still in play, the decided line, the tiles, the notes)
  and **Player Scoreboard** (Who to root for, then MVPs & LVPs). The header stays above both: the week's arrows, the
  record, Provisional / Final and Refresh. Export Recap stays at the bottom. Folded, each says what's inside: "3 leagues ·
  1 still in play" and "Root for 7 · Against 7 · Both 2".
- **Who to root for**: every starter in this week's Sleeper matchups, from the matchups the Scoreboard already loads.
  - On top, the players in 2 or more of your matchups, in three groups: **Root for** (green, thumbs up), **Against you**
    (red, thumbs down) and **Both sides** (amber, crossing arrows). They sit side by side from 900px and stack on phones.
    Each row has headshot, position badge (D/ST for defenses), name, an "in 3" chip, team and opponent, kickoff time /
    Live / Final, and points. Both sides adds a green/red bar and "67% yours (2 of 3)". Five per group, then "Show all N".
  - **Tap a player (or Enter)** to see the leagues on each side ("Yours in", "Against you in"): each league's name ($ if
    paid), his points there, the win chance or "effectively decided" / "final" / "no opponent", and his share of what
    his side has left.
  - Below that, **every starter by kickoff window** ("Sun 1:00 PM · KC @ DEN · under way · 4 yours · 3 against"), with
    your starters beside the ones against you (stacked on phones). Five per side, then Show all. Each window folds.
  - Finished games fold into one line ("6 games final · 8 of your starters, 9 against you"). In a past week every game
    is final, so that line opens and the list shows final points.
  - Guillotine and median-only leagues have no opponent, and a line names them: their starters count for you only.
  - Players who are Out, on IR or on a bye are left off: S11's "lineups need you" box already flags them.
- A muted **credit line under the Scoreboard card**, styled like the Dashboard's version line: "The root-for board was
  inspired by Sleepy, sleepy-football.vercel.app" (linked). The same sentence ends the Guide's new Who to root for line.
- The header's (i) gains one line on the weights. The Guide's Scoreboard item gains the two halves and Who to root for.
  The badge legend gains two lines: the "in N" chips with the three icons, and the share bar.

**Owner's decisions (asked before building; two rounds of sketches at 375 and 1280px, the second at 20-league scale).**
1. **Where:** between the matchups and MVPs & LVPs, with the tab split into **League Scoreboard** and **Player
   Scoreboard**, both foldable ("allowing users control over what they do and don't see").
2. **Layout:** the spotlight over the list by kickoff window. **Each window folds**, every list is **capped with Show
   all** (as MVPs & LVPs are), and **no By Game view**. A key for the folds: agreed. I proposed one key for the halves and
   Who to root for (`mls_scoreboard_folds`) and nothing remembered for the windows, because their times change weekly.
3. **Weighting: combined.** paid × stakes × impact:
   - paid: **1.5** for a "$ Paid" league.
   - stakes: from the Simulator's win chance, 4·p·(1 − p) (0 once decided or done). It's now computed for every open
     matchup, not just Still in play: before kickoff, and while too early to call. Best Ball, guillotine and week 1 count 1.
   - impact: his projected points still to come ÷ his side's.
   - "Percentage rostered" was (a), how many of your leagues he's in. It's the sum itself (each league adds its weight),
     so it isn't a separate factor.
4. **No "close matchups only" switch for now**: a decided matchup already weighs 0. Revisit after seeing it with many
   leagues.
5. **Points:** this week's live points, not an average. The owner corrected my reading of Sleepy: it averages a player's
   score across the leagues you roster him in, not across the season. The number shown is Sleeper's own stat on the
   MVPs & LVPs scale (half PPR by default); each league's own points are in the tap detail.
6. **Credit:** the Guide line plus a line **at the bottom of the page under the card**, like the Dashboard's version
   (owner's idea). Nothing else in the feature names Sleepy, and nobody was contacted.

**Rules as built** (`js/mls/week/rootFor.js`, no app state):
- Root for: yours in 2+ and against in none. Against you: theirs in 2+ and yours in none. Both sides: at least one of
  each. The share score is your leagues' weight ÷ all his leagues' weight, as a percent. When every weight is 0 (games
  over, a past week) it uses the paid weights alone. Players still to play sort first, by weight, then by how many
  leagues.
- Starters only (`starterIdsOf`): Sleeper's starters, or Best Ball's lineup from the points so far (`bestBallLineup`).
  The Scoreboard's own Best Ball scoring now goes through the same function, unchanged in behavior.
- Impact without projections: a player still to play takes the average of the known ones (or an even share), so a
  missing projection doesn't zero him.

**What changed, and where.**
- `js/mls/week/rootFor.js` (new): `buildRootFor`, `stakesOf`, `impactsOf`, `sideOrder`, `starterIdsOf`,
  `PAID_WEIGHT`. `sw.js`: in `PRECACHE_ASSETS`, `CACHE_NAME` v2.8.139 → v2.8.140.
- `js/mls/week/thisWeek.js`:
  - The body is two `foldSection`s ('league', 'player') with Who to root for ('root') inside Player.
  - `rootModel` / `rootHTML` / `rootRow` / `rootWindow` draw the board from the Scoreboard's rows; nothing is fetched.
  - `addBoardChances` gives the board win chances for the open matchups Still in play doesn't cover. They're kept in
    their own map, so the tiles and Still in play show exactly what they did. Both runs queue on the one worker.
  - `toggleRootPlayer` and `toggleRootWindow` work in place, so focus stays put. `showAllRoot` redraws and focuses the
    first row it adds, and a redraw keeps focus on a row or window (`restoreFocus`).
  - The fold transition now names what follows in the enclosing sections too.
  - A 'nomatch' league keeps your side, for median-only leagues.
- `js/mls/badges.js`: `ROOT_ICONS`, `rootCountBadgeMarkup`, `rootShareMarkup`. `js/mls/legend.js`: two lines.
  `tests/unit/badgeLegend.test.mjs`: `mls-root-pos` (sizing) in ALLOWED.
- `js/mls/main.js`: the three actions. `js/shared/storage/keys.js`: `mls_scoreboard_folds` ({ league?, player?, root? },
  only while not open; removed when back to the defaults). `css/mls.css`: the sections, the board, the legend icons.
- `lineup/index.html`: the credit line (`.app-version.mls-scoreboard-credit`) and the Guide text.
- `CHANGELOG.md`: one line under Lineup Strategist, crediting Sleepy.

**Tests.**
- `tests/unit/rootFor.test.mjs` (8): both sides and the groups, a paid league's weight, stakes (50/50, 90/10, decided,
  done, no chance), impact (share of what's left, final, missing projections), the fallback for finished games,
  guillotine / median-only leagues, the window order, starters (bench, empty slots, Best Ball, no slot order).
- `tests/mls-root-for.spec.mjs` (both widths), with a third fixture league ("Third Fixture League", id …031:
  `make-fixtures.mjs` writes `league3.json`, `league3-rosters.json` and `league3-matchups.json`, with Allen and Lamar
  swapped; routes in `helpers.mjs`; the other fixture files are unchanged). It covers:
  - the halves and their order; Root for 7, Against you 7 and Both sides 2 with "in 3", 67% / 33% yours, and 57% / 43%
    once the third league is paid;
  - the tap detail and Enter, Show all and its focus, the MIN window and its fold, the finished games' line (D/ST and K
    in it);
  - the halves folding and remembered across a reload (the key's value, removed at the defaults);
  - a past week (Every game is final, open);
  - the empty states: no Sleeper league, a league still loading then joining, no matchups in any league;
  - nothing past the card's edges at 375 and 1280px, and the credit link.
- `mls-this-week.spec.mjs` and `mls-mvp-lvp.spec.mjs`: two checks that read the card body's direct children now read
  League Scoreboard's.

**Fetches.** None of its own. The extra win chances (Best Ball excluded) run only while the Scoreboard tab is open, and
use the Simulator's usual score-history requests (the same weekly files the Lineup tab's Simulator and Still in play
read, cached in IndexedDB). A first full run caught them starting from the Dashboard and the Lineup tab too, in
`mls-byes`, `mls-flex-choice` and `mls-waiver-flex-unranked` (unstubbed history weeks); fixed by `onScoreboard()`.

### S27, round 5: only open matchups count (owner: "Yes, count only open matchups")

The problem round 4 found, and my proposal: once any matchup is effectively decided or over, it changes nothing about
who you root for, so it's left out of the counts, the groups and the window lists.
- `buildRootFor`: `yours` / `against` hold only open matchups (status not `decided` or `done`; guillotine and
  median-only leagues are always open) while any matchup is open. `allYours` / `allAgainst` keep every league for the
  row's detail. A player only in decided matchups is off the board (`decidedOnly` counts them). When nothing is open
  (a past week, every game over) every league counts, as before. Weights didn't change: a decided matchup already
  weighed 0.
- The detail lists every league. A decided one is dimmed (`.mls-root-part-off`) with "effectively decided", and a
  finished one says "matchup over".
- Who to root for's summary says "in matchups still open" when a player was left out. The (i), the Guide and
  CHANGELOG say only open matchups count.
- On the owner's Monday: the MNF window "3 yours · 4 against" (was 6 / 8); the finished line 39 / 34 (was 102 / 101);
  the groups Root for 1, Against 0, Both 1. D'Andre Swift reads "1 of 2 yours" in Root for. Both his open leagues are
  in play, but his stake is 75% or more (the paid weight, how close each is, his share of each side), so the lean
  rule moves him. That's round 4's rule at work, now with honest counts; put to the owner.

## A1 — Real visitor numbers: privacy-friendly analytics

**User-visible effect.** Nothing changes in how the apps work. Each page now counts visits with **Umami Cloud**
(free plan, no cookies), on mydraftstrategist.com only. One new line in each app's Guide, under "Backup, Restore &
Reset" ("Visitor Counts: …"), and a small muted line at the end of T-Score's **The Research** tab, says what's counted
(the page, the site that linked to it, browser, device type, screen size, language and rough location), that nothing
that identifies you is kept, and that leagues, drafts, rankings and the Sleeper username are never sent. Opening any
page with `?nocount=1` stops counting that browser, with a toast saying so; `?nocount=0` counts it again.

**The owner's decisions (2026-11-03).** After the comparison (now in `docs/improvements/analytics.md`, with sources):
**Umami Cloud, free Hobby plan** ("I'd prefer to not pay anything"); Plausible was out on price, Cloudflare Web
Analytics and GoatCounter count visits rather than people, and GA4 needs a cookie banner. 1–4 "as suggested":
(1) the loader committed in the repo, not Cloudflare's one-click injection; (2) count mydraftstrategist.com only, never
`*.pages.dev` or localhost; (3) `?nocount=1` / `?nocount=0` opt-out; (4) no custom events now, left for K1. Website ID
`9cf14075-37fe-4528-bd9c-a596c26c0671` (public by design). The first look at prices came from search snippets because
the session's network blocked the vendors' sites; the owner allowed them and every number was then re-read on the
vendor's own page (GoatCounter's from its page source on GitHub until its site was allowed).

**What changed, where.**
- `js/shared/analytics.js` (new, in `PRECACHE_ASSETS`): the loader. The last `<script type="module">` on all three
  pages, after the app's module. `shouldCount(hostname, optedOut)` decides (exported, unit-tested); then it adds
  `<script defer src="https://cloud.umami.is/script.js">` with `data-website-id`, `data-domains`
  (`mydraftstrategist.com,www.mydraftstrategist.com`), `data-exclude-hash` (tab switches are `pushState` to `#tab`; without
  it every tab switch would count as a page view against the plan's 100K events a month) and `data-exclude-search`
  (keeps `?week=6` and the like out of the recorded address). Settings are in the loader, no inline snippet.
- `js/shared/storage/keys.js`: `KEYS.shared.analyticsOptOut` = `shared_analytics_optout` (`'1'` = opted out). In the
  `shared` group, like the hand-off keys: site-wide, so in **neither app's Backup** (Restore neither adds nor removes
  it) and **neither app's reset** (Lineup's Factory Reset, Draft's Reset Everything) clears it. Clearing the browser's
  site data does.
- `index.html`, `lineup/index.html`, `t-score/index.html`: the script tag; the Guide lines and T-Score's line
  (`.tscore-privacy` in `css/tscore.css`). `README.md`: a "Privacy and visitor counts" section. `docs/TESTING.md`:
  the script-order line mentions the loader. `CHANGELOG.md`: a line under both apps' Unreleased (T-Score has no
  section). `CACHE_NAME` v2.8.147 → v2.8.148.

**Checked.**
- `sw.js`'s fetch handler returns early for cross-origin requests and for anything that isn't a GET, so neither
  `cloud.umami.is/script.js` nor the beacon (a POST to `gateway.umami.is/api/send`) goes near the cache. Offline or
  blocked, the script just fails to load; the app logs nothing (the browser's own "Failed to load resource" note,
  as for Ko-fi's image). The site sends no Content-Security-Policy, so nothing else needed allowing.
- With the real Umami script (downloaded, served to the test page, its beacon captured and not sent): one POST per
  page load, with the website ID, screen, language, title, hostname, URL without query or hash, and referrer. Two tab
  switches on `/lineup/?week=6` sent nothing more.
- `tests/analytics.spec.mjs` (16 tests, both widths) serves the pages under `https://mydraftstrategist.com` and a
  `*.pages.dev` host from the local server: the script is added exactly once, deferred, with the right attributes, on
  each page for the live host, never on localhost or the preview host; `?nocount=1` stops it on every page (toast, key
  set, parameter removed, other parameters and the hash kept) and `?nocount=0` brings it back; the script's request is
  aborted as for every third party and nothing logs a console.error. `tests/unit/analytics.test.mjs`: the host rule
  (including look-alikes such as `mydraftstrategist.com.evil.example`) and the key's ownership.
- First paint (`npm run perf -- --server cf --runs 3`, this branch then `--ref origin/main`, medians; on localhost the
  loader adds nothing but its own ~1 KB module, which is the part that runs on every visit): cold visits FCP / LCP
  `/` 1.99 / 2.58 s vs main 1.97 / 2.54 s, `/lineup/` 1.97 / 1.97 s vs 1.91 / 1.91 s, `/t-score/` 1.69 / 1.69 s vs
  1.70 / 1.70 s; repeat visits within 0.08 s either way. No change beyond run-to-run noise. Umami's own 3.9 KB script
  loads from another host after the page, so it can't block the first paint either.
- Screenshots: rendered main and this branch in the same container (`RENDER_ENV=any`, Chromium 153; CI's 1194 isn't
  here) and compared with `npm run pxdiff`: exactly six PNGs change, all taller for the new line and nothing else.
  Re-take from CI: `desktop/` and `phone/` `mds-empty-guide.png`, `mls-league-guide.png` (the Guide's new "Visitor
  Counts" bullet in Backup, Restore & Reset) and `tscore-researchTab.png` (the muted line at the end of The Research).
  Not re-taken here, since this container can't render CI's pixels: open the PR, let CI fail on those six, and commit
  its `-actual.png` files after checking each `-expected.png` matches the committed baseline (docs/TESTING.md, "When
  your machine can't reproduce the baselines").

- `npm run check` in the session: check-precache OK (154 files), 485 unit tests pass, 687 Playwright tests pass; the 4
  failures are `visual.spec.mjs` refusing this container's Chromium 153 (the environment check), as on main.

**Bot Fight Mode (on since 2026-11-02): what it could affect, and a check for the owner.** Cloudflare's page
(read 2026-11-03): it challenges "traffic matching patterns of known bots", "may challenge API or mobile app traffic",
**cannot be bypassed with WAF custom rules or Page Rules** (no exceptions on the Free plan; exceptions need Super Bot
Fight Mode, a paid plan), and it turns on "JavaScript Detections", which injects a small script under
`/cdn-cgi/challenge-platform/` into HTML pages (harmless here: the site has no Content-Security-Policy). Requests in
question, all made by a real browser, so normally not challenged, but a challenge on a `fetch()` can't be answered and
shows as a failed request:
- **FFC proxy** (`/api/ffc/<format>`, `functions/api/ffc/`): Draft Strategist's Quick-Start and Fetch Market Value
  call it with `fetch()`. A challenge here would read as "Fantasy Football Calculator unavailable". Its own call out to
  FFC is server-to-server and isn't affected.
- **The service worker**: its install (`cache.addAll` of ~150 files) and its update check (`/sw.js`) are browser
  requests. A challenged file fails the whole precache silently, so offline would stop working for new installs.
- **Installed-app launches**: the first request is a normal page navigation; a challenge there would show
  Cloudflare's interstitial inside the app window.
- Not affected: Sleeper, FantasyCalc, Google Sheets, Ko-fi and Umami are other domains (Cloudflare's setting covers
  only mydraftstrategist.com).

**Two-minute check, after this deploys, on your phone and your computer:**
1. Opt out on each device: open `https://mydraftstrategist.com/?nocount=1` in the browser you installed from, or tap
   **Don't count this device** in a Guide. On an iPhone or iPad, do it **inside the installed app** too (Guide, the link):
   Home Screen apps don't share Safari's storage.
2. Open `/`, `/lineup/` and `/t-score/`. Each should load normally, with no "Checking your browser" page.
3. Lineup Strategist: Sync All Leagues. Draft Strategist: Dashboard, Quick-Start or Fetch Market Value with a Fantasy
   Football Calculator format: players or ADP should load.
4. Offline: once everything has loaded, turn on airplane mode (or DevTools, Network, Offline) and reload each page.
   Each should still open. On the installed app, close it and open it again offline.
5. In Umami (cloud.umami.is), the site should show visits from other people within a day, and none from you after
   step 1. (To check it's live before opting out, open a private window first: it shows up under Realtime.)
6. In Cloudflare, Security, Analytics, Events: anything labelled "Bot Fight Mode" on `/api/ffc/`, `/sw.js` or `/js/`
   paths means real users are being challenged.
If any step fails: turn Bot Fight Mode off (Security, Settings, filter "Bot traffic", Bot fight mode, off). The Free
plan has no way to exempt a path. The rest of the site's protection (DDoS, the WAF's free managed rules) stays on.

**Left over.**
- The six screenshot baselines above, from CI, once a PR is open.
- Custom events (Ko-fi tap, Share, Export…) are for K1 to choose; each is a `data-umami-event` attribute, no code.
- `docs/TESTING.md`'s open follow-ups: none touched.

### A1, round 2: a "Don't count this device" link (owner's request)

The owner asked whether opting out in the browser covers the installed app. On a computer and on Android, yes: an
installed app shares storage with the browser (and profile) it was installed from. On an iPhone or iPad, no: a Home
Screen app keeps its own storage, apart from Safari, and has no address bar for `?nocount=1`. The owner: "Yes, add the
link, with 'count this device again' too. Good to allow people to opt out if they want."

- Each Visitor Counts line (both Guides, T-Score's The Research) ends with a link, `<a data-nocount-link
  href="?nocount=1">`. `js/shared/analytics.js` sets its text from the saved choice (**Don't count this device** or
  **Count this device again**) and toggles it on tap, in place, with a toast and no reload, through a document click
  listener (the module is shared, so not a page's `delegate()`; no inline handler). Opting back in loads Umami
  straight away on the live site. The `href` is the fallback if the module never runs. Styled in `css/base.css` like
  the Guide's badge-legend link (the site's green, semi-bold).
- `tests/analytics.spec.mjs`: the link on each page toggles both ways without navigating, survives a reload in its
  new state, loads nothing while opted out and the script once after opting back in; it also works off the live site
  (only saves the choice). 24 tests, both widths, pass; 485 unit tests pass.
- Screenshots: the same six PNGs as round 1 change (a little taller again); compared with main in the container,
  nothing else moved. Still to come from CI.

CI's run 41583853340 (head bb5129d) failed only in `visual.spec.mjs` (699 passed, 41 skipped, 6 failed), on exactly

- **The two Guides** (Draft desktop +81px, phone +219px; Lineup desktop +93px, phone +232px): identical above the new

`mls-league-guide.png` and `tscore-researchTab.png`.

### A1 follow-up: the error banner over a working app (owner's bug report, 2026-11-03)

After PR #217 deployed, the owner's phone showed boot.js's fatal banner ("Something went wrong loading the app",
technical details "Failed to load: https://mydraftstrategist.com/js/shared/analytics.js") over a Lineup Strategist
Dashboard that had loaded and worked. `js/boot.js` treats any same-origin `<script>` that fails to load as fatal (the
case it was written for is one of the app's modules 404ing); the visitor counter's loader is the first same-origin
script the app can do without.

**Cause, as far as a session can tell.** Something stopped that one request on the phone. Candidates: a tracker
blocker matching the file name (none of EasyPrivacy, uBlock's privacy list, AdGuard's spyware sections or Brave's
first-party list has a rule that matches `/js/shared/analytics.js`, checked 2026-11-03, but other blockers and apps
use other lists); a dropped request (every other file came from the service worker's cache, so the new file was the
only network fetch, and `sw.js`'s stale-while-revalidate has no cached copy to fall back to for a file it hasn't
precached yet); or a Bot Fight Mode challenge on it (Cloudflare, Security, Events would show one). The fix covers all
three.

**Fix (test first).** New in `tests/analytics.spec.mjs`: with `analytics.js` aborted as a blocker would, each page
must load with no banner and no console.error (failed on main on all three pages: "fatal boot banner is showing"), and
a real app file that fails (`js/mls/main.js`) must still raise the banner naming it. Then:
- The loader's tag on all three pages carries `data-optional`; `js/boot.js` logs a console.warn for a same-origin
  script with that attribute instead of showing the banner. Only that tag has it.
- `CACHE_NAME` v2.8.148 → v2.8.149. A line under both apps' Unreleased in `CHANGELOG.md`.

If the counter is blocked on a device, that device simply isn't counted (and its Guide link falls back to opening
`?nocount=1`, which does nothing without the loader; harmless, since nothing is counted there anyway).

Noted, not changed: `sw.js`'s stale-while-revalidate answers `respondWith(undefined)` when the network fails and
nothing is cached (`.catch(() => cached)` with no cached copy), which turns one dropped request into a hard failure for
that file. Every precached file is safe; only a file added since the last precache is exposed, briefly. A small
separate change if it's ever worth it.

#### The cause, found with the owner (same day)

The owner: Chrome on Android, no blocker, and Reload didn't clear it; every page showed it, in Chrome and in the
installed app. Opening `https://mydraftstrategist.com/js/shared/analytics.js` directly showed **Draft Strategist's home
page** (unstyled) in Chrome and the app, but the real code in an Incognito tab; after force-stopping the app and
clearing its cache, the app showed the code too. So the phone was being handed `index.html` for that one URL, and
keeping it. Two Cloudflare defaults, both in Cloudflare's docs (read 2026-11-03):
- **Pages' SPA mode.** "If your project does not include a top-level `404.html` file, Pages assumes that you are
  deploying a single-page application" and "serves the root (`/`) for any incoming path that does not correspond to a
  static asset", with status 200. This repo has no `404.html`.
- **Browser Cache TTL, 4 hours by default** on every plan: Cloudflare overrides the origin's `Cache-Control` when it's
  lower (Pages sends `max-age=0, must-revalidate`) unless the zone is set to **Respect Existing Headers**.

Most likely sequence: during the deploy the phone asked for the new file from an edge still on the previous
deployment, got `index.html` (200), Chrome kept it for 4 hours, and the service worker's stale-while-revalidate stored
it as the script (status 200 was its only check) and served it on every load; its background refresh went through the
same browser cache and stored the page again. Incognito has neither cache. It wasn't the code (all files pass
`node --check`; main's copy is byte-identical to the tested one) and it wasn't Bot Fight Mode.

**Fix in `sw.js` (test first; v2.9.0, the middle number because the service worker's behavior changes):**
`isHtmlForAsset(request, response)`: a `text/html` response for a path the service worker treats as a static asset
(`.js`, `.css`, images, fonts). `cachePut` never stores one; stale-while-revalidate keeps serving the cached copy
instead of it; after the install's `addAll`, any such entry is deleted so the first load fetches the real file. A
network failure with nothing cached now answers `Response.error()` instead of `respondWith(undefined)` (the same
failure, stated plainly). `tests/sw-html-for-asset.spec.mjs` (service workers allowed; three tests, both widths): a
script and a stylesheet answered with the home page after install stay cached as the real files, and a page served
for the new file during install isn't kept and the real file is cached once it's there. All three failed on the old
`sw.js` (the cache held `<!DOCTYPE html>` for `analytics.js` and `text/html` for `tscore.css`).

**For the owner (not done; dashboard settings or your call):**
- In Cloudflare, Caching, Configuration: set **Browser Cache TTL** to **Respect Existing Headers**. Pages' own headers
  then apply (browsers re-check each file, cheaply, with its ETag), so a deploy is picked up at once and a wrong answer
  can't stick for 4 hours. Recommended.
- ~~Optionally a `404.html`~~: the owner said yes; done, below.
- Anyone else who loaded the site in the minutes of the deploy may see the banner until their 4 hours run out, or
  until this fix deploys (the new service worker serves the cached real file and drops the page).

#### `404.html` (owner: "Proceed with adding a 404.html page")

- `404.html` at the root: "Page not found", a line saying saved data is safe, and links to `/`, `/lineup/` and
  `/t-score/` (Feather icons, the site's buttons, `css/base.css`). Absolute URLs throughout, since Pages serves it at
  whatever address was asked for; no scripts; `noindex`. Not precached: offline, an unknown address still gets the
  app shell from the service worker (`htmlFallback`), as before.
- With it, Pages stops treating the site as a single-page app: an address it doesn't have gets this page with status
  404, and a missing script gets a 404 rather than the home page with 200. Checked first that nothing depended on the
  old behavior: no page has ever been deleted or renamed (git history), the apps use `#hash` for tabs, and
  `tests/serve.mjs` already gave real 404s, so every spec has run that way all along. `/lineup` and `/t-score` without
  the slash are redirected to the folder by Pages, as before.
- `tests/serve.mjs` now answers a 404 with `404.html`'s content (still status 404), like Pages.
  `tests/not-found.spec.mjs`: an unknown address (top level and deep under `/lineup/`) shows the page styled, with
  its logo and the three links, and a link works; a missing `.js` gets a 404. CHANGELOG line under both apps; README
  (Hosting).

#### Owner's decisions on the follow-up (same day)

- Browser Cache TTL set to **Respect Existing Headers** in Cloudflare (done by the owner).
- The error had stopped on main by then (the phone's 4-hour copy had expired). Asked whether the rest is still needed
  with `404.html` in place, the owner chose to **keep everything**: the `data-optional` banner fix (a blocker or a
  dropped request on the optional counter must never show the fatal banner), the service worker's HTML-for-asset
  guard (v2.9.0, mostly redundant once `404.html` exists, kept as a safety net) and `404.html`.

## S31 — Lineup tab: this week's opponent strength from your weekly rankings

**The reproduction first.** On main, a Weekly rankings file with a `Matchup` column (or `SoS`, `Schedule`, `ROS`) in
the single-list layout was read as season SoS by the parser's vertical engine and saved over the Roster tab's season
SoS grid on Save (the SoS card then said "from your Weekly rankings"). `tests/mls-opponent-strength.spec.mjs`'s first
test uploads an SoS file, then saves such a Weekly file, and checks the SoS, its source and the Roster badge are
unchanged: it failed on main (commit c831d1e) and passes now. The owner's own weekly file is the side-by-side layout
(`QB Player … QB Opponent … QB Matchup`), read by the horizontal engine, which never read SoS, so the owner's files
weren't the ones doing it.

**The owner's answers** (from the file "Weekly-Rankings-All-11-3.csv", plus a ROS file for reference):
1. Headers: my suggestion, plus **a position may come before the keyword** ("WR Opponent", "WR Matchup", as in the
   file). Opponent's team: `Opp`, `Opponent`, `Opp Team`, `Opponent Team`, `vs`. Matchup rank: `Matchup`,
   `Matchup Rank`, `Matchup Rk`, `Opp Rank`, `Opp Rk`, `Opponent Rank`, `OPRK`. **A Weekly file never writes season
   SoS** (its SoS / Schedule / ROS columns are ignored); SoS files, ROS rankings files and the manual grid still do.
2. Scale 1 = easiest like SoS, and **one switch**: "My SoS files rank 1 = hardest" flips it too.
3. **A badge in the row's meta line** ("vs KC 28", "@ KC 3"), green to red on the SoS colors, explained on tap;
   **"vs KC" with no rank too** ("include opponent always"); and **yes on the Waiver Wire's Weekly mode** ("that could
   impact a user's decision to pick up the player or not").

**User-visible effect.**
- Lineup tab: each row (starters and bench) with this week's opponent gets a badge at the end of its meta line, after
  the rank: "vs KC 28" (home), "@ KC 28" (away), coloured like the SoS badge (1 green, 32 red), or "vs KC" in plain
  badge colours when the file has no rank for him (the owner's file has none for K and DEF). Tap or hover: "This week:
  at SEA, matchup 15 of 32 for RBs (1 = easiest, 32 = hardest), from your Weekly rankings." On phones and touch
  screens "vs" / "@" is hidden ("KC 28"), as the SoS badge drops "SoS: ".
- Waiver Wire Assistant: the same badge on Top Available rows and Auto-Find cards when Rank By is Weekly (not ROS, not
  Check a List's cards). Top Available still has no SoS badge (S7 round 3).
- Shown only when the league's Weekly set has opponent data and was uploaded this rankings week
  (`isSameRankingsWeek`, Tuesday 10:00 UTC to Tuesday), so last week's matchups never show as this week's.
- "vs" / "@": from the file ("vs KC", "@ KC", "at KC"); for a bare "KC" from ESPN's scoreboard when it names the same
  opponent; else "vs". A row with a Matchup rank but no Opp column takes the opponent from the scoreboard
  (`State.gamesByTeam`, `refreshGameTimes`).
- The upload preview says so: "This file also has this week's opponents and matchup ranks (29 players), read as 1 =
  easiest, 32 = hardest [or: read as 1 = hardest and flipped…]. They show on the Lineup tab, and in the Waiver Wire
  Assistant when Rank By is Weekly, until next week's rankings. Your season SoS isn't changed."
- Fixed: a Weekly file no longer changes season SoS.
- Guide → What the badges mean → Schedule: a new line with three samples (vs KC 3, @ KC 28, vs KC).

**What changed and where.**
- `js/shared/rankings/parse.js`: `parseRankingsFiles(…, { weekly })`. With it, the vertical engine reads no season
  SoS and reads `Opp` / `Matchup` (any of the headers above, optionally after a position); the horizontal engine reads
  each section's `<pos> Opponent` / `<pos> Matchup`. Stored on the player as `opp` (team code, aliases JAC→JAX,
  WSH→WAS), `oppHome` (true / false / null) and `oppRank` (1–32 as the file has it, never flipped when stored). Blank
  or non-team cells add nothing. New exports `parseOpponentCell`, `parseOpponentRank`. ROS uploads are read as before.
- `js/mls/rankings/uploadPreview.js`: passes `weekly`; the new `#rankingsPreviewOppNote` (`lineup/index.html`).
- `js/mls/opponent.js` (new, in `PRECACHE_ASSETS`): when to show, the flip at display time (`State.sosReversed`),
  the scoreboard fallback, the tip, `getOpponentBadgeHTML(player)`.
- `js/mls/badges.js`: `opponentBadgeMarkup` (a button with `data-action="explainSoS"`, which toasts `data-tip`;
  `{ sample }` for the legend) and `sosColorStyle(rank)`, now shared with `sosBadgeMarkup` (same output as before).
- `js/mls/render/lineup.js` (both row kinds), `js/mls/scout/waivers.js` (`waiverOpponentBadge`, Auto-Find cards),
  `js/mls/scout/topAvailable.js` (rows). `js/mls/legend.js`: the legend line. `css/mls.css`: `.opp-badge*`.
- No storage key: the fields live in the Weekly set's rows (`mls_ranking_sets_weekly`, and `mls_weekly`), so Backup /
  Restore carries them. The optimizer doesn't read them.
- `sw.js`: `opponent.js` precached; `CACHE_NAME` v2.9.1. CHANGELOG: two lines under Lineup Strategist.

**Tests.**
- `tests/unit/rankingsParser.test.mjs`: opponent cells ("vs KC", "vs. kc", "@ KC", "@KC", "at KC", bare "KC", "ATL",
  aliases, blanks, "BYE", "LA", full names), matchup cells (1–32, "#5", "4.4", 0, 33, text), a vertical Weekly file
  (blanks, a DEF row, no season SoS), position-prefixed and alternative headers, a Weekly file's SoS / Schedule / ROS /
  Matchup columns giving no SoS, a ROS upload unchanged, and the owner's side-by-side layout (QB, WR, DEF, FLEX-only,
  K with and without an opponent).
- `tests/mls-opponent-strength.spec.mjs` (both widths) with `tests/fixtures/rankings-weekly-opponents.csv`: the
  reproduction; the preview note; Lineup badges' text (phone without "vs" / "@"), colour, tip, toast, a blank row
  without one, and the switch flipping 15 to 18; empty states (a Weekly file without the columns, the same set a
  week old, a ROS upload with the columns); the scoreboard fallback; the Waiver Wire with Rank By Weekly and ROS.

**Leftovers.**
- On phones the badge often wraps to its own line under the rank. S32 (phone rows) is the place to tidy it, perhaps
  moving it into a row's details as the card suggests.
- Not on the Roster tab, Check a List's cards or the Trending view, and the optimizer doesn't use it (out of scope).
- The owner's ROS file has `ROS` and `Next 4` columns: `ROS` is read as season SoS as before; `Next 4` isn't read.

**Screenshots.** This container's Chromium (153) can't reproduce the baselines, so main and this branch were rendered
the same way here (`RENDER_ENV=any`) and compared with `npm run pxdiff`: only **`desktop/mls-league-guide.png`** and
**`phone/mls-league-guide.png`** change (taller by 103px and 232px), from the new legend line. Every Lineup, Roster and
Scout screenshot is pixel-identical: the screenshot fixtures' rankings have no opponent columns. Those two PNGs are
to be re-taken from CI's renders once a pull request runs ("When your machine can't reproduce the baselines").

**Checks run.** `npm run check`: check-precache OK, 492 unit tests, 723 Playwright tests pass; `visual.spec.mjs`
fails only on the container's environment check (4 tests, 0 ms each), as on main.

### S31, round 2: my review as a user, and the owner's changes (owner: "Do all of them, and (a) for Top Available")

**The review** (the owner's real weekly file in the fixture league, desktop and phone): helpful, most of all for
waiver tiebreakers; clean on desktop. Clunky: (1) on phones "MIN 32" without "vs" / "@" read like a team and a rank,
beside Pos / Flex / move-chip numbers; (2) phone rows grew a line; (3) Top Available stopped being compact; (4) the
green-to-red badge beside the position colors (green RB chip, red QB chip) blurred meanings; (5) the tap text made you
do the math; (6) "vs" was a guess for the file's bare "MIN".

**The owner's answers:** all six, (a) for Top Available (Auto-Find's cards only), and for (2): **merge the badge with
the "vs LV" / "@ BAL" the kickoff time already shows**, so the opponent is named once.

**User-visible effect.**
- The badge now sits on the badge line after the kickoff time, **in place of** the plain "@ JAX" from ESPN's
  scoreboard: "Sun 1:00 PM  @ MIN 32". Without a matchup rank (K, DEF, a blank cell) it stays the plain text. The
  meta line (position, team, rank) is as before S31.
- Who and where come from the scoreboard when it has the game; the file's rank shows only when the file names the
  same opponent (or none). Before the scoreboard loads, the file's opponent shows on its own, with "vs" / "@" only when
  the file says so ("MIN 32", no guess).
- Phones keep "vs" / "@" (the hiding rule is gone).
- Tap text in words first: "This week at MIN: one of the toughest matchups for QBs (32 of 32, where 1 = easiest).
  Already reflected in your Weekly ranks." Bands: 1–8 easiest, 9–24 middling, 25–32 toughest; a flip adds "Flipped,
  since your SoS setting says your files rank 1 = hardest."
- Waiver Wire: on Auto-Find's cards (merged the same way) when Rank By is Weekly; Top Available is back to main's.
- Legend line reworded; samples "vs KC 3", "@ KC 28", "KC 16". Preview note: "They show after the kickoff time on the

## M2 — Material 3: 48px touch targets, one focus ring, and press and hover feedback

From M1's review (proposals 1, 2 and 3) and round 2's correction 3. **Also does S15's focus half** (S15's contrast half
is M6).

**The owner's answers** (asked before building, with Playwright screenshots at 375px of a Lineup row, a Tracker card
and the Add/Sync League card, today and after, at rest, with keyboard focus and while pressed, plus the hit areas drawn):
1. **Focus ring: 2px green with a 2px gap**, inset where a parent clips it, and a 2px green outline on text fields
   (the suggestion).
2. **Press feedback: the state layer plus Expressive's pressed shape morph**, the corners tightening 2px while pressed
   (the suggestion; not "state layer only" or "a slight press-in").
3. **Phone primaries and the league arrows keep their look with a bigger hit area** (the suggestion; not 44 → 48px),
   so no screenshot changes.

**What changed and where.**
- **css/base.css, new last section "FOCUS, TOUCH TARGETS AND STATE LAYERS"**, every page (both apps and T-Score):
  - FOCUS: one `:focus-visible` rule, 2px `--primary-green` with a 2px offset; inputs, selects and textareas get it at
    -1px, on their own border. T-Score had none; it gets this one.
  - TOUCH TARGETS: every `button`, `a[href]`, `summary`, `[role=button]` and `[role=tab]` gets `position: relative`
    and an invisible `::after` centred on it whose insets are `min(0px, (100% - 48px) / 2)`: it grows the control to
    48px only in a direction where it's smaller. The toggle switches use `::before` (their `::after` is the handle).
    Both rules are `:where()`, zero specificity, so a component's own position or `::after` wins (an earlier draft had
    the exclusions in a plain `:not()`, whose specificity beat those rules). Left out: `.rankings-card-toggle` (its
    `::after` already covers its card header), the skip link, the Lineup row's swap and lock (the lock's area covered
    the swap: S32), and the two tooltip containers that are `position: static` on purpose (`.mls-lc-status`, the
    Power Rankings table: their area would have stretched over the whole card or table, as their popup does).
  - Split gaps (rules right after it), where neighbours sit closer than 48px: the close buttons on banners grow away
    from what they close (right, not left); the header's menu button stops 4px short of the top-left corner (where the
    screenshots park the mouse) and shares the gap with the league arrow (Lineup) or the logo (Draft, T-Score); Sync
    stops at the screen's edge (it widened the phone page by 1px) and shares 8px with the draft menu; the Tracker card's
    star grows left and its colour dot right; a card header's "i" takes half of its 12px gap to the Badges link; the
    Scoreboard's Refresh doesn't grow toward its "i"; Who to root for's rows (2px apart) take 1px each; "This lineup
    needs you" and its first fix button don't overlap; the matchup strip grows down, not up; Live Sync and Auto-Sync
    (drawn at 85%) keep their own size.
  - STATE LAYERS: a `::before` in `currentColor` on every button, `role=button` and `a.btn`, 8% on hover inside
    `@media (hover: hover)`, 10% on `:active`, fading in 150ms; none on `:disabled` / `aria-disabled`. `.btn`, `.btn-sm`,
    `.mds-btn-sm`, `.mls-btn-sm` and `.btn-header` go from 6px to 4px corners while pressed, on `--press-morph`
    (new in `:root`: 350ms `cubic-bezier(0.42, 1.67, 0.21, 0.90)`, Expressive's fast spatial curve; M3's tokens can
    replace it). Reduce Motion: no fade and no morph animation; the layer and the corners still change.
- **Removed, because the shared rules now do it:** `.btn`'s hover lift and press shrink, `.btn-primary:hover`'s
  brightness filter and glow, the hover backgrounds of `.btn-secondary`, `.btn-danger`, `.btn-warning`, the
  hamburger's tint and 92% press, the bottom bar's 5% tint and 92% press, `.pos-filter`'s 2px hover lift and tint,
  `.btn-header`'s lift and 96% press, Taken's and Pick's (`.btn-draft`, `.btn-mine`) hover lifts, tints, brightness
  and 95% press, `.mls-week-step`'s hover tint and its own `::before` hit area; `transform` and `filter` dropped from
  the small buttons' transitions (`border-radius` added). Focus rules that only repeated the ring: base.css's
  `.toggle-switch`, `.tooltip-icon` and `.tooltip-container[data-tt-self]` outlines, `.btn-bare`, `.pos-filter`,
  `.badge-legend-link`, `a[data-nocount-link]`; mls.css's `button.sos-badge`, `button.opp-rank-badge`,
  `.mls-lc-name-btn`, `.mls-week-step`, `.mls-week-tile-btn`, `button.ir-slot-badge`, `.mls-move-chip`,
  `.mls-week-fold-toggle`, `.mls-week-extreme`, `.mls-gap-chip`, `.mls-root-player`, `.mls-root-window-head`; mds.css's
  `.edit-link`; and every `outline: none` plus faint glow on `.form-input`, `select`, `.scout-textarea`, `.sos-input`
  and the switches. **Kept because they differ for a reason:** `.mls-segmented-btn` (-2px, inside its group),
  `.close-chip` (-4px, inside a chip), the skip link (white on its green), the slider (rings its thumb), and the
  tooltips' "show the popup on focus" rules (which no longer draw a ring of their own).
- **css/mls.css:** `.mls-segmented` lost `overflow: hidden` (it clipped its buttons' areas); the end buttons round their
  own corners (7px inside the 8px border).
- **tests/touch-targets.spec.mjs** (new, phone project only): at 375px with the fixture data, on every tab of both apps
  (empty and seeded) and T-Score, plus the toast, hit-tests a 48x48px square around each visible control (centred, or
  pushed to either side as far as the control still fills it, for split gaps) and fails on a miss unless the control is
  on `ALLOW` with a reason; fails on any control whose own box another control's area covers, allowlisted or not; and
  fails when the page scrolls sideways. `DISCOVER=1` prints every short control and what took each missed point.
- **tests/focus-ring.spec.mjs** (new, desktop project only): tabs through every tab of both apps and T-Score (until
  focus comes round) and fails on any focused control without a solid outline of at least 2px.
- Both fail on main (checked by running them in a worktree of main): Chromium's own 1px `auto` ring on most controls,
  and short controls on every tab.
- `docs/TESTING.md`: a Conventions entry for these rules and specs, and an "owner's decision" follow-up for text
  fields. `CHANGELOG.md`: a line under each app (Draft Strategist's names T-Score too). `sw.js`: `CACHE_NAME` v2.9.2.

**The allowlist, with reasons** (in the spec): links inside a sentence; the Lineup row's swap and lock (S32); **text
fields and selects** (44 to 45px; the header draft menu 32px; they can't take an `::after`, and growing them changes
every form, so it's left for the owner, below); the static tooltip chips and Power Rankings cells; and the split gaps
above: the phone header (menu button 40px wide, Sync 42px), the position chips with the Badges link and its "i" (rows
8px apart on Scout, columns 3px apart in the Roster counts; M4 resizes chips), the Scoreboard's lists and fold toggles
(rows 2 to 6px apart), its "i" and Refresh, the Command Center's Edit / Fold all and the first card's fold button and
name, "This lineup needs you" and its fix button, the Trade Analyzer's Clear above its search, Draft Strategist's Team
tab Undo buttons, the Tracker's star and dot (47px with rounding), and the 85% Live Sync / Auto-Sync switches (their
label beside them toggles them).

**User-visible effect.** Small buttons look the same but take taps in a 48px square (Taken and Pick, the "i" icons,
the toast's ✕, the header buttons, the league arrows, text buttons like Badges, Fold all and Check). Hover shades a
button instead of lifting it; pressing shades it and tightens its corners instead of shrinking it, so a tap on a phone
now shows at once. Keyboard focus is the same green ring with a gap on every control on all three pages (T-Score had
the browser's), and a text field being typed in has a 2px green outline instead of a faint glow. Cards (the Tracker's
player cards, Scout result cards, the guide banner) still lift on hover: they're cards, not buttons.

**Screenshots.** None re-taken. In this container (Chromium 153, so rendering main and the branch the same way and
comparing with pxdiff), three PNGs differ, by 9 to 10 pixels each, all at the left end of a segmented control
(anti-aliasing of the rounded end button instead of the clipped group): desktop `mls-league-setup` (10),
phone `mls-league-setup` (9), phone `mls-league-scout` (9), within the 10-pixel tolerance. Two other side effects were
found this way and fixed rather than accepted: the matchup strip's area reaching upward shifted anti-aliasing across
the Lineup tab (63 to 104 px), and the 85% sync switches' areas blurred their handle (300 px).

**Left over.**
- **For the owner:** text fields and selects stay 44 to 45px (header draft menu 32px). Making them 48px is a visible
  change to every form; it fits M5 (text fields) or M7/M8 (type scale). Recorded under "Open follow-ups".
- The dense spots on the allowlist are a layout question each: the phone header, the Roster's position counts, the
  Scoreboard lists, the Command Center toolbar. S32 takes the Lineup and Roster rows; M4 the chips.
- The spec measures each tab as the fixture leaves it (folds as they open by default) in a redraft league; dialogs, the
  upload preview, opened folds and dynasty-only rows aren't measured. One of those was caught by an existing spec:
  `mls-now-and-later.spec.mjs` (phone) timed out clicking a dynasty Roster row's In-Season | Dyn badge, because the
  "Long-term > now" chip 3px below it reached 15px up over it. They now split the gap (1px each).
- `--press-morph` should move onto M3's motion tokens when M3 lands; the state layer's 150ms fade too.
- S15 is now fully covered once M6 lands (its contrast half).

### M2, round 2: Draft Strategist's header like Lineup Strategist's

**The owner, after reading the summary:** "Update Draft Strategist's header; it'll be better to have it be consistent
with Lineup Strategist anyways." (Its draft menu and Sync were on the allowlist: 118x32 and 30x32 on phones.)

**What changed.**
- The header picker is shared: Lineup Strategist's league-picker rules moved from css/mls.css to css/base.css ("HEADER
  PICKER"), renamed `.league-select-wrap` / `.league-select-label` → `.header-picker` / `.header-picker-label`
  (lineup/index.html, the comment in js/mls/leagues/sync.js). Its phone rules (logo hidden, picker fills the row) apply
  to a `.header` that has a picker, so T-Score keeps its logo. The picker's transparent-text rule is
  `.header-picker > select.form-input`, so it still beats `select.form-input` further down base.css (the first try lost
  to it and drew Lineup Strategist's league name twice; caught by comparing screenshots).
- index.html: the draft drop-down sits in a `.header-picker` with a `#draftProfileLabel` over it, in a `.cluster` like
  Lineup Strategist's; its inline height, font and width are gone, and so is css/mds.css's own look for it and
  base.css's 130px cap on phones: it now looks like the league picker (45px, `.form-input`). `refreshDraftDropdown`
  (js/mds/state.js) keeps the label in step (every switch goes through it) and, with no drafts, leaves a "No Drafts"
  option instead of an empty drop-down.
- Sync (`#headerSyncBtn`): its inline sizes moved to css/mds.css: as tall as the picker, at least 44px wide (the league
  arrows' size), padded like them. Its custom hit area is gone (the shared one now gives it 48px).
- tests/touch-targets.spec.mjs: Sync is off the allowlist; the header entry now says the menu button touches the draft
  menu in Draft Strategist too.

**User-visible effect.** On phones Draft Strategist's header is the menu button, the draft menu filling the row, and
Sync (44x45); the logo hides as Lineup Strategist's does. From 768px the logo and title show, and the draft menu and
Sync are 45px tall beside them. A long draft name ends in "…".

**Screenshots: all 16 of Draft Strategist's re-take** (8 tabs/states x 2 widths: `mds-empty-{setup,tracker,team,board,
guide}` and `mds-draft-{tracker,team,board}`, desktop and phone): the header is 10px (desktop) or 13px (phone) taller,
so everything below moves down. Lineup Strategist's and T-Score's are unchanged (compared with main in this container:
only the three segmented-control corners above). **Not committed yet:** this container's Chromium can't render CI's
baselines; they come from CI's renders once a pull request runs the check ("When your machine can't reproduce the
baselines", docs/TESTING.md).

### M2: hand-offs to later cards (owner, after round 2)

The owner asked for explicit notes so the cards that take M2's leftovers know. They're in docs/TESTING.md, "Open
follow-ups", under **"Handed on by M2 to later Material 3 cards"** (every session reads that list), one line per card:
**M5** every text field and drop-down 48px tall, with the header's league arrows and Draft Strategist's Sync to 48px
to match, and the fields' `ALLOW` entry removed; **M14** no hover lift on the Tracker's player cards, Scout's result
cards and the guide banner; **M4** 48px targets for the position chips and segmented controls, their `ALLOW` entries
