## T1 — T-Score: in-season data from your new Google Sheet

**User-visible effect.** The T-Score page opens on a new **In-Season** tab (first in the tab bar; the preseason tabs
are one tap away, and a `#tab` link still opens its tab). It shows rest-of-season projections for **WR, RB and TE**
(TE is new to the page): ROS rank, Player, Team, ROS PPG, FP/G, Tgt % (target share), **Move** (an up or down arrow
and the places from YTD rank to ROS rank, "–" for none) and a green **Buy** or red **Sell** chip, with WR / RB / TE
pills and a **Buy & Sell only** switch. A line above the table says when the rankings were loaded ("Updated today at
4:00 PM"), the description says which week they were projected after, and **How it's projected** (closed by default)
shows the formula, the Model Weights table (intercept, weights, no-prior adjustment, R², Buy/Sell line per position),
what each term means and the Buy/Sell rule. Under the table, the data credit and licenses (nflverse CC BY 4.0,
ffopportunity CC BY-SA 4.0, the projections shared under CC BY-SA 4.0). The tab fetches the sheet every time the page
opens, draws the copy saved by the last visit first, and offline (or when Google doesn't answer) keeps that copy with
"Couldn't reach the sheet, so these are the last rankings that loaded (…)". A first visit offline says the rankings
couldn't load. A tab missing a column the table needs says so in a plain message ("The WR sheet is missing a column
this table needs: Target share.") and keeps that position's last good rankings if it has them. **Sync with most recent
data** fetches the In-Season sheet too. Draft Strategist is unchanged.

**What changed and where.**
- `js/tscore/inSeasonParse.js` (new, pure): reads each position tab and Model Weights by header name (case, spacing
  and column order don't matter; extra columns are ignored; Move falls back to YTD rank − ROS rank when "Rank move" is
  gone); a missing column throws `InSeasonColumnError` naming each one.
- `js/tscore/inSeason.js` (new): the four published-CSV URLs (`IN_SEASON_URLS`, gid 0 WR, 1 RB, 2 TE, 3 Model
  Weights of "T-Score In-Season (public)"), fetching through `mdsFetch`, the saved copy, drawing, the WR / RB / TE
  pills and the Buy & Sell filter. Fails quietly (no `console.error`): what went wrong is drawn on the tab.
- `js/tscore/main.js`: the default tab is `inSeasonTab` (`DEFAULT_TAB`; was `researchTab`), `initInSeason()` on
  DOMContentLoaded, Refresh also calls `refreshInSeason()`, two `data-action`s. `t-score/index.html`: the tab and its
  nav button; the Refresh button's title. `css/tscore.css`: the tab's styles (`.tscore-move`, `.tscore-chip-buy` /
  `-sell`, the message, the credit; the credit's inline links keep their text size: WCAG's inline exception).
- New key `tscore_inseason_cache` (`KEYS.tscore.inSeasonCache`): `{ wr, rb, te, weights, fetchedAt }`, read only by
  the T-Score page; in neither app's backup, like the other `tscore_` keys. The preseason `tscore_cache` that Draft
  Strategist reads is never written by the In-Season tab.
- `sw.js`: the two modules in `PRECACHE_ASSETS`; `CACHE_NAME` v2.9.19. The service worker still lets the sheet's
  cross-origin requests through; offline works from the saved copy, as the preseason tables do.
- Tests: `tests/tscore-inseason.spec.mjs` (draws the fixture, the filter and positions, the panel, saved copy and
  offline, first visit offline, reordered columns and escaping, missing columns with and without a saved copy, Sync,
  deep links); `tests/unit/tscoreInSeason.test.mjs`; fixtures `tests/fixtures/tscore-inseason/{wr,rb,te,weights}.csv`
  (rows from the public sheet of 2026-11-11, after week 9). `helpers.mjs`: `openApp(page, path, { beforeGoto })`,
  `stubInSeasonSheets`, `inSeasonFixture`, `IN_SEASON_SHEET_RE`; `inSeasonTab` first in `TSCORE_TABS` (so smoke,
  touch-targets, focus-ring and visual cover it). `tscore-refresh.spec.mjs`'s route skips the In-Season sheet;
  `chevrons` and `support` open `/t-score/#researchTab`; `touch-targets` allows `.tscore-credit a`.

**Owner's decisions (2026-11-11).**
- Option A: an **In-Season** tab beside the preseason ones, first and the default view.
- Columns: ROS rank, Player, Team, ROS PPG, FP/G, Target share, Move and Buy/Sell (as sketched, without XFP/G and
  FPOE/G).
- Fetch on every page open, saved copy first, last data offline. Draft Strategist: no change.
- **Licensing.** The first version of the sheet was built from the owner's Late-Round Fantasy Football data dump, whose
  terms allow personal use only and forbid sharing it or anything derived from it, modified or not; its ROS PPG, ranks
  and Buy/Sell were computed from Late-Round's XFP/G and FPOE/G, and a published CSV carries every column whatever the
  page shows. The owner stopped publishing that sheet, had the model re-run on open data (nflverse player stats and
  ffopportunity's expected points; the weights were already fit on nflverse) and published a separate sheet, "T-Score In-Season
  (public)", holding only the site's columns plus YTD rank and Model Weights. The credit line is the one that session
  suggested. Preseason data came from other sources and is fine. The first links (2PACX-1vRM3t…) must never be used.
- The "How it's projected" panel: yes, once the weights were open-data based.

**Screenshots.** Compared main and this branch in the same container (`RENDER_ENV=any`, pxdiff): every existing
T-Score PNG differs only in the tab-bar row (the new In-Season button, y 268–300 desktop, 348–380 phone), and
`tscore-inSeasonTab.png` is new (the fixture). Re-taken on CI with `[update screenshots: …]`: desktop and phone
`tscore-inSeasonTab`, `tscore-researchTab`, `tscore-top50Tab`, `tscore-valuesTab`, `tscore-avoidsTab`,
`tscore-sleepersTab`, `tscore-tab-2024` (14). No other app's PNGs changed. The Update screenshots run (#8, commit
"Screenshots re-taken on CI (14 PNGs)") passed the guard on its first try; I opened the PNGs it committed: the
In-Season tab as in the fixture, and only the tab bar's new pill elsewhere.

**Round 2 (owner, 2026-11-11): labels and a write-up.**
- **Labels.** The owner asked for the preseason tabs' labels back. Options: (A) percentiles of ROS PPG, as preseason;
  (B) fixed rank tiers per position; (C) natural breaks. Owner's pick: **A**, open to adjusting the thresholds, with
  **Flex Play** in place of Boom/Bust, worked out by the site. The adjustment: the percentiles are taken over a fixed
  pool per position, **twice the Buy/Sell line** (WR 96, RB 72, TE 36), not over every row in the sheet, whose size
  moves with its games-and-XFP filter (about 136 / 86 / 64 rows after week 9) and would relabel players whose
  projection didn't move. The preseason cutoffs (top 5 / 10 / 20 / 35 / 50%) give WR 1–5 Elite, 6–10 High-End
  Starter, 11–19 Strong Starter, 20–34 Quality Contributor, 35–48 Flex Play; RB 1–4, 5–7, 8–14, 15–25, 26–36; TE 1–2,
  3–4, 5–7, 8–13, 14–18; past the line, Depth Player. So Flex Play ends where Buy and Sell are measured. The lines come
  from Model Weights (defaults 48 / 36 / 18). A `Label` column in the sheet, if ever added, overrides the computed one
  (known names only). The Label column sits after Player, with the preseason badges (`label-*`; Flex Play uses
  `label-boom`'s red). How it's projected gains a Labels line and a Label × WR / RB / TE table of rank ranges. Code:
  `labelCutoffs`, `inSeasonLabel`, `LABEL_BANDS` in `js/tscore/inSeasonParse.js`.
- **Write-up.** "The In-Season Model: Rest-of-Season Points per Game" opens The Research (`#inSeasonModel`), above the
  preseason article and a rule: why a second model (the owner's words, lightly edited and expanded), a What Changed
  table (preseason vs in-season), the three ingredients, how it's fit (2016–2025, refit weekly; week-9 weights and R²
  in prose), reading the In-Season tab, what it doesn't know, and the data credit. The In-Season tab's description
  links to it ("How it differs from the preseason T-Score", `data-action="readInSeasonModel"`). It doesn't mention
  that an AI assistant helped build the model (asked, no answer: left out, easy to add). The week-9 numbers in "How
  It's Fit" will drift as the weights are refit; the paragraph points to the live panel.
- **A fix to round 1:** on phones the In-Season tab's page scrolled sideways (612px wide): the Move column's `.sr-only`
  words are absolutely placed and were held by the page, outside the table's scroll box. `.tscore-move { position:
  relative }` fixes it; a spec checks the page's scroll width, another that What Changed fits a phone.
- **Screenshots:** this branch before and after, rendered in the same container: only `tscore-inSeasonTab.png` and
  `tscore-researchTab.png` change (desktop and phone); re-taken on CI. The tagged push's run was cancelled by the
  next push (the LOG commit, seconds later: the workflow keeps one run per branch), so I started it by hand with the
  same four names (workflow_dispatch, `expected`); it committed exactly those. Lesson: push the tagged commit last.

**Round 3 (owner, 2026-11-11): labels by points, not rank (pending the sheet).** The owner pointed out that round 2's
"A over a fixed pool" is B's method (fixed rank cutoffs) with A's percentages; a percentile within a list is only a
rank over the list's size, so A, B and round 2 all ignore the projections themselves. Options given: keep it; A over
every row; or (3) ROS PPG cutoffs per position, set from history at the preseason percentiles, as the preseason T-Score
labelled by score cutoffs (65.5, 57.4, …). Owner's pick: **3**, because a tier's size can then change from week to
week and season to season (the owner's example: an elite group of WRs, then a big drop, "the death of the WR2").
The owner's sheet session computed the cutoffs and added them to Model Weights (rows 7–14): a block headed
`Label cutoffs (ROS PPG),WR,RB,TE`, one row per label down to Flex Play, then `Cutoffs from,2016–2025 projections
after week 9`. In tenths, at the owner's request. They're the 95th / 90th / 80th / 65th / 50th percentiles of this
week's model applied to 2016–2025 players at the same week (2,886 player-seasons, the public sheet's filter); that
session recomputes them weekly with the weights (`ros_model_open.py`, run by hand for now). After week 9: WR 15.9 /
14.3 / 12.2 / 9.9 / 8.2, RB 17.1 / 15.3 / 13.1 / 10.6 / 8.8, TE 12.6 / 11.0 / 9.3 / 7.6 / 6.3.

**Built.** `parseInSeasonWeights` finds the block by its header row wherever it sits (columns by name, `Boom/Bust`
accepted for Flex Play) and keeps a position only with all five numbers; `inSeasonLabelByPoints` labels by ROS PPG ≥
cutoff; `labelFor` takes the sheet's own Label, then points, then the round-2 rank labels (the fallback when the block
is missing). The panel's Labels line and table show the cutoffs ("15.9+", "under 8.2") and "Cutoffs from"; the Label
tooltip, The Research's What Changed row and its Label paragraph say how they're set (with one week-9 observation:
seven Elite WRs, fewer than usual in the WR2 tiers, from the sheet session's report). The fixture's Model Weights
has the block, and WR gains Dontayvion Wicks (8.01, Depth). Specs: labels by points (seven Elite WRs, Watson 15.79
High-End, Wicks Depth, the TE list), the panel's cutoffs, the fallback without the block; unit tests for the block and
the labels. Screenshots: `tscore-inSeasonTab.png` and `tscore-researchTab.png` (desktop, phone) change, compared
before and after in this container; re-taken on CI with this commit.

**From the sheet session's report, for the owner** (not on the site): the 2026 top is unusually strong (top-6 WRs
average 18.99 projected PPG vs 17.95 for 2016–23) and WRs 13–24 are about 0.6 points lower in 2024–26, but there's
no new cliff right below the elite group, and 2022–23 had gaps as wide. Players near a cutoff (Watson 15.79 vs 15.9)
can change label from week to week. That session also found that its training skipped 2015, so 2016 players had no
prior; it offered a fix and a weekly scheduled refresh: both the owner's call, outside this repo.

**Left over.**
- On phones the table scrolls sideways (Player stays put), as the preseason tables do; ROS PPG and Buy/Sell sit past
  the first screen. M6 and M7 (T-Score's look) could fold columns on phones.
- The hero still says "Wide Receiver and Running Back" and the page's description, link preview and Share text are
  preseason-only (`SHARE_DATA.tscore`, `og-tscore-1.png`); worth a word about in-season and TE when the look changes.
- On a phone the tab bar now holds seven pills, so the active one from "Values at ADP" on starts past the right edge
  (the bar scrolls; `tscore-valuesTab.png` phone shows it). Scrolling the active pill into view on a switch is small;
  left for M6 (T-Score's tabs), which re-takes these PNGs anyway.
- T2 (T-Score's start-up) now also has the In-Season fetch at load; it's async and draws the saved copy first.
- Each week the owner updates the public sheet; the site picks it up on the next page open. The tab's title says
  "2026"; next season it needs the year (or the sheet could carry it).

## W1 — CI: docs-only pull requests pass the required check in seconds

**User-visible effect.** None on the site (no served file changes, so no `CACHE_NAME` bump and no CHANGELOG line).
For the owner: a pull request that changes only docs passes the required `check` in seconds instead of ~20 minutes,
so it can merge right away. Every other pull request, and every push to `main`, runs the full suite as before.

**Owner's decisions (2026-11-12).**
- Docs-only means every changed path is under `docs/` (any file, the review PNGs too), or ends in `.md` and isn't
  under `tests/`, `scripts/` or `.github/`. `llms.txt`, `robots.txt` and `sitemap.xml` are not docs (the site serves
  them). Checked first: no page, spec or unit test reads a `.md` file, and `noEmoji.test.mjs` scans only
  `.js/.mjs/.css/.html`.
- Pushes to `main` always run the full suite; only pull requests get the shortcut.

**Built** (`.github/workflows/check.yml`). Checkout now fetches depth 2, so on a pull request GitHub's merge commit
and both parents are there. A new first step, `changes` ("Docs-only pull request?"), lists
`git diff --name-only --no-renames HEAD^1 HEAD` (main's tip against the merge commit: exactly what the pull request
changes) and writes `docs_only=true` only when every path matches the list. It runs everything when it can't tell:
not a pull request, no merge commit, a failed diff, no files listed, or a path git quotes (unusual characters).
`--no-renames` makes a rename count by both paths, so moving an app file into `docs/` still runs the suite. Every
later step has `if: steps.changes.outputs.docs_only != 'true'` (combined with the existing `!cancelled() && …` and
`failure()` conditions). The job keeps the name `check`, so the required status still matches. Plain shell, no
third-party action. The log and the run's summary say which way it went, why (the non-docs files, up to 20), and the
changed files. A header comment in the workflow says never to use `[skip ci]` or `paths-ignore`.
`docs/TESTING.md` ("The checks", CI bullets) documents the rule.

**Checked.** The step's script, run against real merge commits in a scratch repo: docs and `.md` only → true
(including a PNG in `docs/` and a `.md` outside it); an app `.js`, `tests/README.md`, `.github/x.md`, `llms.txt`, a
`js/` → `docs/` rename, a deleted `.js`, a push event, a non-ASCII path and a commit with no second parent → false;
a 3,000-file diff with one `.js` → false with no SIGPIPE under `pipefail` (`sed -n` instead of `head`).
check-precache and `node --test` pass (the full Playwright suite isn't affected and wasn't run: no app file changed).

**Proof runs on CI** (two throwaway draft pull requests into this card's branch, with the owner's OK; both closed).
- Docs-only: #236 changed only `docs/improvements/analytics.md`. [Run 43160610607](https://github.com/btrerise/My-Draft-Strategist/actions/runs/43160610607)
  passed in 8 seconds; its log says "Docs-only pull request: npm, Playwright and the checks are skipped (every changed
  file is docs)", and setup-node, `npm ci`, the Playwright install, render-env, `npm run check` and the artifact upload
  are all marked skipped.
- App file: #237 changed only a comment in `js/shared/names.js`. [Run 43160615884](https://github.com/btrerise/My-Draft-Strategist/actions/runs/43160615884)
  ran everything and passed (`npm run check` 20 min 25 s, the job about 21 minutes).
- Note: GitHub runs a step without its own `shell:` as `bash -e {0}`, without `pipefail`; the script works under both.

**Left over.**
- The throwaway branches `w1-proof-docs` and `w1-proof-app` are still on GitHub: this session's git couldn't delete a
  remote branch. Delete them from the repo's Branches page (Cloudflare Pages also made a preview of each; harmless).
- W1's own pull request changes `.github/`, so it runs the full suite, as it should.

## S29 — Power Rankings: what your last trade did

**User-visible effect.** On the Roster tab of a Sleeper league, a new box sits under the Positional Power Rankings
table (round 2; it was first under the strip): **"Since your trade with Rival on Sep 8"**, who got whom ("You got Bijan Robinson, Jahmyr Gibbs, Saquon Barkley",
best first by the strip's rankings), then one line per team with only the ranks that moved, right before → right after
the trade ("You: Start 2 → 1 · RB 2 → 1 · WR 1 → 2", green better, red worse; "no rank changes" when none moved).
Columns: Start, Ovr, QB, RB, WR, TE, and Future in dynasty and keeper leagues. With more than one trade this season, a
**Trade** drop-down ("Sep 12: with Rival") picks any of your trades this season (round 3: yours only); it opens on
your newest, and your pick lasts for the visit. A note under it: "Power Rankings right before → right after
this trade, out of N teams, by today's rankings. Moves since then are left out. 1 = strongest." Manual league, no
trades this season, or Sleeper failing: no box, no toast.

**The owner's decisions (Asks you, 2026-11-12).**
1. **Which moves: trades** ("what I mainly care about"). Waiver and free-agent moves are read only to rebuild rosters.
2. **How far back: the whole season, as a log to pick from** (owner asked for a list of all the league's trades rather
   than two weeks, since dynasty rankings change about monthly). Weeks 1 to the current week, every trade in the league.
3. **Where: under the Roster tab's Power Rankings strip** at first; moved under the full table in round 2. Not on the
   Dashboard.
4. **The other team: one line** ("could be interesting to see how much a trade helped/hurt after some time").
5. **Each trade on its own:** "after" is the league right after the trade (every later move undone), "before" the same
   with the trade undone too, both scored with today's rankings. So a trade's numbers are what the Trade Analyzer showed
   for it beforehand (with today's rankings), and two lines don't count the same move twice. The catch: the "after"
   can differ from today's strip when moves came later. The owner asked to check this once built, and confirmed it in
   round 2.

**What changed and where.**
- `js/shared/api/sleeper.js`: `getSleeperTransactions(leagueId, week)` (throws on a non-ok response). S34 can reuse it.
- `js/mls/power/pastTrades.js` (new, pure): `completedMoves` (complete only, deduped, newest first by
  `status_updated`), `undoMove` (each player in a move's adds or drops goes back to the team that dropped him, else off
  every roster; one rule for trades, waiver claims, free-agent adds and drops and commissioner moves), `undoPicks`
  (each traded pick back to `previous_owner_id`, matched by season, round and original team; picks league.picks
  doesn't list are skipped), `leaguesAroundMove`, `tradeEntries`, `defaultTradeId`, `rankChanges`, `makeOwnerOf`
  (roster id → owner as the sync names teams: "You", else `league.teamNames`; a name no roster uses becomes "Unknown
  Team" when that exists, else nobody, so an undone move never adds a team to the rankings).
- `js/mls/power/tradeLine.js` (new): fetches weeks 1..current in parallel (plus the cached player map) for the active
  league only, when the Roster tab is showing, once per visit; a re-sync (new `globalRosterMap`) asks again. Moves after
  the league's `lastSyncedAt` are ignored (today's rosters don't have them yet). Scores with
  `computePositionalPower` and the strip's own source (`resolvePowerRankingsSource`: In-Season and dynasty sets per
  S23, Market Consensus fallback) and future values (picks count in Future, as in S24).
- `js/mls/power/rosterCard.js`: `refreshPowerRankings` calls `renderPastTradeLine` (and hides it with the strip).
- `js/mls/main.js`: the `selectPastTrade` change action. `lineup/index.html`: `#rosterTradeLine` (hidden). `css/mls.css`:
  `.mls-roster-trade*` (reuses `.mls-trade-rank-up/down` for the colors). No new badge or chip, so no legend line.
- `sw.js`: the two new files in `PRECACHE_ASSETS`; `CACHE_NAME` v2.9.19 → v2.9.20. No storage key.
- Fixtures: `league-transactions.json` (no moves, served by default for every league and week),
  `league-transactions-trade.json` (the week-1 trade: your Chase, Jefferson, Lamb for Rival's Bijan, Gibbs, Saquon, plus
  a failed waiver claim) and `league-rosters-traded.json` (rosters after it), from `make-fixtures.mjs`;
  `tests/helpers.mjs` routes `transactions/<week>`.

**Tests.** Unit (`tests/unit/pastTrades.test.mjs`, 16): the undo rule for a trade, a waiver claim with a drop, a
free-agent add alone and a drop alone, a commissioner move, unknown players and teams, picks (back one step, passed on
twice, a draft already held, with a trade's players), failed and duplicate moves, several moves undone newest first with
a player since dropped again, and a scored check that "before" and "after" equal the Trade Analyzer's
(`leagueAfterTrade`) for the same trade. Spec (`tests/mls-past-trade.spec.mjs`, both widths, phone at 375px): the
Trade Analyzer's "after the trade" ranks for the fixture trade, then the trade goes through on Sleeper and the line
shows exactly those changes (and Rival's the other way); two trades (drop-down, newest of yours first, the older one
alone after the later is undone, the pick kept across tab switches); no trades (two requests on opening the Roster tab,
none before it, none again later); Sleeper failing (no box, no toast); a manual league (no box, no request).

**Screenshots.** None change: the box is hidden in every screenshot (no fixture league has a trade). All 44 PNGs,
rendered in this container on main and on the final branch, are pixel-identical (`npm run pxdiff`, 2026-11-12).

**Left over.**
- A trade made after the last sync isn't listed until the league is synced again (its players aren't on today's
  rosters yet). A note saying so would need a comparison with Sleeper's list; not built.
- Trades from last season (a dynasty league's previous season, `previous_league_id`) aren't read; neither are picks
  for drafts already held. Offseason trades in Sleeper's current-season league are (week 1).
- The Guide's Roster tab text doesn't mention the box (a sentence there re-takes `mls-league-guide` at both widths).

### S29, round 2 (owner, after trying it)

1. **Moved under the full Power Rankings table** (inside `#powerRankingsCard`, after `#powerRankingsOutput`): under the
   strip it pushed the start of the roster down on phones. `lineup/index.html`, `css/mls.css` (top margin instead of
   tucking under the strip); the spec checks the box follows the table.
2. **Trades not involving you:** the owner was torn (interesting, or clutter, since users mostly care about their own).
   My view: yours only (the box answers "what did my trade do?"; a busy league's list would bury yours, and the full
   table already compares teams). Owner: **"Yours only"** (round 3).
3. **Question 5 (each trade on its own):** confirmed.

### S29, round 3: your trades only (owner)

`yourTrades` (`js/mls/power/pastTrades.js`) keeps only trades you were in; the box's title is always "Since your trade
with …", the drop-down's options read "Sep 12: with Rival", and a league where only others traded shows no box. The
undo rule still reads every move (others' trades and pickups too) to rebuild rosters. Unit test for `yourTrades`; the
spec adds "a trade you weren't in: no line".

### S29, round 4: a review as a user (owner: "All four")

My review, wearing the user's hat: helpful (nothing else says what a trade did) and in the app's style, but (1) easy to
miss under the table, (2) the answer came after the player list, (3) no verdict, so you add up the ranks yourself, (4)
the bold rank run was louder than the table, (5) a three-line footnote on phones. The owner took all four fixes:
- **A. Found from the strip:** when the box shows a trade, the strip's link reads "See the full breakdown and your last
  trade below ↓" (`stripLink` in `tradeLine.js`, after `renderRosterPowerStrip` draws its own text; back to the old
  text with no trade).
- **B. The answer first:** title, then a verdict sentence (`tradeVerdictText` in `pastTrades.js`: "Your starting lineup
  got stronger (6th → 3rd of 12); overall roster weaker (4th → 6th)." Start first, then Ovr and Future when they moved),
  then each team's ranks, then who got whom in smaller text.
- **C. Calmer ranks:** each change is its own item (weight 500, 14px apart, never split across lines), no dot separators.
- **D. Short note:** "Right before → right after, by today's rankings." with the rest (rebuilt from Sleeper's moves, out
  of N teams, later moves left out, 1 = strongest) behind an (i). The spec checks the tooltip opens on screen at 375px.

Unit tests for `tradeVerdictText`; the spec checks the order, the sentence, the note, the weight and the strip's link
(and its usual text with no trade). Screenshots: still none (no trades in the fixtures, so the link and box don't show).

### S29, round 5: a second review as a user (owner: "Do 2-5")

Tried on a dynasty league (Future, a pick in the trade). Fixed:
- **Title:** "Your trade with Rival · Sep 8" (was "Since your trade…", which read as "from then until now" against the
  right before → right after numbers).
- **Verdict says what the ranks hide:** the ranks are in the lines under it, so the sentence now gives your starting
  lineup's change as a percent of the league average, as the Trade Analyzer's headline does (`tradeVerdictText`,
  `startPercent`: "Your starting lineup stayed 1st of 2 and got stronger (100% → 107% of league average)."). Ovr and
  Future are no longer repeated in it.
- **The other team's line muted** (`.mls-roster-trade-other`: grey label, smaller, 70% opacity); yours stays in full.
- **No break inside a parenthesis** on phones (`.mls-roster-trade-nowrap`).

**Question 1 of the review (a trade made since the last sync).** The owner asked how the site could know a trade exists
without knowing what it is. It does know what it is (Sleeper's list has the players and picks); what it lacks is today's
rosters, which are the last sync's, and the box starts from those and undoes moves. Proposed instead of a Sync button:
apply moves made after the last sync forward onto the saved rosters. The owner chose an automatic sync instead (round 6).

### S29, round 6: a trade since the last sync syncs the league by itself (owner)

The owner asked for a trigger: when the box finds a trade of yours completed after the league's last sync, sync the
roster automatically. `renderPastTradeLine` (`tradeLine.js`): once per league per visit, while the Roster tab shows, it
calls `processSleeperData` as the Roster tab's Sync button does (`isRefresh`, the Added / Dropped / Now OUT toast), with
its error toast off. Meanwhile the box shows the new trade's title and "New since your last sync: updating this league
from Sleeper…". The moves just read are kept for the redraw (`keepAfterSync`), so Sleeper isn't asked for them again.
On a failed sync the box falls back to what it showed before (no toast; the sync logs to the console and flags the
league "Last sync failed" on the Dashboard, as any failed sync does).

What else the sync does, the same as the button: it **re-optimizes this league's lineup** (`optimizeLineup(true)`,
manual swaps reset), refreshes the roster, records and the Power Rankings, and clears the Dashboard's stale-sync age.
Only a trade of yours triggers it, not a waiver claim or another team's trade.

Spec: "a trade since the last sync" (the league syncs once, the box shows the trade with the Trade Analyzer's numbers,
the Added toast, the moves aren't read again, no second sync on the next visit to the tab). Also fixed: the "Sleeper
fails" test's toast check used a selector that matched nothing (`.toast-message` now).

Not tested: a failed automatic sync (the sync's own `console.error` fails `expectClean`); the code path falls back to
the box as it was.

### S29, round 7: a third review as a user (owner: "Go ahead")

Walked through the automatic sync as a user and tried to break it. Found and fixed:
1. **Bug: a league sync that finished after you switched leagues took you back to it.** `processSleeperData` always set
   `State.activeLeagueId` to the league it synced, while the header kept the league you'd switched to, so the Lineup and
   Roster tabs showed one league's data under the other's name. The Roster tab's Sync button had this on main too;
   round 6's automatic sync made it likely on a slow connection. Test first (`tests/mls-sync-league-switch.spec.mjs`:
   the Sync button and the automatic sync, each with the rosters request held until after the switch; both failed
   before the fix). Fix in `js/mls/leagues/sync.js`: a single-league refresh (`isRefresh`, not `skipSave`) whose league
   is no longer active saves the league and stops there: no `activeLeagueId`, rankings, lineup re-optimize or redraw,
   the button put back, and its summary prefixed with the league's name. Sync All and Import All (`skipSave`) are
   unchanged.
2. **The sync is visible where you're looking:** the automatic sync now runs through `syncLeagueForTrade` (sync.js),
   which puts the Roster tab's Sync button in its spinner state, as a tap does (`ROSTER_SYNC_BUSY_HTML`, shared with
   `syncActiveLeague`).
3. **The summary says why:** "Synced for your trade on Sep 8: Added: … · Dropped: …" (`toastPrefix`, a new last
   option of `processSleeperData`; `rosterChangeText` builds the summary for both paths).

The new spec ran 12 times at both widths with no failure (its first version read the stored roster before the sync had
saved it; it now polls). CHANGELOG: a line for the fix under Lineup Strategist.

### S29: the full suite before the pull request (2026-11-12)

`npm run check` in this container: precache OK, 583 unit tests, 936 Playwright tests passed (48 skipped). The 4 failures
are `visual.spec.mjs` stopping at its environment check (the cloud image's fonts), as on main; the screenshots were
compared on main and the branch here instead (identical, above). Nothing re-taken.

## N2 — Starter alerts before kickoff (site open), and the shared notification module

**Owner's answers (2026-11-12).**
1. **Which lineup:** the starters set in Sleeper (what scores), not Lineup Strategist's recommendation.
2. **How long before kickoff:** a drop-down in Settings, 15 to 120 minutes in steps of 15, 60 by default. (The help
   text says inactives come out 90 minutes before kickoff, so 75 or less catches them.)
3. **Questionable:** doesn't count (only `SIM_EXCLUDE_STATUSES`: Doubtful, Out, IR, PUP, SUS, NFI, DNR).
4. **Refresh before each window:** yes, "as long as it makes sense with Sleeper's API and how sync is set up". It does:
   Sync All already re-downloads the player file every tap (`forceRefresh`) and each league's roster (which carries
   your Sleeper starters), so the refresh *is* Sync All, run quietly through the same code. About 3 a Sunday (4:05 and
   4:25 share one window), plus Thursday and Monday.
5. **Stale rankings:** kept, as its own switch under the alert ("Also name old Weekly rankings", on by default, only
   used while alerts are on). It rides along in an alert that fires anyway, and on its own at most once a week.
6. **Survive a reload:** yes, a storage key is fine if it's small: `mls_starter_alerts_sent` is
   `{ week, sent: ['leagueId:playerId', …] }`, under 1 KB, started over each NFL week.
7. **Title flash and M10:** keep the title flash (it's in the browser's tab strip, for a hidden tab; M10's badges are on
   the app's own nav bar, seen only while the tab is in view). M10 should count both from the same "needs you" rule:
   noted in `docs/TESTING.md`'s Open follow-ups.

**What changed.**
- `js/shared/ui/notify.js` (new, both apps; N4 reuses it): `notifyPermission()`, `requestNotifyPermission()` (from a
  tap only), `notify({ title, body, tag, url, label })` (always `registration.showNotification()` with `data.url`; with no
  permission or no API: a toast if the tab is in view, else the title flash), `flashTitle()` ("(N) label" / "(N) page
  title" every second, both carrying the count so a hidden tab's once-a-minute timers still show it; put back on
  `visibilitychange`), `notifyNow()` and `notifyAt(ms, fn)` (a timer re-planned at most every 6 hours, for a laptop that
  slept). In `PRECACHE_ASSETS`.
- `sw.js`: a `notificationclick` listener: closes the notification, focuses a window already on `data.url`
  (`/lineup/`; `'/'` matches only Draft Strategist's `/` or `/index.html`), else opens it. `CACHE_NAME` v2.9.19 →
  **v2.10.0** (middle number: the service worker's own behavior changed).
- `js/mls/lineup/starterAlertRules.js` (new, pure): settings, kickoff windows from `State.gameTimesByTeam` (kickoffs
  within 30 minutes of a window's first share it), `dueWindow` / `nextCheck`, `sleeperLineup(league)` (Sleeper's starters
  in `starterSlots` order, slot names mapped to the app's: `SUPER_FLEX` → SFLEX, `REC_FLEX` → WRTE, `WRRB_FLEX` → WRRB),
  `noGameStarters`, `leagueFindings` (what to name, worded by S11's `lineupIssueItems`, so "Ja'Marr Chase is Out: start
  Puka Nacua instead?" reads as on the Lineup tab), `staleLeagues`, `alertMessage`.
- `js/mls/lineup/starterAlert.js` (new): the settings (`mls_starter_alerts`: `{ on, leadMinutes, staleRankings }`),
  the timer (`planStarterAlerts`: the next window's check time; at once if the page opens inside a window's lead time;
  then, after that window kicks off, the next), the check (`runStarterAlertCheck`: quiet Sync All, then S11's
  `getLineupIssues` on each non-Best-Ball league's Sleeper lineup, plus "has no game" and the stale line), and the
  Settings controls. Injured starters are named in their own game's window; a bye, no game or empty slot (no kickoff
  of its own) at the first check that sees it. A player locked in the app is left out (your call, as on the Lineup tab).
- `js/mls/render/dashboard.js`: `syncAllLeagues(btn, { quiet })` returns a promise that settles when the sync is done;
  quiet means no button, no summary toast and the Sync Logs left folded. A Sync All already running is awaited, not
  started twice. The button's behavior is unchanged.
- `lineup/index.html`: the Starter Alerts row under Show Player Headshots in Advanced Settings (a Feather bell,
  `currentColor`), and, while on, the lead-time drop-down, its hint, the stale-rankings switch and a status line
  (blocked, not allowed yet, or no notifications in this browser). `css/mls.css`: their layout.
- `js/shared/storage/keys.js`: `KEYS.mls.starterAlerts`, `KEYS.mls.starterAlertsSent` (both `mls_`, so Backup carries
  them and Factory Reset clears them). `js/mls/main.js` and `init.js`: the three change actions (`data-action`), and
  `initStarterAlerts()` at start-up.

**User-visible effect.** A **Starter Alerts** switch in Lineup Strategist's Advanced Settings, off until you turn it on.
Turned on, the browser asks for notification permission, and while `/lineup/` stays open on a computer (a background
tab is fine) an alert arrives the chosen time before each kickoff window naming starters in your Sleeper lineup who are
Out, Doubtful, on IR or bye, without a game, and empty slots, plus (optionally) leagues with Weekly rankings over 6 days
old. Tapping it brings the Lineup Strategist tab forward. Blocked or unsupported: a toast in view, "(1) Starter alert"
flashing in the browser tab's name while hidden. The Dashboard also gets fresh injury news and Sleeper lineups from the
pre-window Sync All.

**Tests.** `tests/unit/starterAlertRules.test.mjs` (settings, windows, due and next check, the Sleeper lineup, who is
named and once a week, the words), `tests/unit/swNotificationClick.test.mjs` (sw.js in a vm with stubbed `clients`:
focus an open /lineup/, open one, `/` never matches /lineup/, a bad url opens the root), `tests/mls-starter-alert.spec.mjs`
(fixed clock, stubbed Notification and service worker, the fixture league's Sleeper lineup stubbed: off by default with no
prompt; on asks once and alerts once inside the window, not before, not again after a reload, and the refresh picks up a
new Out; a 15-minute lead; Questionable alone and the stale line on and off; denied → toast, then title flash while
hidden and back on `visibilitychange`; no API → toast; opened inside the window → checks at once; Playwright's fake
timers: the timer fires at lead minutes before kickoff and then plans the next window).

### Proposed card: a Settings page in both apps (owner agreed, 2026-11-12; for the runbook)

Raised by the owner during N2 round 6; recommended by this session and agreed, named **Settings**. Not built in N2.

- **Goal.** Each app's Dashboard is for what you do every week (Lineup Strategist: the League Command Center, Sync All,
  "lineups need you", the Week Recap, league cards; Draft Strategist: its draft setup). The set-once settings (Lineup
  Strategist's Advanced Settings card: Show Player Headshots, Notifications, MVP / LVP Lines, Backup & Restore, Danger
  Zone; Draft Strategist's equivalents) move to a **Settings** page in each app, reached from the menu like the Guide
  (drawer, rail and phone menu). Visible effect: a shorter Dashboard, and a Settings entry beside the Guide.
- **Stays where it is.** Settings that belong to one tool stay beside it: Lineup settings (Lineup tab), Value settings
  (Trade Analyzer), Power Rankings and Simulator settings, Active League Requirements.
- **Add Player Manually** stays on the Dashboard, shown only while the open league is a manual one (beside Active League
  Requirements); Sleeper leagues get their players from Sleeper. (Check first whether anyone uses it on Sleeper leagues.)
- **Order.** After N2 merges and **before N4**, so Draft Strategist's "on the clock" switch goes straight into its
  Settings page (N4's Notifications section). Not alongside M10 (badges on the same menu and rail) or another card that
  edits the nav (`js/shared/ui/navMenu.js`, `js/mls/nav.js`, `js/mds/ui.js`) or the Guide.
- **Asks the owner.** A sketch at 375 and 1280px of the menu entry and the page; the page's order of sections; whether
  Draft Strategist's settings all move; the URL hash (`#settings`) and the tab name in the nav.
- **Scope notes.** A new tab in each app (tab ids in `tests/helpers.mjs` `MLS_TABS` / `MDS_TABS`, tab hash, drawer,
  rail, phone menu); the Guide's text that says "Advanced Settings on the Dashboard" (Lineup Strategist steps 1, 12 and
  N2's Starter Alerts paragraph in step 7, the MVP / LVP line, Draft Strategist's equivalents); specs that open those
  sections on the Dashboard (backup, factory reset, headshots, recap lines, mls-starter-alert, touch-targets,
  focus-ring, tooltips-onscreen); `goToLineupNeeds` and other jumps unaffected. Screenshots: both apps' Dashboards
  (empty and league, desktop and phone) and a new Settings pair per app; follow "Accepting an intended visual change".
  CHANGELOG lines under both apps. No storage keys change.
- **Size.** About a 15k-token session (two apps, nav and tests); it changes what users see.

## F17 — 2-QB leagues: the two QBs follow kickoff order

**Owner's decisions** (asked after reproducing, with a 375px before/after picture of the Lineup tab, 2026-11-12):
1. **The same top-to-bottom rule as RB and WR**: earliest kickoff in QB1.
2. Where the owner saw it: **League of Ordinary Gentlemen** (Keeper 2QB Half-PPR: QB 2, RB 2, WR 2, TE 1, FLEX 2,
   DEF 1), **week 10**: Jayden Daniels (WAS, Thursday night) in the lower QB slot below Brock Purdy (SF, Sunday 4:25).
   Nothing else looked wrong there (below).

**The bug, reproduced on main.** `optimizeFlexKickoffOrder` (`js/mls/lineup/kickoffOrder.js`) took QBs out of the
relabel unless the lineup had an open SFLEX slot (F1 kept a 2-QB league "as on main"). So a 2-QB league's QB1/QB2 kept
the rank-based fill's order, while RB1/RB2 and WR1/WR2 read earliest kickoff on top. Fixture league with a second QB
slot (QB 2, RB 2, WR 2, TE 1, FLEX 1): Allen (ranked ahead, Sunday night) stayed in QB1 above Hurts (1 pm).

**What changed and where.**
- `js/mls/lineup/kickoffOrder.js`: the QB-removal block is gone, so the QB slots take part like the RB, WR and TE
  slots: earliest kickoff first, ties and unknown kickoffs keep the fill order. Players whose game has started stay
  put; a manual lock moves (F13). The header comment says so.
- `sw.js`: `CACHE_NAME` v2.10.0 → v2.10.1 (after N2 merged; v2.9.21 before). CHANGELOG line under Lineup Strategist.

**User-visible effect.** With FLEX Kickoff Optimization on, a 2-QB league shows the earlier-kickoff QB in the top QB
slot. Who starts doesn't change. Superflex with an open SFLEX slot and 1-QB lineups are unchanged slot for slot. The one
other lineup that changes: two QB slots plus a SFLEX slot whose player has kicked off now orders its QB slots the same
way.

**Sleeper comparison.** `sleeperLineupChanges` compares sets of ids, so swapping QB1 and QB2 is never a change to set
(checked in the spec, with a control that a real difference does show).

**Tests.**
- `tests/unit/kickoffOrder.test.mjs`, new "2-QB leagues (F17)":
  - 2 QB with no SFLEX: Hurts moves to QB1. RB, WR and TE come out the same as main.
  - Ties and no kickoff data keep the fill order.
  - A QB whose game has started stays put. A manual lock moves.
  - 2 QB plus SFLEX, and SFLEX already played: as on main.
  - 500 random 2-QB lineups: main slot for slot, except the QB slots in kickoff order.
  - The old random test against main's function is now 1-QB only.
  - Three of these fail on main.
- `tests/mls-2qb-kickoff.spec.mjs` (new, both widths):
  - The QB order (fails on main).
  - A Sleeper lineup with the QBs the other way round shows no change to set.
  - With the toggle off, the QBs keep the rank order.

**Checked against the owner's backup** (`my-lineup-strategist-backup-2026-11-12.json`, uploaded to the session, not
committed; week 10 kickoffs from ESPN's scoreboard). Each league's saved lineup was run through main's function and
this branch's:
- League of Ordinary Gentlemen: main keeps QB1 Purdy (Sun 21:25 UTC), QB2 Daniels (Thu 01:15 UTC). This branch: QB1
  Daniels, QB2 Purdy. The rest was already in kickoff order and stays the same: RB Gibbs (Sunday 9:30 am ET) then Tuten; WR Nabers
  (Thursday) then Lamb; FLEX Corum, then Chase (Sunday night). Sleeper already has Daniels first, so the Lineup tab now
  matches it; there was never a change to set (set-based comparison).
- The other 19 leagues (15 superflex with one QB slot, plus 1-QB and Best Ball): identical slot for slot.
- Nothing else wrong in the league: the unranked bench players are four on bye in week 10 (Irving TB, Coleman and
  Waddle DEN, Monangai CHI) and Mike Evans, who isn't in the weekly file at all. Waddle and Monangai are in the IR
  slot, as Sleeper has them.

**Screenshots:** none changed. No screenshot fixture has two QB slots. Main and this branch rendered in this container
(`npm run pxdiff`): 44 PNGs, all pixel-identical.

**Checks** (full `npm run check`, once, before the last push): check-precache OK, 591 unit tests pass, 942 Playwright
tests pass, 48 skipped. The 4 failures are `visual.spec.mjs`'s environment check (this container's fonts differ from
CI's), as on main; the screenshot comparison above stands in for it.

**Left over:** nothing.

## F20 — T-Score: the touch-target test that failed now and then (two links in The Research)

Found during F17 (PR #241): `touch-targets.spec.mjs`, "T-Score: every control has a 48px target" (phone), failed about
1 run in 30, on main too, with `a "CC BY 4.0"` losing its bottom edge to `a "ffopportunity"`'s hit area.

**Why it was intermittent (the cause).** The layout never changed; what changed was whether the tab was measured at
all. T-Score switches tabs without a View Transition (`switchTab` in `js/tscore/main.js` only moves the `active`
class), and `.tab-content.active` plays `fadeIn` (css/base.css), cut to 1ms where View Transitions exist, but it still
**starts at opacity 0**. `showTScoreTab` waits only for the class, so `measure` usually ran before the animation's
first frame: `checkVisibility({ checkOpacity: true })` saw opacity 0 and dropped **every control in the tab** (logged:
Research 0 of its 10 controls measured, and the same on Top-50, Values, Avoids, Sleepers and 2024; only In-Season, the
tab the page opens on, was measured). When a frame happened to pass between the class change and the evaluate, the tab
was measured, and the overlap (there every time: a probe found `ffopportunity` at the test point 40 of 40) was
reported. Not `scrollIntoView`, not the In-Season data load, not fonts (they're aborted in tests). Draft Strategist and
Lineup Strategist weren't affected: `showTab` waits for `data-vt-busy`, which outlasts the 1ms fade.

**The spec, now deterministic** (tests/touch-targets.spec.mjs, `check`): before measuring, it waits for every running
animation that ends (`getAnimations()`, finite end time; a spinner is left alone). On main with only this change, the
T-Score test **fails 100 of 100** (`--repeat-each 100`, phone), every time on Research's `CC BY 4.0`. Nothing else in
the three apps' tabs turned up (the other T-Score tabs, now measured for the first time, are clean).

**Owner's decision** (asked with the cause and the list of sentence links on every page): **(a) links inside a
sentence get no 48px area**, on all three pages (not T-Score only; not (b) the spec ignoring the overlap; not (c) more
line height, which would have needed about 48px from line to line instead of 29).

**Pages with the pattern** (every link at 375px, Guide folds opened), all directly in a `<p>` or `<li>`:
- Draft Strategist: the Guide's "Don't count this device".
- Lineup Strategist: the Guide's "Don't count this device" and Sleepy link, the Scoreboard's Sleepy credit.
- T-Score: In-Season's "How it differs from the preseason T-Score" and its credit (nflverse, CC BY 4.0, ffopportunity,
  CC BY-SA 4.0; T1 had already turned those areas off in css/tscore.css); The Research's "The Data" (the same five),
  "random article" and "Don't count this device".
- Only "The Data" had two links on neighbouring lines whose areas met (In-Season's credit would have, without T1's rule).
- Standalone links keep their area, none of them directly in a `p` or `li`: Ko-fi, "Powered by FantasyCalc", "ADP by
  Fantasy Football Calculator", "Read Methodology", the cross-app banners, the header's logo and "Back to Draft", the
  menu's links.

**What changed and where.**
- `css/base.css`, TOUCH TARGETS, right after the shared rule: `:where(p, li) > :where(a[href])::after { inset: 0; }`.
  Zero specificity, so a component's own `::after` still wins. **`inset: 0`, not `content: none`:** removing the
  pseudo-element made Chromium anti-alias the `)` right after the two "CC BY" links differently (85 pixels in each
  Research screenshot); dropping their `position: relative` as well didn't help. An `::after` at the link's own size
  leaves all 44 screenshots pixel-identical.
- `css/tscore.css`: T1's `.tscore-credit a::after { content: none; }` folded into that rule (a comment points to it).
- tests/touch-targets.spec.mjs: the animation wait above. `ALLOW` is unchanged (M16's): the sentence links fall under
  its existing `[data-inline-link]` entry, and a link that isn't in a sentence is still checked for 48px, so a
  standalone link put directly in a `<p>` or `<li>` would show up as short.
- docs/TESTING.md, Conventions, touch targets: the sentence-link rule and the animation wait. CHANGELOG: a line under
  each app. `sw.js`: `CACHE_NAME` v2.10.2.

**User-visible effect.** Nothing changes on screen. Links inside a sentence now take taps on their own words only, not
in an invisible band above and below. In The Research's "The Data", a tap on the lower half of "CC BY 4.0" used to open
"ffopportunity" on the line below; now it opens the link tapped. Standalone links and every button keep 48px.

**Checks.**
- On this branch: the T-Score test passes 100 of 100 (`--repeat-each 100`, phone), and the whole spec passes.
- Main with the new spec fails 100 of 100 (above).
- Screenshots: main and this branch rendered in this container, `npm run pxdiff`: 44 PNGs, all pixel-identical.
- Full `npm run check` (once, before the last push): check-precache OK, 611 unit tests pass, 964 Playwright tests pass,
  48 skipped. The 4 failures are `visual.spec.mjs`'s environment check (this container's fonts differ from CI's), as
  on main. The pxdiff comparison above stands in for it.

**Left over.**
- For M16 (not changed here, it's that card's list): `ALLOW`'s `.mls-scoreboard-credit a, .tscore-credit a` look
  redundant with `[data-inline-link]` now; check before removing.
- Other T-Score specs call `showTScoreTab` and don't wait for the fade-in either. None checks opacity or visibility
  the way this one did, so none was affected.

## S46 — When you last synced, on the Dashboard

**User-visible effect.** Under **Sync All Leagues** and **Optimize All Lineups** on the Dashboard, a small centered line
with a clock icon says when you last synced your Sleeper leagues: "Last synced 9:42 AM" (today), "Last synced yesterday
8:15 PM", "Last synced Sun 8:15 PM" (within the last 6 days) or "Last synced Nov 9" (with the year when it isn't this
year's), in the device's locale and time zone. Hovering shows the full date and time. It's muted grey normally and
amber when it's old (below). A league whose last sync failed is named after it in red with an alert icon ("Second
League's last sync failed"; two: "Last sync failed: A and B"; three or more: "Last sync failed in 3 leagues", all named
in the hover text). On phones the failed part wraps under the time. With no Sleeper league synced yet (no leagues, or
only manual / Draft Strategist hand-off leagues) there's no line. The Guide's legend line for the amber and red sync
labels now also explains when this line turns amber.

**Owner's choices (2026-11-12, from a sketch at 375 and 1280px; all four were the suggested options).**
1. **Where:** under Sync All and Optimize All, not in the "need you" strip (which hides when nothing needs you).
2. **Which time:** the most recent sync across your Sleeper leagues (manual and hand-off leagues don't count), naming
   any league whose last sync failed.
3. **Amber:** when a kickoff window starts within 3 hours and you haven't synced since 6 AM (local) that day; otherwise
   once the newest sync is over 24 hours old. Kickoffs are the week's game times the app already loads
   (`State.gameTimesByTeam`, grouped into windows by N2's `kickoffWindows`). With no game times (offline, ESPN down)
   only the one-day rule applies.
4. **Format:** today / yesterday / weekday within 6 days / date, as above.

**What changed and where.**
- `js/mls/leagues/lastSynced.js` (new, pure, in `PRECACHE_ASSETS`): `lastSyncedSummary(leagues, now, gameTimesByTeam)`
  and its parts (`syncTimeText`, `staleReason`, `failedText`, `isSleeperLeague`), from each league's `lastSyncedAt` and
  `lastSyncFailedAt`. No new storage key.
- `js/mls/render/dashboard.js`: `renderLastSynced()` draws `#lastSyncedLine`. `js/mls/render/leagueCards.js`
  (`renderLeagueCards`) calls it beside `renderLineupNeeds`, so it redraws with every Command Center render: Sync All,
  Import All, the Draft Strategist hand-off, a single league's sync, and when the Dashboard is shown. No timer (the
  card asked for none): the age is as of the last time the Dashboard drew.
- `lineup/index.html`: the `<p id="lastSyncedLine" class="mls-last-synced" hidden>` after the two buttons.
  `css/mls.css`: `.mls-last-synced` (centered flex row that wraps; colors from `.freshness-ok` / `.freshness-stale`
  and `.sync-failed`).
- `js/mls/legend.js`: the existing "Synced 3 days ago / Last sync failed" line's text says when the Dashboard's line
  turns amber (same markup, so no new legend row). `tests/unit/badgeLegend.test.mjs`: `mls-last-synced-time` and
  `mls-last-synced-failed` in ALLOWED beside `header-freshness` and `mls-ba-sync` ("where a freshness label sits").
- `sw.js`: `CACHE_NAME` v2.10.3. CHANGELOG: a line under Lineup Strategist.

**Tests.**
- `tests/unit/lastSynced.test.mjs` (Chicago time zone, the Playwright clock): today, yesterday by the calendar, the
  weekday, last week's date and another year's; a future time reads as today; manual / hand-off leagues ignored and
  no line without a synced Sleeper league; the newest sync wins; the one-day rule; the kickoff rule (synced last night
  or before 6 AM with noon's kickoff an hour away: amber; synced 6:30 AM: not; kickoff 3.5 hours away or already
  started: the one-day rule); one, two and three failed leagues; a failed league that never synced.
- `tests/mls-last-synced.spec.mjs` (fixed clock, Chicago): no leagues and a manual-only league give no line; after a
  sync, "Last synced 11:00 AM" muted, its hover text and icon, right under the buttons; yesterday 8:15 PM (not amber);
  last week ("Sep 8", amber) with "Second League's last sync failed" in red; Sync All brings both up to date and clears
  the failure; at 375px the failed part wraps under the time, centered.

**Screenshots.** Main and this branch rendered in this container (`RENDER_ENV=any`, `npm run pxdiff`): 4 of 44 PNGs
change, re-taken by CI through the commit message:
- `desktop/mls-league-setup.png`, `phone/mls-league-setup.png`: the Dashboard gains the line under the buttons
  ("Last synced 4:00 PM": the fixed clock in CI's UTC), 24px taller.
- `desktop/mls-league-guide.png`, `phone/mls-league-guide.png`: the legend line's longer text (17px / 64px taller).

CI's update-screenshots run committed exactly those four (11edd98); I opened them: the line sits centered under the

**Checks.** Full `npm run check` (once, before the last push): check-precache OK, 626 unit tests pass, 970 Playwright

## T1 follow-up — XFP/G and FPOE/G on the In-Season tab (owner, 2026-11-12)

**Why they weren't there.** The owner asked why expected points weren't shown. They were cut in T1 because the first
sheet took XFP/G and FPOE/G from the Late-Round dump (personal use only). After the move to open data, the public sheet
computes them from nflverse's ffopportunity expected points (CC BY-SA 4.0, already credited under the table; its Data
Sources tab says so), but kept the narrowed column list. Nothing stops showing them now.

**Owner's decisions.** Both columns, right after FP/G. The owner's sheet session adds them to the public WR, RB and TE
tabs (columns F and G; Target share moves to H). The site reads columns by name, so the move changes nothing else.

**What changed.** `parseInSeasonPlayers` reads optional `XFP/G` and `FPOE/G` (FPOE/G worked out as FP/G − XFP/G when
only XFP/G is there); the In-Season table shows the two columns (header tooltips, FPOE/G signed with a real minus)
only when the position's rows have XFP/G, so the page looks as before until the sheet has them, and a copy saved before
them still draws. The Research's "Reading the In-Season Tab" gains a line for both. CHANGELOG line; CACHE_NAME v2.10.4 (v2.10.3 was taken by S46 on main).

**Tests.** The fixtures gain the two columns after FP/G with **made-up values** (FPOE/G from a fixed pattern, XFP/G =
FP/G − FPOE/G): not real data, and never Late-Round's. Specs: the columns and their values; hidden when the sheet
lacks them; FPOE/G worked out from XFP/G alone. A unit test for the optional parsing.

**Screenshots.** Compared main against the branch in the same container: desktop `tscore-inSeasonTab.png` (two more
columns) and desktop and phone `tscore-researchTab.png` (the new line); the phone In-Season PNG is unchanged (its
table scrolls sideways and the new columns sit past the first screen). Re-taken on CI.

## U1 — Research: how features are organized (the tabs are getting long)

**Output.** `docs/improvements/organization.md`: an inventory of every tab of both apps at 375px (each card's height,
how far its main action is from the bar, and what it's for), at 1280px in totals; usage (what Umami can tell, the
owner's weekly cards, three proposed Umami events); Material 3's navigation bar, rail, drawer, tabs and canonical
layouts, quoted from m3.material.io (rendered in headless Chromium, 2026-11-13); how each option fits the open layout
cards; three options with mock-ups at 375 and 1280px, and a recommendation. Mock-ups in
`docs/improvements/organization/` (22 PNGs, 256 colours): the real app at main 638d808, the fixture leagues synced and
the fixture rankings loaded, mid-Sunday, its DOM edited in Playwright (a throwaway spec, not committed). No app code,
CSS, baselines or `CACHE_NAME` change (docs only).

**Findings.**
- The length is mostly **setup and signposts, not weekly tools**: 66% of the phone Dashboard is set-once cards
  (Add/Sync League, Active League Requirements, Advanced Settings, the Ko-fi foot); two cards only say where a feature
  moved (Global Injury Auditor on Lineup since S11, Power Rankings on Scout since S29: 204px); Scout's "Sleeper Sync
  Required" banner (177px) stays after a sync until dismissed.
- Scout is two jobs (the Trade Analyzer 1.5 screens below the Waiver Wire Assistant at 375px); Lineup ends with the
  occasional Simulator 3.5 screens down. The Scoreboard is already folds (S22, S27) and is fine.
- Draft Strategist's length is all its Dashboard (4,592px at 375, all setup); S45 and S42 cover it, so no U1 card.
- Umami today: page views per page only (A1's `data-exclude-hash`), plus K2's Ko-fi and Share and K3's Shared link
  opened. Nothing per tab or card.
- Material 3: three to five destinations in the bar; tabs inside a page swipe between themselves, which collides with
  the app's swipe between destinations; on wide windows panes (M12, M13) are the answer to long pages.

**The owner's weekly use** (asked 2026-11-13): League Command Center, Roster list, Power Rankings; Optimal Lineup and
Bench, the Weekly rankings upload, Early Games; all four Scoreboard parts; Top Available, Auto-Find / Check a List and
the Trade Analyzer. Not weekly: the setup cards, the Matchup Simulator, the Trade Finder.

**Options put to the owner.** A base for all (S45's Settings page; the two signposts gone; the banner only while
nothing is synced; Add/Sync League and Requirements folded once a league exists), then: **1** tidy in place, the
occasional tools folded and jump chips on Scout and Lineup (recommended); **2** secondary tabs inside Scout (Waivers |
Trades | Market); **3** trading moves to the Roster tab (renamed Team) and Scout becomes Waivers; **4** a sixth
destination behind More (not recommended, against Material 3). Proposed cards for option 1 are at the end of
organization.md.

**Owner's decision (2026-11-13).** **Option 1: tidy in place, with jump chips**, on top of the shared base. **Yes to
all three Umami events** (Tab, Tool, Section).

**Proposed cards** (for the runbook; details at the end of organization.md):
1. **Dashboard and Scout tidy** (~10k tok), after S45, not alongside it (both edit the Dashboard): Add/Sync League and
   Active League Requirements fold to one line once a league exists (open while there is none; remembered); the Sleeper
   Sync banner shows only while no Sleeper league is synced; the Injury Auditor and Power Rankings signpost cards go.
   Re-takes the league Dashboard, Lineup and Scout PNGs; `mls-empty-setup` stays identical.
2. **Fold the occasional tools** (~10k tok): the Matchup Simulator, the Trade Finder and the SoS card fold like the
   Scoreboard's sections (one `mls_` key in keys.js). Not alongside S20 (the same Scout and Simulator code); if S20 runs
   first, the Simulator can load on first unfold.
3. **Jump chips on Scout and Lineup** (~12k tok), after 2 (a chip opens a folded target): `js/shared/ui/jumpList.js`,
   sticky, the chip in view highlighted, keyboard and Reduce Motion; shown at every width until M12, then hidden from
   1200px where the panes show the sections side by side.
4. **Umami: Tab, Tool and Section events** (~6k tok), beside anything: attributes as in organization.md section 2; the
   Guides', T-Score's and README's privacy lines updated; a spec that the attributes are there. Check the month's total
   against the free plan's 100K a week after it ships.

**Left over.** Draft Strategist gets no U1 card: S45 and S42 cover its Dashboard, M13 its Tracker. Option 3 (trading on
a Team tab) stays an idea to revisit if the `Tool` event shows the Trade Analyzer used as much as waivers.

### U1, round 2: room on phones (owner's question, 2026-11-13)

The owner asked whether "cards in a box" (two layers of padding before any content) costs room on phones, and whether
Material 3 helps. Measured at 375px on the real app: text in a card starts 21px from the edge (333px wide); a Lineup row
28px (319), a Roster row 36px (303), folded sections and toggle rows 38px (299), sub-blocks inside a card (the
Simulator's parts, the Waiver Wire's results) 55px (265). Material 3's compact margin is 16px (343). Material 3: "Don't
force content into cards when spacing, headlines, or dividers would create a simpler visual hierarchy"; "Lists should
extend edge-to-edge in compact windows"; "consider swapping cards for lists" on compact screens; 16dp margins. Two
mock-ups (CSS injected into the real app; organization.md section 7, `organization/room-*.png`): **R1** one box (cards
stay, nothing boxed inside them: rows with dividers, sub-blocks a lighter tone) and **R2** sections on phones (R1 plus,
under 600px, full-width sections with 16px margins and Draft Strategist's player cards as list rows).

**Owner's decision.** **R2, built inside M14** (surface tones and corners), which already asks for mock-ups and re-takes
all 42 PNGs, so one visual card instead of two. M14's scope grows: under 600px `.container` loses its side padding and
`.settings-card` its side borders, corners and shadow (16px padding, an 8px band between sections); inside cards at
every width, rows (`.lineup-slot`, `.roster-item`, waiver results, league cards) become list rows with dividers and
folded sections, toggle rows and sub-blocks a lighter surface tone with no border; meaningful coloured edges stay as a
3px edge; Draft Strategist's `.player-card` becomes a list row under 600px. The exports (Lineup PNG, Export Team,
Export Recap, all html2canvas from the DOM) keep their look at any width. After U1's cards 1 to 3; S32 need not wait
for it.

**Runbook (published 2026-11-13).** Cards named for the runbook: **U4** (Dashboard and Scout tidy; after S45; weeks 12–13),
**A2** (Umami Tab, Tool and Section events; any time; weeks 12–13), **U5** (fold the Simulator, Trade Finder and SoS;
after S20; January), **U6** (jump chips; after U5, before M12; January); **M14** gains R2 (title, goal, ask, scope, done;
~30k tok) and runs after U4 to U6. A new track "Organization (U1)" and a top note. The edits are in
`docs/improvements/organization/runbook-u1-edits.py`, written against runbook version 1794512485-d1fd. This session's
Artifact tool refused to publish: it needed the session to have read all 1,782 lines (512KB) of the live runbook first,
which didn't fit in this session's context. A later session (after this PR merged as #245) read the runbook in full, ran
the script on the saved copy (all six edits applied as written), and published it as version 1794578611-132a: no page
errors, 115 → 119 cards, the four new ones with prompts. It also marked U1 merged (#245), changed the top note's
"awaiting its pull request" to "merged in #245", and gave "New" tags a "Nov 13" label. A second publish
(1794580162-717c) marked F20 (#242) and S46 (#243) merged, noted the T1 follow-up (#244, no card), and added F20's
ALLOW note to M16's scope.

**Update (owner, 2026-11-13, later): R2 is now its own card, M17** ("Room on phones"), in weeks 12–13 before S32, so S32
designs phone rows at the final 343px; M14 is back to surface tones and corners (runbook version 1794583305-ec84). Exports
follow the new look, drawn the same on every device, instead of keeping today's.

## M17 — Room on phones: full-width sections, and nothing boxed inside cards

From U1's round 2 (R1 and R2, `docs/improvements/organization.md` section 7), split out of M14 so it lands before S32.

**The owner's answers** (asked before building, with the Lineup, Roster, Dashboard and Scout tabs and the Tracker at
375px and the Lineup tab at 1280px, today and after, plus Export Lineup made at 390 and 1280px; review page
https://claude.ai/artifact/Pgf3ZTf3vBwtWSCw2jZwQB):
1. **An 8px band of the page colour between sections on phones** (not a divider).
2. **Every coloured edge that carries meaning stays, as a 3px edge**: the league card's amber "needs you" and green
   active (amber wins when both, until nothing is left to do), the locked (green) and swapping (amber) Lineup rows, a
   waiver result marked Start (green), the Danger Zone (red), the Power Rankings summary's tier colour. The week tiles
   stay tiles.
3. **Exports: approved** as shown (the 390px and 1280px images identical, 480px, the framed card with flat rows).
4. **Row dividers a step brighter** than `--border` (owner, on the review page): `--divider`.

**What changed and where.**
- **css/base.css:** two variables in `:root`: `--surface-inset` (#182237, one shade lighter than the card, for nested
  blocks) and `--divider` (#2a3546, between rows); M14 folds both into its surface tokens. `.lineup-slot` and
  `.roster-item` are list rows: no border, background, radius, side padding or gap, a `--divider` line between rows,
  M2's state layer as a 3% tone on hover (computers). `.toggle-row`, `.sos-details-accordion` and
  `.settings-subsection`: `--surface-inset`, no border (the open fold's green border went too). The Danger Zone fold: a
  3px red edge instead of its dashed frame. New last section **ROOM ON PHONES** (under 600px, both apps only, through
  `.has-nav-bar`): `.container` has no side padding; `.settings-card` no border, corners or shadow, 16px side padding,
  8px apart; whatever sits in the page outside a card (banners, Setup Progress, the tier chips, the matchup strip, the
  Ko-fi foot) gets 16px margins. (Round 1 kept T-Score's folds framed; round 2 dropped that, below.)
- **css/mls.css:** locked and swapping rows: a 3px inset edge with 0.75rem of room; empty slots muted, taxi rows a dashed
  divider; `.scout-result-card` (waiver results, Market Disconnect, Trade Finder) list rows with dividers (its hover lift
  gone); `.mls-scan-card-start`'s green border is a 3px inset edge; `.lineup-needs-box`, `.mls-needs-box`,
  `.mls-week-summary-line`, `.mls-roster-power` and `.mls-power-summary-card` lighter with no frame (the summary keeps its
  3px tier edge); `.mls-power-table-wrap` loses its frame; `.mls-lc-card` list rows with dividers. `#rosterList` kept
  `.roster-empty-state`'s 8px side padding once it had rows: dropped, so a Roster row is 343px on a phone.
- **css/mds.css:** under 600px `.player-card` is a list row (16px padding, a divider, no border, corners, gap or lift).
- **Exports:** `EXPORT_WINDOW` in `js/shared/ui/exportImage.js` (`windowWidth: 1280`), spread into all four
  html2canvas calls (Export Lineup and Export Trade in `js/mls/trade/export.js`, Export Recap in
  `js/mls/week/thisWeek.js`, Export Team in `js/mds/export.js`): the clone is laid out at a computer's width, so it never
  takes the phone layout, and Export Lineup's and Export Team's `maxWidth: '100%'` became `'none'` so the 480px holds.
  Before, a phone's Export Lineup was 348px wide in the phone layout.
- **Specs:** `tests/room-on-phones.spec.mjs` (at 375px a Lineup row is at x=16, 343px wide; the Lineup card is 375px
  with no side border or corners and 16px padding; no bordered box inside any card on Dashboard, Roster, Lineup and Scout,
  nor with Check a List's results, which are 343px; no sideways scroll; the Tracker's player cards 375px wide with 16px
  padding; at 1280px every tab's first card keeps its 1px frame and 12px corners and the Lineup rows have no border).
  `tests/export-same-everywhere.spec.mjs` (Export Lineup and Export Team made at 390px and at 1280px: both 960px at
  scale 2 and pixel-identical). Run here: check-precache, `node --test` (627 pass), these two and export-flex,
  export-numerals, mls-mvp-lvp, touch-targets, focus-ring, mls-league-cards, mls-lineup-needs, mls-trade-impact,
  chevrons, scroll-shadows and smoke (146 pass). The full suite hasn't run yet (rule 7: when the pull request is asked
  for).
- `CACHE_NAME` v2.10.5. CHANGELOG lines under both apps. TESTING.md's M14 follow-up no longer lists the Scout result
  cards' lift (gone here).

**User-visible effect.** Inside a card, rows (lineup, roster, waiver and other Scout results, league cards) are plain
list rows with a divider, and folded sections, toggle rows and the "needs you" boxes a lighter shade with no border; the
coloured edges that mean something stay as a 3px edge. On a phone every section runs to the screen's edges with 16px
margins and an 8px band between sections: a Lineup row gets 343px of 375 (319 before), a Roster row 343 (303), Check a
List's results 343 (265). At 390px (the screenshots) the phone tabs are shorter: Lineup by 220px, Roster 233, Tracker
255, Draft Strategist's Guide 285, Lineup Strategist's Guide 562. Tablets and computers keep their cards (the Lineup

### M17, round 2: the Draft Board runs to the edges again, and T-Score's folds match (owner, 2026-11-13)

The owner asked why T-Score got an exception and whether the Board had an edge-to-edge rule.
- **The Board was a regression of round 1.** css/mds.css's phone bleed (`.draft-board-container`, -0.5rem margins
  matched to `.container`'s old 8px) ran the grid to the screen's edges; ROOM ON PHONES's 16px margins for what isn't a
  card overrode it, so the grid sat inside 16px margins. Now the margin rule leaves the board out, and under 600px
  (css/mds.css, last rule) the board is the screen's full width with 16px of inner padding: it starts at the margin
  and scrolls to the edge. The bleed's -0.5rem would have scrolled the page sideways now that `.container` has no side
  padding. `room-on-phones.spec.mjs` checks it (x 0, 375px wide, 16px padding, the grid scrolls, the page doesn't).
- **T-Score matches (owner's choice).** Round 1 kept T-Score's framed folds with a T-Score-only rule; the owner chose
  one look across the site, so that rule is gone and T-Score's folds are the lighter shade with no border like the two
  apps'. T-Score keeps its 8px phone margins (ROOM ON PHONES needs the nav bar; not asked).
- **Screenshots** (re-taken by CI, below): phone `mds-draft-board` and `mds-empty-board` (the grid from 16px to the
  screen's edge); desktop and phone `tscore-inSeasonTab`, `tscore-researchTab` and `tscore-top50Tab` (2px shorter,
  folds lighter without a border).
- **A timing race, not a change:** CI's first try (run 43489877214) refused because desktop `mls-league-setup.png`
  changed too. After Sync, the button shows "Synced Successfully" (green) for 2.5s (`flashButton`), and `visual.spec`'s
  first MLS shot usually lands inside that. Main's baseline, all four renders in this container and that run show it
