### M17, round 4: Setup Progress is a section on phones; banners and the matchup strip stay framed (owner, 2026-11-13)

From the round 3 mock-up. The owner liked Setup Progress as a section, and asked whether Material 3 covers banners and
whether the strip stays clearly tappable without its frame. Checked on m3.material.io (rendered in Chromium): Material 3
has **no banner component** (its component list has Snackbars, Cards, Lists…; `/components/banners` is a 404; banners
were Material 2's). Snackbars are short updates at the bottom of the screen; a message that stays until you act is a
card in the content. A single-action surface reads as one by its container (a card's "primary action area"); a list
item's trailing icon "can communicate an action" only among other list items. So:
- **Built:** under 600px `.setup-checklist` has no frame, 16px padding and the 8px band (css/base.css, ROOM ON PHONES),
  like a section. It's content you work through, and its text gains about 40px.
- **Kept framed at 16px margins (owner's choice, agreeing):** the guide banner, Scout's Sleeper Sync banner, Draft
  Strategist's banners and the matchup strip. They come and go, and the frame says so; the strip's frame is what makes it
  read as tappable. 16px over 8px: the frame's edge lines up with every section's text (Material 3's compact margin).
- **Screenshots** (local render against round 3's): phone `mds-empty-setup`, `mls-empty-setup`, `mls-league-lineup`,
  `mls-league-roster`, `mls-league-setup`. Specs: room-on-phones, mds-setup, touch-targets pass.
- **Re-taken on CI** (commit 1843a0b, "Screenshots re-taken on CI (5 PNGs)"): exactly those 5.
- **Main moved (S36, #248) and was merged in.** `CACHE_NAME` v2.10.6, above main's v2.10.5. S36 and this card both
  changed three baselines (desktop and phone `mls-league-guide`, phone `mls-league-scoreboard`): the merge takes main's,
  then CI re-takes those three with both changes (main and the merged branch rendered here: only those three differ from
  main among S36's four; desktop `mls-league-scoreboard` matches main; every other PNG matches this branch before the
  merge). The merge first committed conflict markers in CHANGELOG and LOG (both sides kept since). Specs after the merge:
  room-on-phones, export-same-everywhere, mls-final-window, mls-mvp-lvp, mls-this-week, mls-root-for, touch-targets,
  badge-legend (126 pass); check-precache; `node --test` (639 pass).
- **Re-taken on CI** (commit f6b40f0, "Screenshots re-taken on CI (3 PNGs)"): exactly those 3; opened (the Scoreboard
  with S36's changes, running to the screen's edges).

## S36 — The Final Window: what rides on the last game of the week

**User-visible effect.** On the Scoreboard, when every player still to play in your head-to-head matchups is in the
week's last night window (Sunday night, or Monday's one or two games), a new section, **Final Window** (a moon icon),
sits above League Scoreboard and folds like the halves (remembered):
- **Its title line:** the games and kickoff, and how many matchups ride on them ("MIN @ CHI, KC @ DEN · Mon 7:15 PM ·
  3 matchups ride on them").
- **Four tiles** (two across on phones, four from 900px): **At stake** ("2 W · 3 live matchups"), **Expected** ("0.3 W ·
  middle 80%: 0–1 W"), **Best case** ("2 W · 3% of runs") and **Clean sweep** ("3% · all 2 at stake"). While the
  simulation runs they read "working it out"; if it fails, "couldn't run" and a line to tap Refresh.
- **What each matchup needs:** one card per live matchup (one column on phones, two from 900px), most uncertain first,
  settled ones last and dimmed: the league ($ if paid), the margin, "You 100.0 · Rival 102.0", a green/red win-chance
  bar with "26% to win", one line in words ("Down 2.0. **Justin Jefferson** needs 2.0+ more than **Harrison Butker**.",
  "Up 5.2. Hold **James Cook III** + **Jordan Mason** under 5.2 more.", "…: even a zero from X isn't enough",
  "…: anything from X does it"), who's live on each side with position badges, and, when exactly two players decide it
  (and it isn't settled), a table: "If A scores" Quiet: 0.0 / Projection: 8.3 / Big night: 10.0, against "B must score"
  "under 6.3", "2.5+", "anything" or "even a zero isn't enough".
- **Notes** under it: Best Ball leagues and leagues with no opponent are named, not counted; one line on how the runs work.
- A muted credit line under the Scoreboard card ("The Final Window was inspired by FF Historian, ffhistorian.com"),
  under Sleepy's, and the same credit at the end of the Guide's new Final Window item. The badge legend gains the
  win-chance bar.
- Nothing shows before the last window (Sunday afternoon, say), in a past week, or with no live head-to-head matchup.

**Owner's decisions (2026-11-13, asked before building, with sketches at 375 and 1280px).**
1. Name: **Final Window** (my recommendation; it fits Sunday night and two Monday games). Credit: **FF Historian,
   https://ffhistorian.com/**, in the Guide and under the Scoreboard, as S27 credits Sleepy.
2. When: **the last window, Sunday night too**: every live player's game in one night window (same day, US Eastern, at
   5 PM or later), so SNF counts when nobody you care about plays Monday.
3. Math: **one shared simulation** across every live matchup (players shared by matchups move together), with the four
   tiles as sketched.
4. The two-player table **in points**; stat lines stay with S37.

**What changed, and where.**
- `js/mls/week/lastWindow.js` (new, pure): `finalWindow` (the window from live teams, kickoffs and game states),
  `simulateWindow` (10,000 runs; one standard normal per player per run, shared by every league he's in, each league
  scaling it by its own mean and spread of his points still to come, floored at 0; a QB and his own team's WR/TE share
  part of it, the Simulator worker's 0.35, here across leagues), `certainResult`, `shareText`, `needsLine` (names
  escaped), `twoPlayerTable`. "At stake" leaves out matchups that are certain (only one side can still score and it's
  that side's) or settled in the runs (under 0.5% or over 99.5%, the Scoreboard's own rule); the other tiles count the
  matchups at stake.
- `js/mls/week/lastWindowWorker.js` (new): a module worker running `simulateWindow`. Measured: 10,000 runs × 15
  matchups take ~34 ms in Node and 75–100 ms in Chromium with the CPU slowed 4x, so it runs off the main thread (on the
  page as a fallback if the worker can't start). The card suggested the Simulator's worker; that one is a classic
  script, and a module worker importing `lastWindow.js` keeps the math in one place.
- `js/mls/sim/winChance.js`: the history fetch is shared (`historiesFor`), and `remainingProfiles` gives each live
  player's points still to come in each league's scoring (`getPlayerVarianceProfile`, less what he has; mid-game, the
  Simulator's shareLeft rule). Unlike the Simulator, a player with no history still counts (projection with the
  fallback spread), so a matchup is never dropped. `matchupWinChances` is unchanged in behavior.
- `js/mls/week/thisWeek.js`: `finalModel`, `addFinalWindow` (queued after the board's chances, only while the
  Scoreboard is open, rerun when scores, live players or game states change; Refresh reruns it), `finalHTML`; the
  `final` fold in the `mls_scoreboard_folds` key (comment updated in `keys.js`; no new key).
- `js/mls/badges.js` `finalChanceMarkup`, `js/mls/legend.js` (one line), `css/mls.css` (the `mls-fw-*` rules),
  `lineup/index.html` (the Guide item and the credit line), `CHANGELOG.md`, `sw.js` (the two files in PRECACHE_ASSETS,
  CACHE_NAME v2.10.4 → v2.10.5).

**Tests.** `tests/unit/lastWindow.test.mjs` (9): the window (two Monday games as one, SNF alone, an afternoon game
left, unknown kickoff, a final game), a sure win and a sure loss, a settled blowout, a shared player on both sides of
different matchups (every run 1 or 2 wins, never a sweep), two leagues' scoring moving together, the sweep and middle
80% over six coin flips, `shareText`, the needs lines (escaping), the two-player table (two yours, two theirs, one
each, none with one or three). `tests/mls-final-window.spec.mjs` (both widths): Monday night with MIN @ CHI and KC @
DEN left (S27's three fixture leagues, Butker's points set to 0): the section above League Scoreboard, its title, the
tiles, three matchups with their lines, the table, the settled one last without a table, the fold remembered, the
credit, nothing past the card; the worker blocked (the page runs it); Sunday afternoon, no section.
`mls-root-for.spec.mjs`: its credit check picks Sleepy's line by text (there are two now).

**Screenshots.** Rendered main and this branch in this container and compared them (`npm run pxdiff`): four change, by
design; the other 40 match main. The screenshot league isn't in a final window, so the section itself isn't in any.
- `desktop/mls-league-scoreboard.png`, `phone/mls-league-scoreboard.png`: the Final Window's credit line under Sleepy's.
- `desktop/mls-league-guide.png`, `phone/mls-league-guide.png`: the Guide's Final Window item and the legend's
  win-chance bar line.
CI re-takes these four (`update-screenshots.yml`, named in this round's commit message).

**Checks run.** check-precache OK; `node --test`: all pass but `tscoreInSeason.test.mjs`, which needs `npm ci` in tests/
(papaparse), as on main; Playwright: `mls-final-window`, `mls-root-for`, `mls-root-kickoff-find`, `mls-this-week`,
`badge-legend`, `touch-targets`, `tooltips-onscreen` pass (both widths). The full suite hasn't run yet: per rule 7 it
runs once, when the pull request is asked for.

**Left over.**
- In-game players use the Simulator's model (points so far plus the rest of his usual game); real live stats are S37.
- Best Ball is left out with a note, as in the Simulator (the open follow-up on Best Ball there applies).
- S37 (stat lines) builds on this: `remainingProfiles` and `finalHTML`'s matchup cards are where its lines would go.
- S20: `thisWeek.js` now imports `remainingProfiles` from `sim/winChance.js` too; load it through S20's loader with
  `matchupWinChances`.
- The window's "night" is 5 PM Eastern or later; a late-season Saturday afternoon game as the last window wouldn't count.

### S36, round 2: my review as a user, and the owner's "do all six"

After looking at the section on the real Scoreboard at both widths, I listed what felt clunky; the owner asked for all six:
1. **No repeat under League Scoreboard.** While the Final Window is open and shows every matchup Still in play would
   list, Still in play is one line instead: "Still in play: 2, in the Final Window above". Fold the Final Window and the
   list is back (the fold redraws the card); with a swing it doesn't cover, a Best Ball one say, the list shows as before.
2. **No twin tiles.** When the best case is a sweep, the third tile is **Most likely** ("0 W · 73% of runs", the most
   common number of wins) instead of a Best case that repeated Clean sweep. `simulateWindow` returns `likely` and
   `likelyShare`.
3. **The table reads more easily:** "you lose either way" instead of "even a zero isn't enough" in the "must score"
   column, and last names in its headers ("If Jefferson scores | Butker must score"; `shortName`). The sure-loss line
   above the cards keeps "even a zero … isn't enough", as the card asked.
4. **Settled matchups share one line** under the cards ("Settled: Second Fixture League (W, +42.2).") instead of a card
   each with a line like "Butker needs 42.2+ more than Jefferson".
5. **No You/Them line.** The position badge sits beside each name in the sentence (`needsLine` takes a `nameHTML`).
6. **Plainer words:** "usually 0–1 W" instead of "middle 80%: 0–1 W"; the "each of 10,000 runs…" paragraph moved into
   an (i) beside "What each matchup needs". The Guide says "the usual range (the middle 80% of outcomes)".
The kickoff in my screenshots read "Tue 12:15 AM" only because the test browser runs on UTC; users see their own time.

Tests: two unit tests (`needsLine`'s `nameHTML`, `shortName`) and `likely`. `mls-this-week.spec.mjs`: its mid-Sunday
fixture (only Jefferson left, on Sunday night) is now a final window, by the owner's rule, so its tests start with the
Final Window folded (`mls_scoreboard_folds` = { final: false }) to keep testing Still in play's own list. The spec now checks two cards and the
settled line, the badges in the sentence, the Most likely tile, "usually", the short headers, the (i) (opened, on screen) and
Still in play's pointer (and the list back once the window is folded). Ran: check-precache, `node --test` (lastWindow,
badgeLegend, noEmoji), and `mls-final-window`, `mls-this-week`, `mls-root-for`, `mls-root-kickoff-find`,
`tooltips-onscreen`, `touch-targets` at both widths. Screenshots (this round's render against round 1's, same container: only these two differ): the Guide's text changed again, so `desktop/mls-league-guide.png` and
`phone/mls-league-guide.png` are re-taken on CI again; the Scoreboard PNGs CI took last round (opened: only the credit
line under Sleepy's is new) don't change this round, since the screenshot league isn't in a final window.

1. **One count for one idea.** The title line counts what's at stake once the runs are in, and the settled ones apart

**Follow-up for this session, before the pull request (owner, round 3; done in round 5, below):** "3% to win" is green on a losing, red-edged
card, which reads like good news at a glance. The League Scoreboard's tiles and Still in play use the same green, so
the change is to color the percent by who's favored (red under 50%) in all three places at once: the Final Window,
the league tiles and Still in play. Not done yet; the owner asked to keep it for this session before the PR.

### S36, round 5: the win chance's color (round 3's follow-up; owner's choice B, in all four places)

**The issue, as I put it to the owner.** Every chance to win was bold green whatever its number (S21 picked green to
make the percent stand out, not to mean anything). Green means good everywhere else in the app, so a losing matchup
could show a red margin, a red edge and a red L chip beside a green "3% to win". Options: A leave it; B green at 50% or
more and red under; C the plain text color, leaving the margin, chip, edge and bar to say ahead or behind (my
recommendation); D the Final Window only. **The owner chose B, in all four places, as part of S36.**

**Outside this card.** The rule was shared, so this changes three S21 places as well as the Final Window: the
Scoreboard's league tiles (`.mls-week-tile-chance`), Still in play (`.mls-week-chance` in `swingHTML`) and the Lineup
tab's matchup strip (`renderMatchupStrip`). The card's "don't touch" list has the Scoreboard's tiles: their rules are
unchanged; only the percent's color, at the owner's request. Who to root for's league detail ("26% to win" in plain
text) was never colored and isn't now.

**What changed.** `chanceTone` in `thisWeek.js` (`is-fav` at 50% or more, `is-dog` under) on the three S21 spans;
`finalChanceMarkup` (badges.js) the same; `css/mls.css` one rule (`is-dog`: #ef4444, the margin's red when behind).
The badge legend's Final Window line shows a green and a red sample and says the rule holds for every chance in the
app. CHANGELOG line under Lineup Strategist.

**Tests.** `mls-final-window.spec.mjs` checks every percent's class and color against its own number in the Final
Window (including six at stake with favored and not side by side), on the league tiles, in Still in play (with the
window folded) and in the Lineup tab's strip. `mls-this-week` and `badge-legend` still pass.

### S36, round 6: a fourth pass as a user (owner: do 2a; explain 1 and 3 first)

- **2a, built:** each card's two-player table sits behind a **"What if…"** toggle (chevron, `aria-expanded`), open on
  the first card and folded on the rest, so six cards that hinge on the same two players don't show six tables. A tap
  opens or closes it in place (`toggleFinalWhatIf`, main.js), remembered by league for the visit in memory (a Refresh
  keeps it). Spec: open on the first, folded on the second, a tap opens it with focus kept, closing the first, both
  surviving a Refresh.
- **1, asked about, not built yet:** the Final Window said 27% to win where the same league's tile said 25%. Measured
  over six runs: both numbers wobble between 25% and 27% from run to run, so it isn't two different models but the
  same one (the Simulator's per-player model, `getPlayerVarianceProfile`) rolled twice, 10,000 runs each, about ±1
  point of noise. Two real differences could make the gap bigger in rare cases: the Final Window counts a player with
  no game history (at his projection; S21's run leaves him out), and it links a QB and his own team's WR/TE across
  leagues (S21's only within one roster). Proposed fix: while the Final Window has its numbers, the tiles, Still in
  play and the Lineup tab's strip show its chance for those leagues. Waiting on the owner.
- **3, asked about, mock-up sent:** with linked matchups, Expected 2.0, "usually 0–6" and "Most likely 0 W" are hard
  to square, because the outcomes pile up at both ends. Mock-up (375 and 1280px): keep At stake and Expected, and swap
  Most likely and Clean sweep for one row of bars, one per number of wins (0 to 6), the last bar being the sweep; with
  six unrelated matchups for contrast (a hump in the middle). Waiting on the owner.

### S36, round 7: one chance per matchup, and the bars (owner: "do the fix for 1, and build the bars for 3")

- **One number per matchup.** While the Final Window has its numbers, `chanceOf` (thisWeek.js) returns its chance for
  those leagues (`finalChanceOf`), so the league tiles, Still in play, the Lineup tab's strip, the "effectively decided"
  rule and the root-for weights all read the same figure. New scores clear the Final Window's numbers (they wait for its
  next run, and meanwhile S21's chances show, as before); outside the window nothing changes. Best Ball leagues aren't in
  its run, so they keep none, as before.
- **The bars.** The tiles are now **At stake** and **Expected** ("1.6 W · on average"), then **"How many of the 6 you
  win"**: one bar per number of wins among the matchups at stake (0 up to a clean sweep), its height the share of runs
  that ended that way (tallest 72px), labeled with the percent ("<1%" for a sliver; past 10 at stake only bars of 5%
  or more are labeled). A text alternative names every bar (`role="img"`, aria-label "0 W 37%, 1 W 24%, …"). On phones
  the tiles sit two across over the bars; from 900px the two tiles stack at the left of the bars. Most likely, Best case,
  Clean sweep and "usually" are gone (the bars show all of them). `simulateWindow` returns `dist`; the bars use the
  site's blue (`--btn-blue-bg`). No legend line: a chart, not a badge.
- Tests: a unit test for `dist` (four coin flips 1-4-6-4-1, the sure win left out, a linked trio all-or-nothing); the
  spec checks the two tiles, the bars' heading, count, labels, aria-label, that they add up to 100 and that the
  tallest is the most common, seven bars with six at stake, and that the tile, Still in play and the Lineup tab's strip
  show the Final Window's own percent. That test found a double rounding (26.49% read "26%" on the card and "27%" on
  the tile, rounded to 26.5 first): `finalChanceOf` now passes the share unrounded. In the live specs, which card comes
  first depends on the runs, so they open "What if…" when it's folded (`openWhatIf`). Ran `mls-final-window` three
  times over (54 passed), `mls-this-week`, `mls-root-for`, `mls-root-kickoff-find`, `tooltips-onscreen`, unit tests,
  check-precache. Screenshots: only the two Guide PNGs change (its text); re-taken on CI.

### S36, round 8: the whole Scoreboard on a Monday night (owner: "do A and B, note C and D")

My review as a user of the whole tab (six matchups riding on the last games, seven leagues): fine on desktop (about two

## F18 — Scoreboard: count Sleeper's auto-subs

**How Sleeper reports auto-subs (checked 2026-11-12).**
- **The setting** is `settings.max_subs` on each league (`GET /v1/league/<id>`): 0 = off, 1–3 = AutoSubs allowed per team
  per week. Two related keys: `sub_start_time_eligibility` (1 = "Require AutoSub To Not Play Before Starter": the sub's
  game must start at the same time as the starter's or later) and `sub_lock_if_starter_active`. In the owner's 21 leagues
  (2026): 17 have `max_subs` 1–3 (LOOG - Chop Shop also has `sub_start_time_eligibility` 1), four have 0 (LRD Cucks,
  2026 Late Round Charity League, Legacy Best Ball, Slow Mock Draft); `sub_lock_if_starter_active` is 0
  everywhere.
- **What it is** ([Sleeper help: How does Player AutoSubs work?](https://support.sleeper.com/en/articles/9731991-how-does-player-autosubs-work),
  read in full on 2026-11-13): "If a starter is inactive, the assigned sub will automatically move into the lineup." The
  commissioner turns it on in General League Settings → Player Auto-Subs and picks how many each team can use per week;
  managers set a sub per starter on the Swap Player screen (mobile app only; shown on web). Both games must not have
  started, and the sub must fit the starter's slot. "As soon as one player in the AutoSub pairing kicks off, both players
  lock." One sub per starter; no injury tag needed. Subs show in Sleeper's team and matchup views (yours and your
  opponent's). The page doesn't say when the swap is written. Sleeper added the start-time option later
  ([SleeperHQ on X](https://x.com/SleeperHQ/status/1824543398432223396)). The Footballguys thread sits behind a
  Cloudflare challenge, so it couldn't be read.
- **The public API** ([docs.sleeper.com](https://docs.sleeper.com/)) has no auto-sub endpoint or field. A matchup entry
  has exactly `roster_id`, `matchup_id`, `points`, `custom_points`, `starters`, `starters_points`, `players`,
  `players_points` (every entry of the owner's 21 leagues, weeks 1–10). Rosters carry no sub assignments either (their
  keys: `starters`, `players`, `reserve`, `taxi`, `settings`, `metadata` with notification switches, `player_map` null).
  So nothing names a swap, and nothing says which bench player is a starter's sub before it happens.
- **`points` is always the sum of `starters_points`**, in the same order as `starters`: 2,196 of 2,196 finished entries
  (weeks 1–9, all 21 leagues, Best Ball included). Sleeper's app counts the sub in the team score, so once Sleeper makes the
  swap, `starters` must already list the sub and `points` include him; the starter he replaced is a bench player.
  Not yet confirmed on a known swap (asked the owner for one).
- **Sleeper's own weekly recap:** not in the public API (confirmed on docs.sleeper.com: users, avatars, leagues,
  rosters, users in a league, matchups, playoff brackets, transactions, traded picks, NFL state, drafts, picks, players,
  trending). The runbook's answer stands.

**What the app does today.** Every Scoreboard view reads Sleeper's `starters` and the tile's score is Sleeper's `points`
(`custom_points` when a commissioner set it), fetched again when older than 2 minutes or on Refresh. No view adds up
starters itself, except Best Ball by design (`bestBallLineup`). So after Sleeper's swap the tiles, record, Still in play,
Root for / Against you and MVPs / LVPs / Started already count the sub. Before the swap (starter ruled out, his game not
started) the starter is left out as Out and the sub is a bench player.

**The owner's check (2026-11-13).** The mismatch the owner saw is gone when he looks again, so Sleeper's matchups catch
up with the swap after a delay. Where a delay can come from:
- Sleeper writing the swap (not documented; the starter has to be inactive, which is only certain at his kickoff).
- Sleeper's CDN: `GET /v1/league/<id>/matchups/<week>` answers with `cache-control: public, s-maxage=60,
  stale-while-revalidate=300`, so a response can be up to about 6 minutes old.
- The app: the Scoreboard re-fetches matchups older than 2 minutes (`FRESH_MS`, thisWeek.js; Refresh forces it). Nothing
  stores matchups across a reload (`State.lineupActualPoints` is in memory), and the service worker doesn't touch
  cross-origin requests.

**Owner's decisions (2026-11-13).** After the research the owner checked again and no longer saw the mismatch, so it was
Sleeper's matchups catching up with the swap, not the app. Of the options offered: **1, a regression test and one shared
rule, no visible change**. Not done:
- The "auto-sub" mark on the sub's row (asked in the card): the API never names a swap, so the app could only mark one it
  had seen happen between two of its own fetches. Dropped.
- Counting the sub as live before the swap (asked in the card): the API doesn't say who anyone's sub is. Dropped.
- Timing Sleeper's swap on a Sunday by fetching the owner's leagues around kickoff (research only): not now; auto-subs
  aren't that common and nothing would be built on it yet.
- A "Sleeper may auto-sub" note on an Out starter: not chosen.

**What changed, and where.** No user-visible change, so no CHANGELOG line, no legend line, no screenshots.
- `js/mls/week/playerRecap.js`: `starterIdsOf` (who counts as a team's starters: Sleeper's `starters`, or Best Ball's
  lineup) moved here from rootFor.js, beside `bestBallLineup`, with a comment on how Sleeper reports auto-subs. The MVPs &
  LVPs (Started, started zeros, LVPs) now use it instead of their own copy of the rule; the same result.
  `js/mls/week/rootFor.js` re-exports it. The card asked for the function beside `summarize` in scoreboard.js; it already
  existed in rootFor.js, and playerRecap.js is the place that avoids a circular import (rootFor.js imports
  `bestBallLineup` from it).
- `js/mls/week/thisWeek.js`: the tiles' and Still in play's sides read their starters with `starterIdsOf` (its own
  `starterIds` copy removed). S36's Final Window is built from those same sides, so it follows too.
- Totals: every tile shows Sleeper's `points` (`custom_points` when a commissioner set one). No view adds up starters
  itself, except Best Ball by design (its lineup is picked after the games).
- `sw.js`: `CACHE_NAME` v2.10.5 → v2.10.6 (above main's after merging S36); no file added.

**Tests.**
- `tests/fixtures/sleeper/make-fixtures.mjs`: `league-autosub.json` (Fixture League with `max_subs` 1),
  `league-matchups-autosub.json` (CeeDee Lamb inactive, Sleeper has put Amon-Ra St. Brown in his FLEX slot: 106.0–110.0)
  and `league2-matchups-inactive.json` (Second Fixture League, no auto-subs: Lamb started on 0.0, St. Brown's 24.0 on the
  bench: 105.4–95.2).
- `tests/unit/playerRecap.test.mjs`: the sub counts as started (an MVP, Started 50% (1)); the starter he replaced
  started only where there was no sub (LVP, a started zero in one league).
- `tests/mls-auto-sub.spec.mjs` (new, both widths; the Final Window starts folded, as in mls-mvp-lvp.spec.mjs, since
  only Sunday night is left): the tiles are Sleeper's scores; St. Brown is in your finished
  starters and Lamb in one league only; MVPs (St. Brown, Started 50% (1)), LVPs (Lamb, 50% (1)), Started zeros and Benched
  booms (St. Brown named only in the league without auto-subs). And with no swap, the same lineups read as before (98.0 and
  130.4; Lamb in 2, St. Brown on both benches).

**Left over.** Nothing for the app. If Sleeper ever adds an auto-sub field to the public API, the mark and the live
anticipation become possible.

**Checked.** check-precache, `node --test` (640 pass), and after merging main (S36): `mls-auto-sub`, `mls-final-window`,
`mls-mvp-lvp`, `mls-root-for` (and before the merge `mls-this-week`, `mls-root-kickoff-find`), all passing at both widths.
The full suite hasn't run: it runs once, when the owner asks for the pull request.

tests pass (48 skipped). Five failures: the four `visual.spec.mjs` cloud-fonts guards (MDS and MLS, both widths), which fail

## S34 — Waiver claim or free agent: the Dashboard's upgrades say which, and when

**User-visible effect.** In Sleeper leagues, each player on a league card's **Best available**, on **Auto-Find**'s cards
and on **Check a List**'s free agents now carries a small chip after his rank: **FA** (grey outline: a free agent, add
him now, until his game starts) or **W** (amber: on waivers). A tap (or hover) says why and when, in the viewer's time
zone: "Waivers (FAAB) · runs Wed 12 AM: his game has started, so Sleeper locks him until the weekly run.", "Waivers
(FAAB) · clears about Sat 5 PM: dropped lately, …", "Free agent: add him now on Sleeper until his game starts (Sun 10
AM), no claim needed." On **Weekly**, an upgrade who's on waivers and plays before the claim runs is greyed with "too
late for this week", isn't counted in the card's "1 upgrade" (so the card can fold), and the position's next free agent
gets the upgrade check instead; other waiver players whose game is before the run are greyed too. On ROS he's marked W
but still counts (it's about the rest of the season). Manual leagues show no chip. A league synced before this card
has no waiver settings, so no chips until its next sync. The Guide's badge legend has two new lines.

**Owner's decisions (asked before building, with 375px sketches):**
1. **A chip on both** ("FA" and "W"), not my suggestion of W only.
2. **Muted and not counted** ("too late for this week"), my suggestion; and the next free agent at his position is
   checked instead.
3. **Auto-Find and Check a List** get the chip; **not Top Available** (nor the All Leagues search).

### Sleeper's rules, as the app uses them (sources)

Sources: Sleeper's help pages "After Game Waivers / Custom Daily Waivers" (support.sleeper.com/en/articles/3242468),
"Waivers for regular season & playoffs" (…/3978868) and "Why was my waiver time pushed back" (…/4042901), read through
a web search's summary (support.sleeper.com isn't reachable from this cloud session); and the owner's 21 leagues'
settings and their transactions for weeks 7–10 of 2026 (`api.sleeper.app/v1/league/<id>/transactions/<week>`, 10
leagues, ~600 moves), with kickoffs from ESPN's scoreboard.
- **`waiver_type`:** 0 rolling waivers, 1 reverse standings, 2 FAAB (only the FAAB leagues' claims carry a
  `waiver_bid`). The type doesn't change who's on waivers; the chip's tap text names it.
- **Game start:** a player is locked on waivers once his game starts, until the weekly run ("After Game Waivers":
  Sleeper's help says a Thursday player is "locked and on waivers until Tue After Day (or 12:05 a.m. PST on
  Wednesday)"). In the transactions, all 178 free-agent adds of a player with a game that week came before his
  kickoff (one apparent exception was a player who has since changed teams); Sunday adds stop at 10 AM PT, Tuesdays
  have only drops, and Wednesday has the big batch of claims.
- **The weekly run:** the day after `waiver_day_of_week` (2 = Tuesday, Sunday = 0, so Wednesday) at
  `daily_waivers_hour`, **Pacific time**: claims land at Wed 12:07–12:15 AM PT in the hour-0 leagues (daily or not),
  and at Wed 9:02 AM PT in REDRAFT Late Round Board of Directors (hour 9) and 9:05 in Legends of the Pocket Dynasty
  (hour 9). The app computes it in America/Los_Angeles and shows it in the viewer's zone.
- **Dropped players:** on waivers for `waiver_clear_days` days, each on his own clock: claims landed 47 h after the
  drop in 2-day leagues (Penix Wed 6:24 PM → Fri 5:31 PM; Dobbins Thu 5:08 PM → Sat 4:12 PM; Hurst Thu 10:15 AM → Sat
  9:22 AM) and 23 h in 1-day ones (Gainwell Wed 12:15 AM → Wed 11:15 PM). The app uses drop + N days and says "about".
  One exception: in REDRAFT a Tuesday drop cleared at Thursday's 9:02 AM daily run (49 h), so on some days a daily
  league waits for its run hour; that's the next item.
- **`daily_waivers` / `daily_waivers_days`:** daily processing on the days in a 2-bits-a-day mask (5461 = every day
  "1"; 1360, 6140, 10580, 10581, 10583 in other leagues). Sleeper's per-day options are "FA", "Locked" and "Waivers →
  FA". I couldn't decode the mask with confidence from the data (REDRAFT's 1360 matches Sunday-first days shifted by
  one, other leagues don't), so the app **doesn't use it**: it's stored, and only the game-start lock and the drop
  clock decide. Free-agent adds happen in daily leagues all week, so most unrostered players are free agents, as the
  card said.

### What changed, and where
- `js/mls/scout/waiverStatus.js` (new, pure): `waiverSettingsFrom(settings)`, `nextWeeklyRun(waivers, after)`,
  `waiverStatus(player, waivers, drops, kickoffs, now)` → `{ status: 'free' | 'waivers', clearsAt, tooLateThisWeek,
  reason: 'dropped' | 'played', atRun, kickoff, claimType }` (null when the app can't tell), `waiverStatusText`.
  Nothing is "too late" once the week's last game is over (about 4 hours after the last kickoff): the Wednesday run
  is before next week's games.
- `js/mls/scout/recentDrops.js` (new): each Sleeper league's drops from this week's and last week's transactions
  (S29's `getSleeperTransactions`), 4 leagues at a time, cached for the visit and re-read after a league syncs. On
  an error that league gets null: no chips, no toast (a console warning).
- `js/mls/leagues/sync.js`: `league.waivers` from Sleeper's settings, kept from the last sync if a response lacks
  them. It's on the league object, so Backup / Restore carries it (no new storage key).
- `js/mls/scout/bestAvailable.js`: the upgrade check per position walks past waiver players who are too late (Weekly
  only), lists them muted after the real upgrades, and draws the chip on every player. Drops load with the player map,
  projections and kickoffs; kickoffs count only when they're this week's.
- `js/mls/scout/waivers.js`: `waiverChipHTML` (the one helper), and `buildWaiverContext` gives `waiverStatusOf` to
  Auto-Find's cards; `js/mls/scout/engine.js` adds it to Check a List's free agents in a synced league.
- `js/mls/badges.js` `waiverChipMarkup`; `js/mls/main.js` `explainWaiver` (the tip as a toast, like SoS);
  `css/mls.css`; `js/mls/legend.js` two lines; `sw.js` (two new files, `CACHE_NAME` v2.10.8: F18 took v2.10.6 and M17 v2.10.7).

### Tests
- `tests/unit/waiverStatus.test.mjs` (17): settings mapping, the weekly run (12 AM and 9 AM, before and after the
  hour, across the end of daylight saving time), dropped yesterday in a 2-day league, a 1-day drop that has cleared, a
  Saturday drop locked at a Sunday kickoff, game started, the Monday-night player on Sunday, daily waivers at 9 before
  and after the run hour, Tuesday with every game over, FAAB vs rolling, a bye-week player, the tap texts.
- `tests/mls-waiver-status.spec.mjs` (5, both widths): nobody dropped (every chip FA, "1 upgrade", the tap toast, the
  settings stored on the league); game day with `league-transactions-drop.json` (Cook dropped Saturday, kickoffs set:
  Cook muted, "too late for this week", W, not counted; Chase Brown, whose game had started, W and muted; Auto-Find and
  Check a List chips); the same drop on ROS (W, still counted); transactions failing (no chips, the upgrade counts, no
  toast about it); a league synced before S34 (no chips). Fixture league now has waiver settings
  (`tests/fixtures/sleeper/make-fixtures.mjs`).
- Existing specs changed by the new chip and fetch: `mls-waiver-flex-unranked` (its pinned card texts now have "FA"
  after the team) and `mls-past-trade` (the Dashboard now reads this week's and last week's transactions once per
  visit, so the Roster tab's requests are counted from after it; the manual-league test counts from after the league
  turns manual). `buildWaiverContext` only reads drops for a synced league.

     carries the week (`leg`) it was filed in. In the owner's 10 leagues (weeks 7–10, ~900 moves): week 7 ran from

     from Wed 11/04 12:20 AM PT to Wed 11/11 12:11 AM PT, week 10 from Wed 11/11 12:11 AM PT. Sleeper doesn't document
     the state endpoint's rollover (a web search found only "use display_week"); `state/nfl` read week 10 on Fri 11/13.
     So on Tuesday the app's week is the finished one, and its kickoffs are all in the past.
   - Two scheduled checks of `state/nfl` (Tue 11/17 8:30 PM PT and Wed 11/18 1 AM PT) confirm the endpoint flips with
     `leg`; their readings go here.

### Round 7: each league's daily waiver schedule (from the owner's Sleeper settings screens)

The owner sent the General Settings screens of LRD Cucks, Fantasy Leftovers and Legends of the Pocket. Against their
`daily_waivers_days` they decode exactly: two bits a day, read from the low end **Sunday, Saturday, Friday, Thursday,
Wednesday, Tuesday, Monday** (not Sunday-first, as round 6 assumed), with

| Code | Sleeper's label | Seen in |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | Free Agency | LP Sun, LRD Sun |
| 1 | Waivers (Processing Time = `daily_waivers_hour`) | LP and FL Wed–Sat, LRD Mon–Tue |
| 2 | Locked | LP and FL Mon–Tue |
| 3 | Waivers until the hour, then Free Agents | FL Sun, LRD Wed–Sat |

Every other league's transactions agree in that order: Friday Night Beverages, Gridiron and Legacy Best Ball (10581)
are Locked Monday and Tuesday and Waivers every other day, as the owner said of Gridiron; Managers of the Round Table
(5460) is Free Agency Sunday only; REDRAFT (1360) Free Agency Saturday to Monday, Waivers Tuesday to Friday at 9 AM PT.
Claims also run at 12 AM PT when a Free Agency day starts (REDRAFT's Saturday and LP's Sunday 12 AM claims). Sleeper's
screens show times in the viewer's zone ("1 AM MST" = 12 AM PT). "After games waivers clear" is `waiver_day_of_week`:
None (0), 1 AM Tue (1), 1 AM Wed (2) ...; its time is 12 AM PT, then the day's schedule applies (LP's Wednesday is
Waivers at 9 AM, hence its 9:05 AM claims). `daily_waivers` 0 (weekly waivers): Free Agency every day, a run at the
after-games time.

`waiverStatus` now works from that:
- `dayCode`, `modeAt` ('fa' | 'waivers' | 'locked'), `afterGamesAt`, `nextRunAt` (pure, unit-tested).
- His own hold first (his game: until the after-games time; his drop: the clear days on his own clock; a cut: the
  after-games time), then the day's schedule: Free Agency → FA; Waivers or Locked → W until the next run, with the
  reason in the tap text ("A waiver day in this league: every pickup is a claim." / "Adds are locked today in this
  league."). A hold that ends on a Waivers day clears at that day's run.
- `nextWeeklyRun` (the Tuesday line) counts from the latest after-games time, so on Wednesday at 8 AM in a 9 AM
  league it's that morning's run.
- The Guide's W line and the Command Center's tooltip say "by the league's waiver schedule".
- The fixture league is now a weekly-waiver league (the specs' free-agent cases); a spec sets Friday Night Beverages'
  schedule.

Tests: unit (26): the day codes of the three screens and REDRAFT; LRD's Monday waiver day ("until Tue 12 AM") and
Sunday and Wednesday free agency, with a Friday drop still a claim; FL's Monday locked until Wednesday; LP's Friday →
Saturday 9 AM and Saturday → Sunday 12 AM runs, and a Sunday player until Wednesday 9 AM; FNB's Sunday-night player too
late; REDRAFT's Saturday free agency and Tuesday 9 AM. Spec: FNB's schedule on Saturday (three W chips, "waivers until

### Round 8: back-testing the rule against the owner's real adds and claims (a fifth review)

I replayed weeks 7–10 of 16 of the owner's leagues (Sleeper transactions, ESPN kickoffs) through `waiverStatus`:
for every free-agent add, would the app have said FA just before it; for every waiver claim, would it have said W
when the claim was placed, with "until" within an hour of when the claim actually ran. About 590 events. (Script kept
out of the repo: it reads the owner's league data.)
- **First run: 17 mismatches.** Fixed from what they showed:
  - **Weekly-waiver leagues lock everyone Monday and Tuesday.** No free-agent add on a Monday or Tuesday in League of
    Ordinary Gentlemen, How I Met Your Punter or Chop Shop (87 on other days); claims placed then, a bye week's defense
    too, ran Wednesday 12 AM. `dayCode` now gives a weekly league Waivers on Monday and Tuesday (claims at the after-games
    time) and Free Agency otherwise.
  - **A drop clears about an hour short of the full days:** 1-day drops were claimed or added 23 h 04 to 23 h 49 later,
    2-day ones 47 h 04 to 47 h 10. So a 12:11 AM drop makes the next day's 12 AM run (Kimani Vidal, Friday Night
    Beverages), which drop + 24 h missed by a day. Now drop + N days − 1 h ("about").
  - **A waiver day's run after his kickoff:** Tyson Bagent, claimed Monday morning in Managers of the Round Table
    (Monday a Waivers day) before a Monday-night game, ran Wednesday, not at Tuesday's 12 AM run: he locked at kickoff.
    Now a player on waivers by the day's schedule whose game starts before the next run clears after the after-games
    time, as a dropped one already did.
- **After: 8 mismatches of ~590 (98.6%).** Two claims placed in the first minutes of a run that still made it (12:01 AM
  into a 12:00 run); three players dropped in week 6, before the data starts (their drop clocks are invisible); one
  player who has since changed teams (the back-test uses today's team); and LRD Cucks' Seth McGowan, added as a free
  agent 6.5 h after a drop in a 1-day league, twice (unexplained; perhaps a player dropped the day he was added goes
  straight back to free agency, not checked).
- Tests: unit (29): a weekly league's Monday (a bye-week defense, claims Wednesday), Bagent's Monday-night lock, Vidal's
  12:11 AM drop; the drop-clock expectations an hour shorter. Spec: the "nobody dropped" case moves to Thursday (the
  tests' clock is a Tuesday, now a waiver day), and Tuesday's player with no game keeps a W. Ran `node --test` (669),
  check-precache and the Best available, waiver, Scout, past-trade, league-card and touch-target specs (77 passed). No
  screenshot changes.

## F16 — Closed tooltips stop taking up space

**Why.** Found in S28's checks (S28, "Left over"): the shared tooltip (`js/shared/ui/tooltips.js`, its rules in
`css/base.css`) was `visibility: hidden` while closed but still laid out, absolutely positioned under its trigger. A long
one (the Scoreboard header's (i)) hung below a short page and added empty scroll: before kickoff the Scoreboard on a
computer ended 116px below its last card. Needs F11 (merged, #232).

**What changed.**
- `css/base.css`, TOOLTIPS: a closed `.tooltip-text` is `display: none`; each open state (hover, keyboard focus, a
  self-trigger's focus, the tap class `.mobile-visible`) sets `display: block` and a nested `@starting-style` (opacity 0,
  translateY(-4px)) so the fade-in still runs. A second `transition` line adds `display 0.2s allow-discrete`, so the
  fade-out still runs too; browsers that don't know `allow-discrete` drop that line and keep the first (they show and
  hide without the fade-out; older ones without `@starting-style` also open without the fade). The dismissed-on-hover
  rule sets `display: none`. Of the two options on the card, `position: absolute` within its anchor was already the case
  and is exactly what spilled; `display: none` is the one that takes it out of the page's scroll size.
- `js/shared/ui/tooltips.js`: `toggle` sets `mobile-visible` first and then calls `place` (a display: none tooltip has no
  box to measure). Same task, so it never paints unplaced. Hover and keyboard focus already placed it from `mouseover` /
  `focusin`, by which time the hover/focus style applies, so they needed no change (the spec checks the long Scoreboard
  tip still opens wider on hover). Power Rankings' cell tooltips were already `display: none` while closed
  (`css/mls.css`), so `place` returned early for them on a tap; it now measures them too, and only moves one that would
  cross the window's edge (none does in `tooltips-onscreen.spec.mjs`).
- Screen readers: unchanged. `aria-describedby` reads a hidden element's text (the spec checks the Scoreboard (i)'s
  accessible description while closed); icon-only status markers take their name from `textContent` at load.
- T-Score's table-header tooltips have their own rule (`css/tscore.css`, `.table-header-tooltip`) and don't use the shared
  one; they sit at the top of their tables and add no room (the spec measures every T-Score tab), so left as they are.
- `sw.js`: `CACHE_NAME` v2.10.7 → v2.10.8, then v2.10.9 after merging main (S34, which took v2.10.8); no file added. CHANGELOG: a line under each app. docs/TESTING.md's Tooltips
  convention: closed is `display: none`; a new way of opening one needs `display: block` and the `@starting-style`.

**User-visible effect.** No blank space below short pages: the pre-kickoff Scoreboard on a computer ends at its last
card (116px shorter). Tooltips look, open, fade and sit exactly as before, on tap, hover and keyboard.

**Tests.** `tests/tooltip-closed-space.spec.mjs` (new, both widths, phones at 375px): every tab of Lineup Strategist,
Draft Strategist and T-Score measured with and without its tooltips in layout (scroll height and width must match), and
the pre-kickoff Scoreboard on its own. On main it fails on desktop: "scoreboard: 116px taller (943 → 1059)". It also
checks a tap opens the Scoreboard's (i) with its opacity transition running, placed (wider, inside the window), and a
second tap hides it; and on desktop that hover and keyboard focus open it and Escape closes it. At 375px main has no
extra room on these tabs (the pages are taller than the hanging tips), so the failure there is desktop only.

**Screenshots.** Main and this branch rendered in this container and compared (`npm run pxdiff`): one PNG changes,
- `desktop/mls-league-scoreboard.png`: 1280×1059 → 1280×943. The top 943 rows are identical; main's extra 116 rows
  were plain page background (the closed tip hanging below the page).
The other 43 are identical. Re-taken by CI (`[update screenshots: ...]` in the commit message; the workflow committed
exactly that PNG). With CI's fonts it goes 1280×1016 → 1280×929: the top 929 rows are identical to the old baseline and
the 87 rows dropped were plain page background. Opened: the page now ends at its footer credit line.

**Checked.** check-precache OK; `node --test` 640 pass; Playwright: `tooltip-closed-space`, `tooltips-onscreen`, `smoke`,
`touch-targets`, `focus-ring`, `badge-legend`, `mls-keyboard`, `mls-this-week`, `mls-final-window`, `mls-mvp-lvp`,
`mls-league-cards`, `mls-lineup-needs`, `mls-now-and-later`, `mls-past-trade`, `mls-root-for`, `mls-trade-fair`,
`mls-trade-impact`, `room-on-phones`, `support` at both widths: all pass. The full suite runs once, when the owner asks for
the pull request.

**Left over.** Nothing for this card. F19 (shorter tooltips) can start: it edits the same shared tooltip. A tooltip
opened some new way (a new CSS open state) needs `display: block` and the `@starting-style`, as docs/TESTING.md says.

### F16, round 2: tooltips checked against Material 3 Expressive (owner, 2026-11-14)

**Ask.** The owner asked whether the tooltips follow Material 3 Expressive. m3.material.io's tooltip Guidelines, Specs and
Accessibility pages were read in headless Chromium, and a throwaway spec (not committed) measured all 59 distinct
tooltips on every tab of both apps and T-Score at 1280px. Verdict: they are M3 *rich* tooltips (explanations, opened by
a click and kept open), and role, screen-reader text, keyboard, one-at-a-time and staying on screen already match.
Off: (1) length (median 24 words, 18 over 40, 6 over 80, up to 324; no bullets) and (4) few subheads: F19's job;
(2) no 1.5s linger after the mouse leaves; (3) shape and spacing (8px corners, 12px padding all round, a heavy shadow,
see-through blur); (5) the click-to-keep tooltips also open on hover, which M3 reserves for transient ones: kept on
purpose, it's useful on a computer; (6) motion: no motion tokens yet, the M3 card's job. Expressive itself changes
shape and motion, not tooltips. **The owner chose to do (2) and (3) now.**

**What changed.**
- (2) `js/shared/ui/tooltips.js`: a `pointerout` from a mouse (not touch) that leaves a shown tooltip's host adds
  `tt-linger` for 1.5s. Hovering another tooltip's host, keyboard focus on a trigger, any click or tap (`closeAll`) and
  Escape end every linger at once, so only one tooltip shows at a time; Escape that only ended a linger doesn't reach a
  dialog behind it. `css/base.css`: `.tt-linger` shows like the open states (also for T-Score's column headers,
  `.table-header-tooltip`); `css/mls.css`: Power Rankings' cell tooltips (their own display: none rule) take it too.
  Phones are unchanged: a tap opens, the next tap closes.
- (3) `css/base.css`: the shared tooltip has 12px corners (M3 shape "medium"), 12px top / 16px sides / 8px bottom
  padding, an opaque `rgb(15, 23, 42)` (was 95% with an 8px background blur) and elevation level 2's shadow
  (`0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,.3), 0 2px 6px 2px rgba(0,0,0,.15)`; was `0 10px 25px rgba(0,0,0,.5)`). Colour and text size
  unchanged (M14 brings the surface tokens).
- One exception: at 480px and under, a wide tooltip (`tt-wide`, the window's width) keeps 12px sides. With 16px the
  Scoreboard header's 197-word tip lost its last line at 375px and scrolled inside (16px over), which the owner reported
  in S27; once F19 shortens it, the exception can go.
- T-Score's column-header tooltips (`css/tscore.css`) are short labels, which M3 calls *plain* tooltips (4px corners,
  8px padding, 24px tall, an inverse light box). They got the linger only; their look is left for the owner (below).
- `CACHE_NAME` v2.10.9, above main's v2.10.8 (S34). CHANGELOG: a line under each app. docs/TESTING.md's Tooltips line.

**Tests.** `tooltip-closed-space.spec.mjs` adds: after the mouse leaves, the Scoreboard tip is still shown at 0.9s and
gone by 3s; Escape ends a linger; on the Lineup tab hovering a second (i) hides the first at once, and a click ends the
second's linger; and the computed corners, padding, background and no blur. **Screenshots:** all 44 identical to round
1's render here (tooltips are closed in every baseline), so nothing to re-take.

**Checked.** check-precache OK; `node --test` 640 pass; Playwright `tooltip-closed-space`, `tooltips-onscreen`, `smoke`,
`touch-targets`, `mls-keyboard`, `focus-ring`, `badge-legend`, `mls-this-week`, `mls-past-trade`, `mls-trade-impact`,
`mls-league-cards`, `mls-final-window`, `room-on-phones` at both widths: all pass.

**Left over.**
- F19: shorten the long tooltips with a subhead and bullets (M3's rich tooltip), then drop the 12px phone exception.
- For the owner: T-Score's column headers as M3 plain tooltips (a light box with dark text, 4px corners), or kept dark
  like the rest.
- The M3 card (motion tokens): the tooltips' 0.2s fade and 4px slide should use them.

### F16, round 3: T-Score's column headers as M3 plain tooltips, and notes for F19 and M3 (owner, 2026-11-14)

**The owner's choice.** T-Score's column-header tooltips become Material 3 *plain* tooltips (they name a column in a few
words), and the cards that follow (F19, M3) get notes.

**What changed.** `css/tscore.css`, `.table-header-tooltip .tooltip-text`: the inverse surface, a light `#e5e7eb` box
with `#111827` text (was a dark navy box), 4px corners (was 6px), 4px 8px padding with at least 24px height, no border or
shadow (M3's elevation 0; was a heavy shadow). Closed it's `display: none` like the shared tooltip, with the fade kept by
`@starting-style` and `display ... allow-discrete`. Text, size, placement (centered below, right-aligned on the last
columns) and the round-2 linger unchanged. CHANGELOG: a line under Draft Strategist, where T-Score's lines go.
`tooltip-closed-space.spec.mjs` adds a desktop test on the Top 50 tab: hidden until hovered, then the computed corners,
colours, no shadow and 24px minimum height, and the linger after the mouse leaves. Screenshots: all 44 identical to
round 2's render (the tooltips are closed in them). `CACHE_NAME` stays v2.10.9 (main is v2.10.8).

**Found, not changed** (in docs/TESTING.md's Open follow-ups): these header tooltips open on hover only. They aren't
`js/shared/ui/tooltips.js` triggers, so a tap or the keyboard can't open them and phones never show them.

#### For F19 (shorter tooltips, bullets)

- Material 3's rich tooltip is the target for the (i)s: an optional one-line subhead, short supporting text, and no
  critical information that exists only in the tooltip (it's easy to miss). Round 2's inventory, measured at 1280px with
  the test data: 59 distinct tooltips, 27 on an (i), 26 on a badge or cell, 6 T-Score headers; median 24 words, 18 over
  40, 6 over 80. Longest: Top Available (Scout, 324 words), the League Command Center (Dashboard, 267), the Scoreboard
  header (197), Power Rankings (97), Optimize Lineup (94), FLEX Kickoff Optimization (88). None has a list; 26 have a bold
  title (Power Rankings cells, the position-room chips).
- The shared tooltip is `display: none` while closed (F16), so a `<ul>` inside it needs no layout work, and
  `aria-describedby` still reads it. Its padding is now 12px top, 16px sides, 8px bottom: lists need their own spacing.
- **Delete the 12px phone exception** in `css/base.css` (`@media (max-width: 480px)` under the `tt-wide` rule) once the
  Scoreboard tip is short enough to fit a 375px screen with 16px sides; `tooltips-onscreen.spec.mjs` ("a long tooltip
  opens wider and fits the window") checks it.
- T-Score's column headers are M3 *plain* tooltips: keep them to a few words (M3: avoid wrapping to several lines). The
  In-Season tab's "Label" header (about 20 words) is the longest.
- Specs that match tooltip text: `tooltip-closed-space.spec.mjs` checks the Scoreboard (i)'s accessible description
  starts "Your matchup in every Sleeper league"; update it if that opening changes.

#### For M3 (toasts, Sync All's progress, Expressive's motion)

- The tooltips' motion is plain `0.2s ease` with a 4px slide: `css/base.css` (the shared tooltip's two `transition`
  lines and the `@starting-style` lines in its open states) and `css/tscore.css` (the header tooltip). Both carry an
  "Improvements M3" comment where the tokens go. M3's effects curve fits a fade (no overshoot); keep
  `display 0.2s allow-discrete` (or the token's duration) so the fade-out still runs, and keep the first plain
  `transition` line as the fallback for browsers that don't know `allow-discrete`.
- The 1.5s hover linger (`LINGER_MS` in `js/shared/ui/tooltips.js`) is M3's tooltip timing, not a motion token: leave it.
- Reduce Motion: the tooltips have no rule of their own yet; if M3 adds a site-wide reduced-motion rule, the 4px slide
  should go with it (the fade can stay).

### F16, round 4: T-Score's column headers on phones and the keyboard (owner, 2026-11-14)

**Why.** Found in round 3: `.table-header-tooltip` wasn't a trigger for `js/shared/ui/tooltips.js`, so the column-header
tooltips opened on a mouse's hover only. A phone never showed them, and the keyboard couldn't reach them.

**What changed.**
- `js/shared/ui/tooltips.js`: `.table-header-tooltip` joins `TRIGGER`, `enhanceAll` and `tipsOf`. Each header gets
  `tabindex="0"`, `role="button"` and `aria-describedby` pointing at its tooltip (its own text, "RZ TGTS", names it), so
  it behaves like the (i)s: a tap toggles it, a tap elsewhere closes it, Tab shows it, Enter or Space toggles it, Escape
  hides it. The headers don't sort or do anything else on a click.
- `place` now keeps a tooltip inside the nearest ancestor that scrolls or clips sideways, as well as the window (4px in
  from its edges). T-Score's tables scroll sideways in `.data-table-wrapper`, and on a phone 15 header tooltips near the
  wrapper's visible edge were cut off by it. It also applies to the shared tooltip, which only moves when it would
  otherwise be cut off.
- `css/tscore.css`: the header tooltip opens on `:focus-visible` and `.mobile-visible` (keeping its own centring, which
  the shared `.mobile-visible` rule's `transform` would undo), hides when dismissed while hovered, and takes `place`'s
  `translate: var(--tt-dx)`.
- docs/TESTING.md: the Open follow-up from round 3 is removed (done); the Tooltips convention mentions the headers and
  the scroll-box rule. CHANGELOG: a line under Draft Strategist (T-Score's lines go there).

**Tests.** `tooltip-closed-space.spec.mjs` adds: at 375px and 1280px, every visible column header on every T-Score tab
tapped in turn (more than 20): its tooltip shows inside the window and inside its table's scroll box, screen readers get
its text, and a second tap hides it. Before the `place` change it failed on phones with 15 "cut off by its table". On
desktop: Tab shows a header's tooltip (role button), Escape hides it, Enter shows it again. `touch-targets` and
`focus-ring` pass for T-Score with the headers now counted as buttons. Screenshots: all 44 identical to round 3's render.
A phone screenshot by hand: "RZ TGTS", the last column of Values, opens "Red Zone Targets" slid left inside the table.

**Checked.** check-precache OK; `node --test` passes; Playwright `tooltip-closed-space`, `tooltips-onscreen`,
`touch-targets`, `focus-ring`, `smoke`, `support`, `mls-keyboard`, `tscore-inseason`, `tscore-refresh` at both widths: all pass.
`CACHE_NAME` stays v2.10.9 (main is v2.10.8).

**Left over.** Nothing for this round.

## S32 — Phone rows on the Lineup and Roster tabs: less at once

**The owner's answers** (2026-11-14, asked before building, with real 375px renders on M17's 343px rows: the fixture's
Henry, Chase and Nacua Lineup rows and two Roster rows, today and in three stackings, demo badges added with the app's own
markup; a bottom sheet against the row expanding; the lock in the details against kept on the row; then two ways to put
the lock on the slot badge):
1. **Stacking: B, the slot (or position) badge on the photo's lower edge**, over A (badge above the photo) and C (badge
   beside a 20px photo).
2. **What a phone Lineup row always shows: as in the mock-ups**: slot, photo, name and bye week, position, team, rank
   badge, the kickoff *with its opponent* (one unit, so S31's opponent colour stays), the warnings that need you (injury,
   BYE, the IR slot, Bench / Starting in Sleeper), points and swap. Behind the tap: LOCKED / AUTO-LOCKED (the green edge
   still says it), EARLY, TAXI (the TX slot says it) and the rank-change chip; "+N" after the name counts them.
3. **The details: Material 3's modal bottom sheet** (M11's shared sheet), over the row expanding in place.
4. **The lock (owner's idea): on the slot badge.** The photo and slot badge are one button that locks him (or, when his
   game has started, runs today's override). Shown as **L2, a padlock dot on the photo's corner**: faint and open when
   unlocked, solid green when you locked him, solid blue when auto-locked (over L1, a padlock inside the slot pill).
   **The sheet also has a labelled lock button** (owner: yes, both).
5. **Tablets and computers unchanged** (proposed with the questions; not objected to).

**What changed and where.**
- `js/mls/render/lineup.js`: the slot badge and photo are wrapped in `.mls-row-lead` (with the lock button on starters),
  the name in `.mls-row-name-line` with the "+N" button after it; `phoneRowParts` counts what a phone hides and marks a
  badge line a phone would leave empty (`is-phone-empty`). The row's lock controls get `.mls-row-lock`. The photo's lock
  keeps the row lock's name ("Lock Josh Allen", `aria-pressed`). `js/mls/render/roster.js`: the position badge and
  photo in `.mls-row-lead`.
- `js/mls/badges.js`: `lockDotMarkup`, `leadLockMarkup`, `rowMoreMarkup` (one helper each, used by the rows and the
  legend); `sleeperLineupBadgeMarkup` gets a class, `mls-sleeper-lineup-badge`, so the details can name it (no visual change).
- `js/mls/render/lineupDetails.js` (new, precached): `openLineupDetails(opener)` opens the shared sheet titled with the
  player's name: his slot (when it isn't just his position), position, team and bye week, then every badge on his row as
  an inert copy (buttons become spans) with the legend's sentence, or the badge's own tap text where it has one (IR slot,
  rank change, opponent strength), then a lock button ("Lock in this spot" / "Unlock" / "Override the lock") with a line on
  what it does. The lock button closes the sheet, runs the same action and puts focus back on the row's "+N".
- `js/mls/legend.js`: `MEANING`, the sentences both the legend and the details show (rank badge, rank change, injury, bye,
  kickoff, LOCKED, EARLY, TAXI, IR slot, Sleeper lineup, opponent), so nothing is worded twice; two new legend lines, the
  padlock dot and "+N". `js/mls/main.js`: `openLineupDetails` action (imported right after render/lineup.js, so the load
  order doesn't move).
- `css/mls.css`, new last sections **PHONE ROWS** and the details' styles. At every width `.mls-row-lead` and
  `.mls-row-name-line` are `display: contents` and the phone controls are hidden, so tablets and computers lay out as
  before. Under 600px: a 42x44px lead with a 36px photo and the badge on its lower edge (ringed in the card colour; with
  headshots turned off the badge stands alone); the lock button covers the lead (48px with the hit area); the hidden
  badges; the name and badge column is the details button's target (its `::after` covers the column, 10px past it, above
  the rank badge's split parts, which pass taps through; the IR and opponent badges sit above it and still explain
  themselves); points over a 44x30px swap with its own 48px area; the row's info clips 6px outside itself (Lineup only)
  so the padlock dot isn't cut. `css/base.css`: the TOUCH TARGETS comment.
- `lineup/index.html`: the Guide's Lineup step says how to lock and open the details on a phone.
- `sw.js`: `lineupDetails.js` precached; `CACHE_NAME` v2.10.9 (main is v2.10.8, after S34 merged in; see below).
- Export Lineup is unchanged: it's drawn at a computer's width (M17), where the rows are as before
  (`export-same-everywhere.spec.mjs` passes).

**User-visible effect.** On a phone, a Lineup or Roster row's slot or position badge sits on the bottom of the player's
photo, so the name has about 40px more room. Lineup rows show only what you need at a glance; LOCKED, EARLY, TAXI and
the rank-change chip are a tap on the name away ("+2" says how many), in a bottom sheet that lists every badge on the row
with what it means and has a Lock button. Tapping the photo or slot badge locks or unlocks the player; a padlock dot on
the photo shows the state. Swap has a 48px target and the lock is no longer stacked under it. Starter rows are 78px tall
(95 to 125 before), so the fixture's phone Lineup tab is 86px shorter; Roster rows are 60px (56 before). Tablets,
computers and the exported image are unchanged. The Guide explains the padlock and the "+N".

**Specs.**
- New `tests/mls-phone-rows.spec.mjs`: on a phone the slot badge sits on the photo's lower edge, centred; the injury
  shows, EARLY doesn't and is counted ("+1"); the photo locks him (dot green, LOCKED hidden, "+2" with its accessible
  name); a tap on the name opens the sheet with LOCKED, EARLY and the rank badge and their legend sentences, as text;
  Unlock there closes it, unlocks him and focuses his "+N"; a row with nothing hidden still opens, with its injury and
  "Lock in this spot", and Escape returns focus. Keyboard: Enter opens it with focus on the title, Tab stays inside,
  Escape closes it back to the button; Enter on the photo's lock locks him. On desktop every badge and the row's lock
  show, the phone controls don't, and the badge sits beside the photo. Roster: badge on the photo on a phone, beside it on
  desktop.
- Changed: `mls-lineup-row-actions` (on a phone points over swap, no lock in that column); `touch-targets` (the swap /
  lock `ALLOW` entry is gone: every Lineup control has its 48px now); `mls-headshots` (36px on phones);
  `mls-display-ranks` (the split check at 270px: at 300px Henry's badge now fits on one line, as M17 found at 320px);
  `mls-rankings-change` (on a phone's Lineup tab the chip's explanation is in the row's details); `mls-keyboard`,
  `room-on-phones`, `mls-lineup-needs`, `mls-locked-kickoff`, `mls-weekly-locks` (click the visible lock: the row's on
  desktop, the photo's on a phone).
- Run here: check-precache OK; `node --test` 640 pass; Playwright: those specs plus badge-legend, export-same-everywhere,
  export-flex, export-numerals, sheet, mls-byes, mls-roster-counts, mls-opponent-strength, focus-ring, the kickoff and
  FLEX specs, smoke, svg-icons, tooltips-onscreen, mls-now-and-later, mls-sos, restricted-flex, mls-mvp-lvp, chevrons and
  scroll-shadows, both widths, all pass. The full suite hasn't run (rule 7: when the pull request is asked for).
- Two findings on the way, both fixed before the commit: the Roster's dual-rank tooltip (S24) was clipped by a first
  version of the dot's clip margin (now Lineup only), and the rank badge's split parts took taps meant for the details.

### S32, round 2: one lock on an auto-locked row, and no jump on lock or swap (owner, 2026-11-14)

The owner liked the result and raised two things.
1. **An auto-locked starter showed the blue lock twice on a phone**, on the photo and at the right. A bug in round 1: the
   right-hand override button kept its look in an inline style whose `display: inline-flex` beat PHONE ROWS' rule that
   hides it. Its look is now the class `.mls-autolock-btn` (css/mls.css, the same declarations), so on a phone the row
   has one lock, the blue padlock on the photo; wider screens keep it at the right as before.
2. **The screen jumped when you tapped a lock or swap.** Locking adds "Unlock All (1)" above the rows, swapping adds the
   swap hint, and the next tap takes them away again, so everything below moved by a line (35px at 375px) under your
   finger. `renderLineupUI` replaces the whole lineup, so the browser's scroll anchoring had nothing to hold on to, and
   Safari has none. Now `lineupAnchorTop` / `keepLineupAnchor` (js/mls/render/lineup.js) keep the first lineup row's
   distance from the top of the screen across the redraw, so the rows stay put and the added line goes in above them.
   Only when that row is above the bottom of the screen (a change below what you see moves nothing you see), and not
   while the tab is hidden. Every width.
- **Specs first** (`tests/mls-phone-rows.spec.mjs`, both failed before the fixes): an auto-locked starter on a phone has
  one override control, the blue padlock (there were 2); the row you tap keeps its place on the screen through lock,
  unlock, swap and cancel, from a starter row and a bench row (Jefferson moved 35px).
- **Screenshots:** none change (rendered before and after in this container: all 44 pixel-identical). Specs run:
  mls-phone-rows, mls-lineup-row-actions, touch-targets, mls-keyboard, mls-lineup-needs, mls-locked-kickoff,
  mls-weekly-locks, room-on-phones, mls-display-ranks, export-same-everywhere, export-flex, the kickoff and FLEX specs,
  mls-tab-redraws, mls-this-week, mls-sim, smoke (180 pass); `node --test` 669 pass. `CACHE_NAME` stays v2.10.9 (main is
  v2.10.8).

### S32, round 8: nothing that needs Safari's missing features (owner, 2026-11-15)

The owner has no iPhone, and this container has only Chromium, so instead of a Safari check on a device: S32's CSS and JS
searched for features Safari lacks. One mattered: `overflow-clip-margin` (round 1), which let a Lineup row's info box clip
6px outside itself. Measured now, the padlock dot sits at least 6px inside that box at 375 and 320px, headshots on or off
(the box stretches to the row's height since round 2), but the photo's lock's 48px tap area reached 3px left of the box,
and the clip margin was what kept that part: without it `touch-targets.spec.mjs` failed (the lock's left edge). On
Safari the lock would have had about a 45px area. Now the box is 3px wider on the left with its content where it was
(`margin-left: -3px; padding-left: 3px`) and the clip margin is gone, so the lock's whole area is inside the box in every
browser. Otherwise: `:has()` (Safari 15.4+) is fine; `@starting-style` (Safari 17.5+) only drops the snackbar's slide-in
on older Safari. Android's Chrome and Samsung Internet are Chromium, which the specs run.
- **Spec:** `mls-phone-rows.spec.mjs`, every padlock dot inside its row's info box at 375 and 320px, headshots on and off.
  With the clip margin removed and nothing else, `touch-targets` failed as above; with the wider box it passes.
- **Screenshots:** all 44 pixel-identical with round 7 (rendered here). Specs run: mls-phone-rows, touch-targets,
  room-on-phones, mls-display-ranks (all pass).

### Proposed card W2 — CI: a WebKit (Safari) run of the phone specs (owner asked for it, 2026-11-15; for the runbook)

**Why.** Every spec and screenshot runs in Chromium only (CI and cloud sessions). That covers Chrome, Edge and Android
(Chrome, Samsung Internet), but not Safari: every browser on an iPhone or iPad is WebKit underneath. The owner has no
iPhone, so nothing checks the site there. S32 round 8 found a CSS property Safari lacks (`overflow-clip-margin`) doing a
quiet job (a lock's 48px tap area) by reading the code; a WebKit run would catch that kind of thing by itself.
Playwright ships a WebKit build that runs on Linux, close to Safari's engine (not Safari itself: no iOS chrome, no real
touch).

**Goal.** A second CI job runs a chosen set of specs in Playwright's WebKit at phone size, so a pull request that breaks
something only Safari users would see fails before it merges.

**Asks you (before building).** (1) Which specs: my suggestion is smoke, the phone-row and sheet specs
(`mls-phone-rows`, `sheet`, `badge-legend`, `room-on-phones`), touch-targets, mls-keyboard and the export spec, about 3 to 5
minutes, rather than all ~500 tests; (2) required (blocks merging) or advisory at first (my suggestion: advisory for a
week or two, then required once it has been green); (3) screenshots: none in WebKit (my suggestion; its fonts and
anti-aliasing differ, so they'd need their own baselines and double the re-take work).

**In scope.**
- `tests/playwright.config.mjs`: a `webkit-phone` project (Playwright's iPhone device: 390px, touch, WebKit), matched
  only to the chosen specs (`testMatch`), skipped unless `WEBKIT=1` so cloud sessions and the normal `npm run check`
  don't need the browser (cloud sessions may not install browsers: CLAUDE.md).
- `.github/workflows/check.yml`: a separate job (or step) that installs WebKit (`npx playwright install --with-deps
  webkit`, pinned to the repo's Playwright 1.63.0, cached) and runs `WEBKIT=1 npx playwright test --project=webkit-phone`;
  the docs-only shortcut (W1) skips it too.
- Run it once and fix or list what fails: real Safari gaps get fixed (or a card); test-only differences (a spec that
  assumes Chromium behaviour) get a `test.skip` with the reason, per spec, never a blanket skip.
- `docs/TESTING.md`: the WebKit run in "The checks" and how to run it locally on a machine that can install WebKit.

**Out of scope.** Screenshots in WebKit (unless you choose them), Firefox, real-device testing, changing what the site
supports.

**Done when.** The WebKit job runs on every pull request with the chosen specs and passes; anything skipped in WebKit is
listed with its reason; TESTING.md says how it works; LOG lists what it found.

**Size and placement.** ~15k tokens; touches only `tests/` and `.github/` (no served file, no `CACHE_NAME`). Runs beside
any card except one editing `check.yml` or `playwright.config.mjs` (e.g. a Playwright upgrade).
